  - **Legacy fields are read, never written.** `sleepQuality` / `stressLevel` / `energyLevel` / `mood` / `clientFeel` / two-state `bodyStates[].state` still exist on old sessions; read them through `features/rating/session-reads.ts` (`doseOf`, `readinessDial`, `regionDial`) or the clinical review's `facts.ts`, never directly. `state` is still WRITTEN, derived from `dial`, for those readers.
  - **Loudness stores the journal's `standard / elevated / critical`**; only the words changed (Note · Heads up · Critical). `elevated` now shows on the briefing while it still matters (`headsUpEntries`, `HEADS_UP_WINDOW_DAYS = 21`, or its `effectiveUntil`). The Wrap-up's Profile note defaults to `standard` (Note), which keeps it on the profile and off the briefing.
  - **An unfiled note is `kind: "general"` with `!isLegacy`** (`isUnfiled`). The composer writes it when no category is picked; the To-file tray (`NoteSweep`) files it by writing only `kind` and `category`. Unfiled notes are kept off the catalog's shelves and search until filed. **The Note for the next trainer is one of them, and must never be discarded from a tray** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026; AJ: "Ideally the end session note is made for the next sessions pre session briefing but also can be filed to the profile"): Discard archives, and the journal drops archived notes, so a discard would take it off the next briefing. `NoteSweep` takes `isNextTrainerNote`, labels the card "Note for the next trainer · on the next briefing" and offers no Discard; filing it is safe (kind and category only). The Wrap-up recognises it by its journal id once the save answers, before that by this session, `origin: "post_session"`, a Heads up and the same words (`isNextTrainerNote`, `note-catalog.ts`). The Notes page asks `isNextTrainerNoteOfSessions`, which matches the session's own copy in `recentSessions`, and so keeps Discard while the sessions load, for a session older than that window, or when the session's words were edited later.
  - **The Note for the next trainer is on the client's Notes ONCE** (Sep 27 2026; a bug since Sep 18). `useClientJournal`'s read-only "Session summary" of `sessions.notes` is dropped by `sessionsWithoutJournalCopy` when a native entry has the same `sessionId`, origin `post_session` and the same `journalBodyOf` words (trimmed, first 5,000 characters). It checks the UNFILTERED native list, so archiving the journal copy does not bring the read-only card back; a note edited later in History, and sessions from before Sep 18, keep their card.
  - **A Pulse area has a colour only when all three statements are answered.** `scoreCategory` gives a part-answered area `status: null` (the Pulse screen shows "1/3", never Red); any other reader of saved Pulses takes that `status`, never `ragForFraction(percent)` - one statement answered low is not a red area. The Deep Dive's Pulse trend (`clinical-review/pulse-trend.ts`) coloured from `percent` until Sep 23 2026; it now skips a part answer as a reading and says so ("not enough answered in Sep", "one full reading so far").
  - **Update Pulse writes through `useCheckInDraft`** (the same debounced autosave and change log as the full panel). Don't add a second write path to `progressReports`. The Client Progress Report no longer writes `subjective` at all; it shows a read-only snapshot.
  - **One `useCheckInDraft` per client per screen** (client codex, phase 6, Sep 24 2026). A screen that shows the Pulse AND mounts `ClientCheckInPanel` owns the one draft and passes it as the panel's `draft` prop; the panel's own hook is then disabled. On Notes & Profile that screen is Body & Pulse (phase 12). Two hooks on one client each load the history and each autosave — two drafts side by side. The draft handed in must be for the panel's `client`.
  - **A surface that is dark whatever the theme** must wrap a Dial in `.dark` + `data-theme="dark"` so `--eq-*` resolve — the same rule the FORD sweep already followed. The Wrap-up is no longer the example: since Sep 27 2026 it follows the app's theme entirely, its Dial, Loudness and trays included (they were pinned dark and drew white-on-white words on the light theme), every colour on it is a token, and `NoteSweep`'s `dark` prop is gone.
  - **Two files whose names differ only by case are ONE file on Windows.** `dial.ts` beside `Dial.tsx` typechecked and tested green in the Linux container and broke on AJ's PC (`./dial` resolved to `Dial.tsx`), which is why the pure module is `scales.ts`. Before shipping, check `git ls-files | tr A-Z a-z | sort | uniq -d` prints nothing.
  - **The 4 P's store `score` (rank × 20)**, with `status` derived on the talking points for older readers (`four-ps.ts`). `UNRATED_SCORE = 0` is "not rated", never Needs work.

- **The check-in is Pulse on screen** (reporting round, Sep 16; it was "Assessment" from the fix round): the code, the Firestore fields (`progressReports`, `isCheckInOnly`, `subjectiveSnapshot`) and file names still say check-in / subjective. The journal's focus "check-ins" are a different thing and keep their name.

- **A settings copy and a Set-up note have the same kind and category** (machine menu, Oct 4 2026). Every settings save files a copy into the client's journal ("Leg Press — Seat 4 → 5. Range of motion", `kind: "equipment"`, `category: null`, the machine's id), and a note a trainer types under Coaching & equipment · Set-up stores exactly that kind and category (`storedKindOf("coaching", "Setup")`). So neither field, and no test on the words (an arrow in the body), can tell them apart: filter on them and real notes vanish ("Seat 4 -> 5 helps the knee"). A copy is recognised only by an EXACT rebuild of its body from a `settingHistory` row plus `occurredAt` within two minutes of the row's `timestamp` (`machine-menu/settings-copy.ts` `isSettingsCopy`; `saveSettings` now writes the two moments equal). When the history wasn't read, nothing is hidden: a copy shown as a note is untidy, a hidden note is lost. **If `saveSettings`' journal body ever changes, change `rebuildCopyBody` with it** (`mutations.test.ts` asserts the copy is recognised). Lists that can't read every machine's history (the grid's and the rail's note counts) still count copies, at plain loudness, so a mark's loudness is never moved by one.

- **"Has this note's window ended?" is `windowEnded`, never `!mattersOn`** (client codex, phase 7, Sep 24 2026). A note whose start is pushed ahead does not matter YET — it has not ended. `JournalEntryCard` asked `!mattersOn` and printed "Ended — Matters from Sep 20" on a note written on the 10th for the 20th. `windowEnded` (`client-notes/mattering.ts`) is true only for a range whose last day has gone by or a one-off day that has passed; never for an "always" note, a yearly day, or a resolved or archived note (closed is not ended).

- **The Hub card's red triangle is the day's Critical notes, read once for the day** (Sep 24 2026, question 12 of the Sep 20 audit — `docs/rounds/2026-09-24-hub-critical-flag.md`). Until then it read `client.priorityNote` / `hasPriorityNote` / a High `client.events[]` entry, which nothing writes, while the Loudness control said Critical "marks the Hub card". Now `useHubCriticalNotes` (one live `journalEntries` listener per thirty booked clients: `clientId in [...]` and `importance == "critical"`) feeds `criticalNotesOn` (`lib/hub-critical-notes.ts`: a Critical ROOT that `mattersOn` the BOOKING's studio day), which feeds `getClientAlertState(client, liveCritical)`. **Read by client, never by studio** — a note written in a session carries the studio the trainer stood in, one written on the profile the home studio, so a studio query misses a visiting client's note. **Never cache a count on the client** (AJ): a window that opens or runs out changes the answer with no write. **The triangle only**: no note turns the left edge red, a legacy priority note included. **Red for everyone**: the Hub ignores dismissals, like `CriticalLine`. **Unknown is `null`, never `[]`**: a group not answered, failed or at `HUB_CRITICAL_GUARD` leaves its silent clients unknown and the Hub prints one line under the day strip — never hand a card `[]` for a client nobody read. Hand the hook the DAY's clients: the week's would multiply the listeners.

- **The record's note selections live once, in `client-notes/record-selectors.ts`** (client codex, phase 7). How to coach her, injury threads, notes by machine, older life notes by pillar, the FORD door's count, the sub-toggle line, the critical line's words — every Notes & Profile page reads `journal.threads` through these; don't write a second one in a page. Three traps they already hold: **"how to coach her" goes by `root.kind`** (`preference` / `coaching`), never by `noteCategoryOf` — the catalog files every `general` note (legacy session summaries, `sessions.notes`, included) under Preference; **a count that cannot be known is `null`**, never 0 (`fordDoorCount` for a reader FORD refuses, `notesTabMeta` while loading); and **a note that leaves Notes must land somewhere** — `notesOnRecord` settles a life note only when `olderLifeNotesByPillar` will place it (both ask `isOlderLifeNote`), because an orphaned update (its root archived or past the read limit) inherits the root's `life` kind and would otherwise be on neither page. The critical line (`CriticalLine`) uses the briefing's `criticalEntries` and ignores dismissals, and never clips: whole sentences, no "…".

- **The Notes page (client codex, phase 9, Sep 24 2026) — read `src/features/client-notes/README.md` first.** Four traps it holds:
  - **Archive a thread WHOLE** (`archiveThread` → `archiveJournalEntries`, one `writeBatch`). Archiving only the root turns each update into a note of its own, because `assembleThreads` promotes an update whose root is not in the load. Imported rows (`isLegacy`) have no `journalEntries` document; one missing document refuses the whole batch, so they are left out.
  - **A composer clears only once the save landed.** `JournalComposer.submit` catches a rejected `onSubmit` and keeps the words ("Not saved — still here, try again"). A host that catches its own error must RETHROW (`NotesPage.handleCreate`, `QuickNoteDialog.submit`), or the composer believes it saved and wipes the only copy.
  - **FORD / Life saves in place, and a FORD detail holds 2,000 characters** (`FORD_BODY_MAX`, the rule's cap) where a note holds 5,000 — the composer checks the length before it sends. It is offered only to a reader the FORD create rule accepts (`access.fordWritable` since phase 19 — it was `access.canEdit`, which an administrator who works elsewhere passes and the create rule does not); anyone else gets the hand-off, whose sentence depends on whether they may READ FORD (`fordReadOnly`, below).
  - **Notes' own anchors are requests, not scrolls.** `note-{id}`, `notes-compose` and `notes-resolved` reach the page as a one-shot request keyed by the navigation move (`notes-intent.ts`); the page clears a hiding filter, opens a row or unfolds Resolved first and THEN scrolls, in an effect, never a layout effect. A thread still in the To-file tray (unfiled) lands on its tray card — the critical line can point at one. A request made while the notes load waits for them rather than finding nothing.

<a id="profile"></a>

## The client profile - four tabs, the record, FORD

- **The client profile is FOUR tabs, run by depth, and each one is a question, not a screen name** (AJ, Sep 27 2026: "The four tabs run by depth: Journey (what she has done, the glance on the floor), Programming (what she's meant to do), Notes & Profile (who she is), Activity Archive (the whole record). Don't reorder, merge or add a tab without asking."). `profile-nav.test.ts` holds `PROFILE_TABS`, and `ClientProfileView.tabs.test.ts` holds the row drawn on screen to it. Journey (what has she done) · Programming (what is she supposed to do — Routines + Equipment) · Notes & Profile (what do we know — seven pages since the client codex: Overview · Notes · FORD · Body & Pulse · Goals & Focus · Story · Account) · **Activity Archive** (what has already happened — Clinical + History; renamed from "Clinical History" in the audit round, tab id still `clinical`). **Where the trainer is is NOT a string**: it is a `ProfileLocation` — a tab AND a segment inside it — owned by one reducer in `src/features/client-profile/profile-nav.ts` and reached through `useProfileNav`. Never add a second copy of it. Every tab id the profile has ever used still resolves, through `legacyLocation()`; add to that map rather than chasing call sites. Read `docs/rounds/2026-09-15-four-tab-profile.md` before touching the profile's navigation.

- **The sub-toggle is one component and its rules are load-bearing.** `ProfileSubnav` — sticky, 48px, segments as EQUAL fractions of the full width, brand blue (never hero orange, which is Start Session). The iPad is held and often not looked at: a segment is found by POSITION, so never size a segment to its text and never move the bar. **Never hide a segment** — Routine B with no B reads "OFF" and the switch to turn it on lives behind it. **Seven segments wrap, they don't clip** (client codex, Sep 24 2026): Notes & Profile passes `wrap`, which lets a label and its meta line take a second line and grows the row — every segment together, so they stay equal — and is still 48px wherever nothing wraps (on every portrait iPad below the 13-inch — 744, 820, 834pt — "Body & Pulse" and "Goals & Focus" take two lines and the row is ~75px; a meta line that wraps grows it too, and metas land after the bar draws, so keep them short). Every wrap rule is scoped to `.psub-shell[data-wrap]` and `profile-nav-css.test.ts` holds it there: Programming and the Activity Archive don't pass `wrap` and render exactly as before, so never move a wrap rule into the base styles. `flagTone: "warn"` draws the dot plum (`--eq-warn`); `idPrefix` gives the segments ids and `aria-controls`, so a host that passes it keeps an element with every panel id in the page from the start — an empty, hidden `role="tabpanel"` placeholder for a page not yet visited, or `aria-controls` points at nothing.

- **The codex's look is a scanned contract, not a style guide** (client codex, phase 5, Sep 24 2026). Every Notes & Profile page is built from `src/features/client-codex/kit/` and its tokens, and `kit/scale.test.ts` reads the files: text only at 11 / 12 / 14 / 17 / 22 / 30px (22 since Oct 4 2026), no raw hex (the tokens file included — every `--cx-*` colour is an alias of an `--eq-*` or `--ford-*` token), no colour a component sets itself, no ellipsis or one-line clip (a list row's note body may clamp to two lines, only in the exactly named body selectors — `LINE_CLAMP_BODIES` — so a name or meta never can), no regex lookbehind, no raw invisible character. **A new codex file must be added to `CODEX_FILES`** — a file in the folder that is not on the list fails the suite. The `--cx-*` tokens are declared on `.cx`, not `:root`, so they follow any theme wrapper around the codex; **a kit piece rendered in a portal (a dialog) finds no tokens unless its content carries `.cx-kit`**. Heads up is plum and Critical crimson in the codex too: `LoudChip` reads the one Loudness mapping (`LOUDNESS_TONE`), never its own.

- **Switching a sub-view must cost no fetch.** Every pane reads what the profile already loaded, or keeps its own gate (the Deep Dive, view id `trends`, still generates on request). Mount rules: Routine A/B and Reports unmount when hidden; **All Machines, Setup and the Deep Dive are mounted on first use and hidden thereafter** (they hold a selection, drafts, a generated report); Calendar and Sessions are ONE mount of `ClientHistoryTab`. Don't "tidy" any of those into plain conditional renders.

- **The client profile's record tab is the whole non-training record — as seven pages since the client codex** (phase 8, Sep 24 2026). The long scroll (`ClientInfoSheet` → `ClientDossier`, eight sections, a jump rail) is gone: Notes & Profile is `features/client-codex/` — `ClientCodex` and an Overview plus Notes · FORD · Body & Pulse · Goals & Focus · Story · Account, the page and card coming from the nav (`recordPage`, `recordAnchor`). `DossierSection` and `sectionForEntry` stay (journal entries may carry `profileSection`), and every old section id still lands through `SECTION_TO_PAGE`. The Pulse composes on Body & Pulse; the filed shelf is still Activity Archive → Reports. The old journal area, `ClientJournalTab`, is deleted (phase 19; its Notes area went in phase 9 — Notes is `client-notes/NotesPage`, which takes the journal as a REQUIRED prop and has no fallback hook; its Pulse in phase 12; its focus board in phase 14 — Goals & Focus mounts `FocusBoard` itself, on the tab's journal, with the writes in `goals/useFocusActions`), and so are the dossier's `DossierPrimitives`, `JournalRail`, `entriesForSection` and `DOSSIER_SECTIONS` — the codex loads `useClientJournal` ONCE (`useCodexData`) and hands it to every page, so don't add a second hook on a page.

- **The codex loads once, mounts on first visit, and saves through one form** (client codex, phase 8, Sep 24 2026). `useCodexData` opens the journal, FORD, the InBody scans and the trainer's dismissals ONCE for the tab, and derives the Pulse history from the profile's progress-reports listener (`useProgressReports`) — never a page's own listener for any of them; FORD is never opened for a reader its rule refuses (`codexAccess().fordReadable`). The Overview mounts with the tab and every other page on its first visit, then stays mounted and hidden — so **no `autoFocus` inside a page**, and anything that measures its width uses a feature-detected ResizeObserver (a hidden page measures 0). The record panel is `keepMounted` after the tab's first visit on a client, and `ClientCodex` is keyed on the client. Every client field a page edits goes through `useRecordForm` (`record-form.ts`): **`FIELD_HOME` must stay total over `RECORD_FORM_KEYS`, and every home's anchor must be in `RECORD_ANCHORS`** (the test fails otherwise), or the Save bar's Show lands on a page top. A key not on the list is refused, so `recoveryMetric` (retired, decision 7) and the Mindbody fields can't be written from the tab. Who may edit is `codexAccess` (the clients update rule, via `canRecordInBody`) — pages take `canEdit` / `fordReadable` as props and never work it out again, and `useRecordForm` takes `canEdit` too, so a reader who may not edit can stage nothing even through a control that escapes the page's lock.

- **Personal detail goes in FORD, not the journal.** `clients/{id}/ford/{id}`, studio-scoped by the client it hangs off; `journalEntries` is readable by any signed-in user, which is why it is not there. `pillar` is NULLABLE by design and the rules do not validate it — null means "caught mid-set, filed at teardown", and making it required puts a decision between a trainer hearing something and recording it. "Personal" was removed from the journal composer; old `life` entries still render. `client.events` is READ as FORD through an adapter and never written. Read `src/features/ford/README.md` before touching any of it.

- **Colour by urgency, never by pillar** on FORD screens, and `clients/{id}.fordSummary` is a cache — the subcollection is the truth.

- **The client-profile audit round (Sep 16) — read `docs/rounds/2026-09-16-client-profile-audit.md` before touching the profile.** The load-bearing parts:
  - **One machine window.** Every machine tap on the profile (Journey grid, Routine A/B rows) opens `features/equipment/ClientMachineWindow.tsx` — the All Machines detail. Settings save through `equipment/mutations.ts` (reason, history, journal). `MachineSettingsDashboardModal` survives only for `WorkoutChartGrid`; don't bring it back to the profile. *(Out of date since the machine menu, Oct 4 2026: every machine tap on the profile and in a session opens the machine menu, `features/machine-menu`, one card in both doors; `ClientMachineWindow` and the session's `MachineSheet` are deleted. Settings still save through `equipment/mutations.ts`, the reason asked and never required. `MachineSettingsDashboardModal` went on Oct 2 2026. Read the Active Session section's machine-menu traps.)*
  - **Names go through `src/lib/client-name.ts`.** `client.nickname` (coach-owned, never synced) replaces the legal first name in headers; stored session names stay legal.
  - **Master Sync is the only client sync** (`src/lib/mindbody-master-sync.ts`, the header's Sync button, `POST /api/mindbody/client-master-sync`). By Mindbody id only (`mindbodyIdOf`), and it REFUSES a record whose ids disagree (`mindbodyIdConflict`) — the old profile sync used a name search and may have staged a namesake's id. Mindbody-owned identity is read-only on a linked client. `syncClientCommercialData` and `/api/mindbody/client-commercial` have no callers left.
  - **The waiver is three states** (`src/lib/client-waiver.ts`): never synced is "Not synced yet", not "Not on file".
  - **`client.machineStats` is trusted only once `machineStatsBackfilledAt` is set** — Programming's "performed / never tried" and the routine rows' % and × say nothing before it.
  - **Clinical watch-outs quote the matrix** (`src/lib/clinical-watchouts.ts`); the "Common constraints" category in `data/clinical-matrix.ts` holds the quick toggles. Display names drop anything in brackets (`shortCondition`) — don't put meaning in brackets.
  - **Setting suggestions are never values** (`features/equipment/setting-suggestions.ts`): an empty field, a band of `MIN_CLIENTS`, more than one client on the value, and a tap on Use.
  - **The contract tier lock** (`client.contractTierOverride`, `features/client-admin/`) wins on the record only; the renewal engine does not read it yet.
  - **Sticky elements inside the record** measure the scroller's padding with `features/client-profile/use-scroller-pad.ts`.
  - **The notes catalog leaves out profile fields the record edits in its own sections** (`SHOWN_ELSEWHERE_ON_RECORD`).
  - **The progress-report banner above the header reads one probe per client**, not the shelf (which loads on two tabs only).

- **Every field a Notes & Profile editor writes must be a `RECORD_FORM_KEYS` entry** (`src/features/client-codex/record-form.ts`). `seedForm()` seeds exactly those keys, and `useRecordForm`'s `updateField` refuses any other, so a field outside the list opens as an empty box however much is stored and cannot be saved. Wingspan shipped that way in the old `ClientInfoSheet` (its form never seeded it, while machine fit's Setup screen was sending trainers to that box); the codex seeds it, and `record-form.test.ts` reads the codex, account, life, FORD-page and goals editors and fails on any `updateField("...")` key the seed does not carry (landing, Sep 24 2026, from `claude/elastic-margulis-5fc2d8`). Seeding writes nothing: a save writes only the dirty fields.

- **`useClientJournal`'s four unbounded collections carry a 200-item guard rail, unordered on purpose** (`JOURNAL_GUARD_LIMIT`). Don't turn it into a "newest N": the focus board counts every past focus and its history, so an ordered cut would miscount (several focuses may be active at once since the audit round). When the rail is hit the hook says `capped` and the journal tab says so.

- **Every per-client FORD query filters on the client's studio, and every FORD writer stamps that same studio** (client codex, phase 1, Sep 24 2026). Rules are not filters: the FORD read rule tests `resource.data.studioId`, so Firestore refuses any list that does not pin the studio in its own `where` — even when every document in it would pass one by one. `useClientFord` listed `clients/{id}/ford` unfiltered from Sep 15 to Sep 24, so **every trainer below franchise owner saw an empty FORD** and nobody noticed, because AJ is an administrator; `refreshFordSummary` had the same fault. Both now use `where("studioId", "==", fordStudioIdOf(client))` (home studio, then the older `studioId`) with a limit and **no `orderBy`** — an orderBy beside the equality needs the composite index `ford(studioId asc, occurredAt desc)`, which does not exist. The hook reports `status` (`loading` · `ready` · `failed` · `denied`) and only `ready` may be drawn as "nothing on file"; a cross-train visitor is `denied` (they read the client document, never her FORD). `failed` is a listener error other than the rules, or a client with no studio — **not offline**: the persistent cache answers an offline read, so an iPad that never opened this client's FORD shows it empty while offline. **Test anything FORD with a Life Transformer's sign-in, not an administrator's.** `tests/firestore.rules.test.ts` → "FORD — who can read her life".

- **The FORD page (client codex, phase 10, Sep 24 2026) — read `src/features/ford/README.md` → The FORD page first.** Five traps it holds:
  - **A settled life note leaves Notes only because the FORD page shows it.** `notesOfJournal` passes `lifeOnFord: true`, so the journal's standing or resolved `life` notes are FORD's (`notes.record.lifeSettled`, placed by `olderLifeNotesByPillar`) and no longer in Notes' zones or counts. They are journal notes, so the FORD page draws them for EVERY reader — a cross-train visitor who is refused FORD included. Never gate them on `fordReadable`, or they vanish from the tab for that reader; and never settle a note `olderLifeNotesByPillar` would not place (both ask `isOlderLifeNote`).
  - **The FORD count means FORD's details plus those older notes** (`fordCountOf` in `client-codex/codex-data.ts`, over `fordDoorCount({ olderLifeCount })` and `olderLifeCountOf`; the Notes door and the sub-toggle both read it — don't call `fordDoorCount` from a page). While the journal is still loading the count is `null` — the Notes door shows no number and the sub-toggle says "loading" — never an undercount that jumps. When the journal FAILED, the count is FORD's own details (what the page draws), but a 0 is `null` and the sub-toggle says "couldn't load": the older notes are unknown, so "nothing on file yet" would be a failed read passed off as empty.
  - **Two save models on one card.** The Occupation and Recreation bands are record fields: Edit / Done only opens and closes the editor, the ONE Save bar saves, and the band says "Not saved yet" until then. A FORD detail saves the moment it is added. Don't make Done save, and don't route a band field through a FORD writer (or a FORD detail through the form).
  - **Ask next is retirement-aware by `FORD_PROMPT_WHEN`, keyed by the prompt's exact text.** Rewording a prompt in `FORD_META` without its key silently asks a retired client about work again; `ask-next.test.ts`'s drift guard fails first. The briefing cue passes the same `{ retired }`.
  - **`FordDetailDialog.onSave` answers `false` when nothing saved**, and the dialog stays open with the words. A host that ignores the writer's `null` / `false` (the old hub did) closes the dialog on a refused save and loses the sentence. A gesture new in the dialog at Idea is unowned (`ownerTrainerId: null`) — the Delight queue's "Needs an owner" depends on it.

- **`clients/{id}/ford/one-line` is In one line, not a detail** (client codex, phase 11, Sep 24 2026). It is a FORD document with a FIXED id, stamped with `fordStudioIdOf(client)` like every detail so the ONE filtered listener delivers it, and written **`isArchived: true`, `pillar: null`, `kind: "one-line"`** on purpose: every existing reader of details (the tray, the rollup, Coming up, the Delight queue, the post-session sweep, and an old iPad bundle mid-deploy) drops archived documents, so none of them can show it as an unfiled capture. `useClientFord` takes it out FIRST (`splitOneLine`, before the legacy merge) and hands it out as `oneLine`. **Anything new that lists `ford` documents must drop archived ones or split it out** — and never offer "discard" on it: the delete rule lets the last author delete a pillar-null document of their own. It is FORD text, so it is never on the client document (a cross-train studio reads that) and never shown to a reader FORD refuses. It is REPLACED, not added to, so it is rewritten only over a line this iPad has read — a failed FORD read offers no editor (a detail is still offered; a line is not). Its first save is the whole document (`setDoc`), every rewrite an update of the words and author only — the rule holds `studioId` and `clientId` immutable. **So a client who moved home studio cannot get a line at the new one**: the old line (stamped with the old studio) is filtered out, the page says "No line yet", and every save is a `setDoc` over it that the rules refuse as a `studioId` change — worse than an older detail, which is only hidden while new ones still save. `saveFordOneLine` answers `blocked` for a refused FIRST line and the panel says a line from an earlier home studio may be in the way and an administrator can clear it (the delete rule allows administrators and franchise owners), never "try again". `tests/firestore.rules.test.ts` → "FORD — In one line and Follow up next time".

- **Adding to FORD follows the FORD CREATE rule, not "may edit the record"** (client codex, phase 19). The create rule is `isTrainerOfStudio(studioId)` with no administrator or franchise clause, so an administrator who works elsewhere may change the record — and, by the update rule, what FORD holds — but every new FORD document they try is refused. `codexAccess().fordWritable` mirrors the create rule; the FORD page's adds (Remember something, a pillar's Add, the birthday plan, an idea, "Asked it" with an answer, a FIRST In one line), Notes' FORD / Life, the header's Note dialog and Account's intake card all offer FORD only on it, and the FORD page says why to a reader who may edit but not add. Don't gate a FORD add on `canEdit`. **And say the right thing to whoever is turned away:** the composer's hand-off tells a reader who may read FORD but not add to it (`fordReadOnly`, from `fordReadable && !fordWritable`) that adding isn't offered; only a reader FORD refuses altogether (a cross-train trainer) is told the home studio alone can read it — that sentence beside a working Open FORD is false for an administrator.

- **Follow up next time is stamped only when the question changes** (client codex, phase 11). `followUp` / `followUpAt` / `followUpBy` on a FORD detail; the detail dialog sends every field on every save, so the page passes the dialog's value through `followUpPatch` (ask-next.ts), which leaves all three out when the trimmed question is unchanged — writing them anyway re-dates the question and reorders Ask next. "Asked it" clears all three (`clearFollowUp`, with no rollup refresh — a follow-up is not in it); saving an answer first creates a new detail, and if the clear then fails the line says so and does NOT offer to save the answer again (it would file twice). **An open "Asked it" holds the question it was opened on**: Firestore applies `updateDoc` to the local cache before the server answers, so the listener shows the question cleared at once and Ask next moves on — a line keyed per question remounts, closes the panel as if the clear had worked, loses "The answer is saved…", and a retry would act on another detail. `AskNextLine` keeps the opened question, every action names it, and the page gives the line no per-question key; a fake Firestore that writes nothing locally first cannot catch this (`AskNextLine.render.test.tsx`, and `holdUpdate` in `FordPage.render.test.tsx`). A question on a detail Ask next is not showing is marked with the question itself ("follow up next time: “…”"), and one on an unfiled capture shows in the tray — a question nobody can see is a write with no reader.

- **`useClientJournal` says what it could read, and hands out the sessions it already streams** (client codex, phase 1). `loadState` is `{ notes, focuses, sessions }`, each `loading | ready | failed`, keyed by client so the last client's answer never stands for this one; a failed group is "couldn't load", never "No notes yet". `recentSessions` is the 40 newest session documents from the listener the hook already runs for each session's own note (the session summary, `sessions.notes`: the Note for the next trainer, and older sessions' summaries) — **a screen holding the journal must not open a second sessions query.** It is gated on the sessions listener itself (`loadState.sessions === "ready"`), not on "anything answered for this client": the listeners are re-created per client but the state is not, so gating on any answer handed out the LAST client's sessions whenever the notes listener answered first. The legacy lists are emptied on every resubscribe for the same reason.

- **The Story is built once, from the tab's one load, and a source that is not ready makes no moment** (client codex, phase 15, Sep 24 2026). `buildStory` (`client-story/story.ts`) is memoised in `useCodexData` as `CodexData.story`; the Story page and the Overview's Story slot read that one — never build a second, and never give the page a read of its own. Each source arrives as a `Src<T>` from `storySourcesOf` (`codex-data.ts`): `ready`, `loading`, `failed`, or `off` (FORD at a cross-train studio). A new source goes through it with all four states — passing `[]` for "not read yet" would quietly drop her moments and let the page say "Nothing recorded yet." "First Pulse in Journey" rests on `AssessmentHistory.complete`, honest only because the history is built from the RAW progress-reports page (`pulseFromReports`). **Nothing dated after the studio's today is a moment**, and the shell's render test runs on the real clock — a Story fixture there must be dated in the past or it silently disappears. The Story's "since" (`storySince`) is its own function, not `resolveClientSince`, because it has a third kind ("since at least") and reads the prior record's `from`; since the landing (Sep 24 2026) both count Journey's first session only when Journey holds her whole story, so the header and the Story no longer disagree about a long-standing client whose only date is the day Journey met her. **An empty filter is unknown while a read feeding it is missing**: `unreadUnder` decides it from `STORY_READ_KIND`, so a new read must be filed there under the kind of moment it gives, or its filter will say "Nothing under … yet" while it is unread. The prior record's `through` is typed with no maximum, so the panel for those years is moved back to today rather than dropped with the after-today moments.

- **Account reads a linked client's identity off the RECORD, never the form, and never calls on-hand sessions "left"** (client codex, phase 16, Sep 24 2026 — `src/features/client-admin/README.md`). The record form re-seeds from the client only while nothing on the tab is unsaved, so with any edit pending its copy of her name, phone or birthday can be older than what Master Sync just wrote: `contactFacts` (`client-admin/account.ts`) reads the record for a client Mindbody holds (`isMindbodyLinked`) and the form only for one it does not, whose identity the coach types. Only the renewal snapshot's `sessionsLeft` is "left" (with "(estimated)" when its source says so); what Mindbody says she holds right now is "on hand" — for a monthly client it is the few sessions between payments — counted ONE way, `onHandTotal` (every pricing option with sessions, as the fine print lists them), by the package card and the sub-toggle alike: the renewal snapshot's `sessionsOnHand` leaves out options the package table does not match, so reading it in one place and the pricing options in another printed "8 on hand" beside a sub-toggle's "5". The sub-toggle's `accountTabHint` prints no estimate at all. A package paid in full has no charge date: its date is the renewal's `runOutDate`, an estimate, said so. The interim disabled fieldset (`RecordLock`) is gone: each page gates its own editors on `canEdit`, and the form's `canEdit` is the backstop.

- **The intake matcher copies a Mindbody line only at a tap, only to one place, and never judges medical coverage** (client codex, phase 17, Sep 24 2026 — `src/features/client-admin/README.md` → The intake matcher). Account's Mindbody account notes card (`IntakeNotesCard`, over `intake.ts`) is the ONE place a line of `mindbodyNotes` is copied anywhere; Goals & Focus shows the Goals lines read only, and FORD's bands have no intake slot — don't add a second offer on another page, or the same line gets added twice. Occ and Goals fill `occupation` / `globalNotes` only while EMPTY; Med is ADDED to `medicalHistory` (never in place of it) unless those exact words are already there, whatever Body holds — a sentence saying the history "covers" or "matches" the line is the app judging a medical record, and the tests forbid the words. Those three are staged on the ONE form; Activity is a FORD detail and saves at once (`origin: "mindbody_intake"`, stamped `fordStudioIdOf(client)` and the Auth uid), so it is offered only once FORD answered — with FORD loading, failed or refused the row says so and offers nothing, because a second copy is the one harm a tap can do. The FORD tap is guarded by a ref, not only the disabled button: a second tap lands before the re-render. The last line of notes at the sync's 1,000-character cap may be cut mid-sentence and is never offered. A job title that is itself a retirement ("Retired dental hygienist.", which the Occ tap copies verbatim) is kept as the Work band's sentence (`workSentence`); only the old list's two status entries ("Retired (Active Lifestyle)") say nothing about the work. "These exact words are already there" compares WHOLE words in order (`holdsWords`), never a substring — "knee" is not in "Kneecap fracture", and a substring test there is a false medical claim that hides the offer. **Offer a FORD add on `codexAccess().fordWritable`, never on `canEdit`:** the FORD create rule (`isTrainerOfStudio`) has no administrator or franchise clause, so an administrator who works elsewhere may edit the record and read FORD but is refused a new detail — and the refusal reads like a dropped connection. (The FORD page's own adds, Notes' FORD / Life and the header's Note dialog had the same gap until the cleanup, phase 19 — the trap above.)

- **A yes/no field saved through the codex record form must be an OBJECT, never a bare boolean** (auto-renew box, Sep 25 2026). `record-form.ts` counts `undefined`, `null`, `""`, `false` and `[]` as the same "nothing", so an untick (`false`) over a field that was never set is never dirty and never saved — the Save bar simply doesn't appear. `autoRenewMark` is `{ renews, contractId, setAt, setById, setByName }` for that reason, as the tier lock (`contractTierOverride`) is; clearing it is `null`. Keep such a key out of `BLANK` (seed it only when the record has it), and tap back to the SAVED object itself (`markAfterTap`) so an undo leaves the form clean.

- **A reader that already has the tab's load passes it in; given one, the piece reads nothing** (client codex, phase 6, Sep 24 2026). `InBodyCard` takes `inbody`, `ClientCheckInPanel` takes `draft` (and `FordSection` took `ford` until the FORD page replaced it in phase 10 — the FORD page has no hook at all); each disables its own hook rather than skipping it (hooks cannot be conditional), exactly as the old journal area (`ClientJournalTab`, deleted in phase 19) did with `journal`. Left out, each reads for itself as before. `useNoteDismissalsState(uid)` says whether the trainer's dismissals have been read (`loading · ready · failed`) — `useNoteDismissals` returns `{}` until then, which cannot be told from "nothing hushed". **ClientProfileView is not remounted per client**, so an answer it holds is stamped with the client it is for and read through `answerFor` (`src/features/client-profile/client-answer.ts`): `progressReportsStatus` (the reports list is not cleared on a client change, so filter it by `clientId` AND wait for `ready`) and `journeyCompletedCount` (null until counted — `calculatedSessionCount` starts at 0 and keeps the last client's total, so never read it as "new").

- **A dialog the header or a notice opens lives in the profile's frame, never inside a tab panel** (session record, phase 6, Sep 26 2026). The profile's tabs draw only the panel on screen, so a dialog drawn inside Programming does not exist while Journey, Notes & Profile or the Activity Archive is showing. The header's Discard and the stale notice's "Discard it" set `discardTarget` and nothing opened; the question then appeared out of nowhere the next time Programming was opened. The Discard question now sits after `</Tabs>`, and `ClientProfileView.discard.test.ts` checks it stays there. The same holds for any dialog a control outside the tabs opens. **A routine card says the Active Session's routine, never a choice the session does not read**: "Use today" wrote `preferredTodayRoutineId`, which the tracker never read, so the card could say A while the session ran B. Both now read `nextRoutine` (`src/features/routines/next-routine.ts`), and the field is retired but still on older records.

- **Routine B's swaps are tied to PLACES in Routine A, and a swap is in B only when B lacks the A machine it replaces** (B molded in, the review of Round 2, Oct 9 2026; `routine-plan/b-routine.ts`). Three ways it went wrong before the review: counting a swap as made because B held its machine (A taking B's planned machine from its own road made B read "2 of 3", drew the machine twice, and the next change to A dropped the A machine from B); tying a swap to the A machine's id (A swapping Leg Press for Leg Curl left B with Leg Extension AND Leg Curl, four machines for A's three: `bSwapsAfterA` moves the swap to the new machine at the same place, written with B as `plan.swaps`); and rebuilding B from A without carrying a place a trainer took out of B (`droppedFromB`). Swap in stops at a swap that would only add or take out a machine (`bSwapWait`), and B's offers leave out A's machines still to come (`aIntended`). Any new reader of B goes through `swapsMade` / `bColumnOf`, never `bRoutine.includes(swap.with)`; any new writer that moves Routine A's machines asks `bFollowOf` and writes its answer in the same batch (`withBFollowing`). A Wrap-up after a B session makes a ticked swap (`bAfterTicks`), never `routineWith` alone, which adds it beside the A machine.

- **The B switch never decides off routines it hasn't read** (Round 2, Oct 9 2026). `useBSwitch` asks `bSwitchStep(on, routineB, routinesKnown)`: an unread or failed list is "can't tell", never "no Routine B" (which would open Plan B over a B that may hold machines, saying "Routine A has no machines yet").

- **Every writer that moves Routine A's machines writes Routine B in the same batch** (B follows A, Round 2 and the whole-branch review, Oct 9 2026). The Lineup, the Edit routine drawer, the session's plan sheet and the Wrap-up's Next time each ask `bFollowForPlanWrite` (`routine-plan/b-routine.ts`: `bFollowOf` for a started B, `plannedBFollowOf` for a B planned with A, then the client's can't-do) and hand its answer to `store.ts`, which writes B beside A. A writer that forgets leaves B holding A's old machine beside the new one (four machines for A's three before the review), or a planned B's swap pointing at a machine A no longer has. A Routine B of its own from before Round 2 (no plan of swaps) is left alone: the answer is null.

- **Programming's routines are read live, and a plan's first write never carries the routine's machines** (the whole-branch review, Oct 9 2026). A change made from the floor while the profile is open used to be rewritten from the profile's old copy; the profile now listens to the client's routines (one listener, at most two documents), and an empty answer from the iPad's cache is "loading", never "no routine" (the speed round's rule). Keeping a starting lineup on a routine that is empty writes the plan without `machineIds`, so it never empties a Routine A another iPad filled meanwhile. And the drawer keeps the plan: a drawer save on a routine with a plan writes the matching plan change in the same batch (`drawer-sync.ts` through `store.ts`'s `saveRoutineEdit`), or the routine and its plan drift apart.

<a id="tracker"></a>

## The Active Session and set data

- **Set data has four outcomes; only `performed` counts.** Read an outcome through `outcomeOf()` / `isPerformedLog()` in `src/lib/set-outcome.ts`, never off the `outcome` field (older logs don't have it — a count means performed, no count means skipped). Every average, rollup, "last time" and progression figure filters to performed sets; a new reader of `exerciseLogs` does the same. Session start seeds a weight-only log for every planned machine, so a weight alone is not "the trainer worked on this" — `isBegunLog()` is. **Never block a save**: End Session confirms, it does not refuse (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §1.6, `docs/rounds/2026-09-12-floor-round.md`).

- **Nothing on the Active Session deletes a session except Discard.** A 60-minute "abandoned session" loop used to `deleteSession` with every set in it; it is gone (tracker round, Sep 13). Abandonment is a read-side rule (`isSessionValid`). The bottom tab resumes the trainer's own live session after a crash (`src/lib/live-session.ts`); per-machine time is `src/lib/machine-clock.ts` and runs only while a machine is current — never "since the last one". `docs/rounds/2026-09-13-tracker-round.md`.

- **A read-side rule only works if EVERY reader reads it** (Sep 24 2026). The profile hid a stale In-Progress session and offered Start, but the Active Session adopted any In-Progress session, so Start the next morning reopened yesterday's abandoned session and filed the day's sets under yesterday's date. Anything that decides which In-Progress session is running goes through `splitInProgress` (`src/lib/live-session.ts`): `live` is carried on with silently, `stale` only after the trainer answers `StaleSessionDialog` (Resume / Start a new session) — a confirm, never a block. The session already on screen is never taken away because its heartbeat aged. The device's remembered id is followed only while live, and only for the client on screen — compare against the `clientId` prop, not `selectedClient`, which is still null when a mount effect runs. A stale session is discarded from the profile's unfinished-session notice. Nothing closes one by itself: what should happen to it is AJ's open decision. `docs/rounds/2026-09-24-stale-sessions.md`. **Since Oct 2 2026 the question has a third answer, "Finish it as it was"** (AJ): `completeWorkoutSession(..., { asOfDay: s.date })` finishes it under its own day, by the trainer who ran it, counting it as ONE more session (never `sessionCount = sessionNumber`, which would renumber her count back), moving `lastSessionDate` only forward, and writing a machine's `currentMachineMetrics` and next weight only where nothing newer is on file. Any new late-finish path passes `asOfDay`.

- **The session is saved at End Session, once.** `commitEndSession` is the only caller of `completeWorkoutSession` (its counters are `increment()`s — a second call double-counts). The Wrap-up only appends: `dose` by `updateDoc`, the Profile note by `createJournalEntry`. There is no Finalize button to bring back. **`completeWorkoutSession` writes no journal note** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026): its signature is `(db, session, client, logs, currentSessionNotes, authTrainer, clientMachineSettings, userId, sessionExtras?)` and it returns `{ totalsSaved }`; the post-session screen's data (`PostSessionData`, `noteSaved`) and `importanceFromPriority` are gone. The tracker files the Note for the next trainer's journal copy, and the Wrap-up writes the dose and the Profile note itself.

- **Never await the database's answer on the floor** (session record, Sep 26 2026). A write is on the iPad the moment it is made; its promise waits for the SERVER, which offline never answers. Finish (which carries the Note for the next trainer onto the session), the Profile note and the unsaved draft wait through `settleOrQueue` (`src/features/session-record/finish-wait.ts`): at most `FINISH_WAIT_MS`, never while offline, and past it the screen says "saved on this iPad". The next-trainer note's journal copy fires and forgets with a toast. A new write on the Active Session or the post-session screen does the same, or fires and forgets with a toast on failure. Finish runs once (`finishingRef`) and asks the server first whether another iPad already finished the session (`finishedElsewhere`). The sessions listener leaves a Finish in flight alone; clearing the session there flipped the screen to the briefing mid-Finish.

- **Another trainer's session is watched, never adopted** (session record, phase 7, Sep 26 2026). The Active Session holds it as `watchedSession`, apart from `currentSession`, and draws `WatchingSession` in place of the recording screen, so no effect, sheet or write keyed on `currentSession` runs while watching. Every way a session reaches the screen asks `isAnotherTrainersSession` (`src/lib/live-session.ts`) with `myTrainerIds`: the client's sessions stream, the open-session stream, the device's remembered id and Resume on a stale session. The ids are the trainer document's, the sign-in uid and a claimed placeholder's, because older accounts differ. A new way in must ask it too, and must fail toward recording: a session with no trainer, or a person the app cannot identify, is never someone else's. A take-over writes `takeOverPatch` and is protected for `JUST_STARTED_GRACE_MS` from a snapshot that has not caught up with it. The iPad it was taken from sends its waiting sets (`flushAllLogWrites`) before it turns to watching. The grid's display memos read `shownSession`; a write never does.

- **A write made after sign-out is made as nobody** (session record, Sep 26 2026). Anything queued in a screen and sent on unmount is sent AFTER the sign-out that unmounted it, and the database refuses it. The Active Session sends its waiting sets on `SEND_SETS_NOW_EVENT`, which `logOut` raises first (`src/features/session-record/sign-out-check.ts`). A new screen that queues writes listens for it too. Firestore also keeps each person's unsent writes under that person, so `logOut` asks before signing out with saves unconfirmed.

- **A set still in the write queue wins over the snapshot** (session record, Sep 26 2026). The `exerciseLogs` listener builds `logs` through `keepPendingEdits` (`src/lib/pending-log-edits.ts`), never `setLogs(snapshotMap)`. When another machine's save lands, the snapshot carries this set's older numbers, and a plain rebuild reverts what the trainer is typing. A new writer of set data goes through `queueLogWrite`, so the listener knows the set is pending. A tap that finishes something is sent at once (`sendsAtOnce`), and leaving a typed field sends it (`SessionNowBar`'s `onCommit`). `docs/rounds/2026-09-26-session-record.md`.

- **`WorkoutTrackerView` draws five screens and the order is a rule**, `lib/tracker-screen.ts`: post-session (the Wrap-up) first while its snapshot exists, then watch (another trainer's session, read-only), then none / briefing / tracker. The client's sessions stream turns pre-session mode on whenever nothing is In-Progress — including the beat after Finish — so never check the briefing before the post-session screen. "none" is a real screen since Sep 26 2026 (`NothingOnScreen`, session record): never return null there, because the routing does reach it.

- **Sessions still use the app-wide machine list**, not each studio's roster, so a studio's own or adopted machines aren't in the session picker yet (ROADMAP).

- **The machine menu (Oct 4 2026) — read `src/features/machine-menu/README.md` before touching the card, the Journey grid's machine names, or anything that writes at a machine.** One card in both doors (a session and the profile), and the Notes block is the only layout difference (`doors.ts`, held by `doors.test.ts`). It found three traps: the two below, and "A settings copy and a Set-up note have the same kind and category" under Ratings, notes and Pulse.

- **A set's settings snapshot is the saved settings at WRITE time, not the settings the set was done on** (machine menu, Oct 4 2026). The tracker writes `machineSettings` onto an `exerciseLog` with EVERY write — Start's seed, each typed number, Finish's not-reached logs — from the client's saved settings at that moment (`WorkoutTrackerView`, `currentSettings[...]`), while the log's `createdAt` stays the moment Start seeded it. So a change saved mid-session, after Start, is already in that session's snapshot, although its `settingHistory` row's timestamp is later than the set's `createdAt`: compare the two times and the change looks as if it came after the session, and a chart draws it twice. A set entered on Log past session carries `{}` ("not recorded"), never "nothing set". The Staircase's model handles all three (`timeline-model.ts`: a change whose netted values the column already holds is drawn once, before it; `{}` never makes a boundary; another studio's snapshot is never compared). Anything else that reads set-up history off the sets does the same, or reads `machines/{id}/settingHistory` instead.

- **Never await Firestore writes one after another on the floor** (machine menu, Oct 4 2026). `saveSettings` used to `await` the settings document, THEN the `settingHistory` row, THEN the journal copy. Offline the first promise never answers, so the second and third were never even issued, and a reload or a new-version load dropped them: a setting with no history and no note. An awaited write is not "saved", it is "the server answered". Issue EVERY write before anything is awaited: what must land together goes in ONE `writeBatch` (make the new document's ref up front, `doc(collection(db, ...))`, as `machine-fit/setup-save.ts` does); a copy that must never fail the save (the journal copy, machine fit's row) is issued in the same tick and caught; and the floor waits on the batch's promise only through `settleOrQueue` (`session-record/finish-wait.ts`). Firestore's persistent cache then holds every write through a reload. `equipment/mutations.test.ts` checks the order (a fake `writeBatch` that never commits, and the journal write already issued). The same holds for any new screen that saves more than one document at a machine.

### The speed round (Oct 5 2026)

- **A session's writes are issued, never awaited on the UI path** (speed round, R9; `src/features/session-record/README.md`). Start makes its ids on the iPad (`doc(collection(db, "sessions"))`) and issues ONE `writeBatch` of the session, the routine (only when one is needed) and the seed sets; Discard is one batch; Back to Hub issues both notes and navigates; the machine menu's Save and Undo use `settleOrQueue(write, online, 0)`. Offline a Firestore write never answers until the Wi-Fi is back, so an `await` on a tap is a screen that hangs. A refusal comes back through the write's promise and is said in a toast. A Start refused LATE (on reconnect, after a whole offline session) sends its waiting set writes and deletes only the untouched prefills (`refusedStartSweep`): a set the trainer typed into, or one this iPad wrote, is the only record of what the client lifted and is never deleted (the final review, Oct 6 2026). A new button that writes follows the same shape: issue, move on, report a refusal.
- **Never put a write a role may be refused inside a batch with the session** (speed round, R9). A batch is all or nothing: a visiting or cross-train trainer's refused `clients` field (`isRoutineBActive`, `firstSessionDate`) would roll back the whole Start, so the client fields are their own write. The same holds for deletes: Discard used to delete the legacy `sessionNotes`, which only an administrator may delete, and that one refusal stopped a trainer's Discard halfway; it no longer touches them.
- **An empty list is not "none" until the server says so** (speed round, R9). A listener that has not answered, or an EMPTY answer from the iPad's cache (`metadata.fromCache`), must not create anything: gate a create (Routine A) or a prefill on a known flag, never on `length === 0`, and never gate Start or Finish on it. To hear the server confirm an empty cached list (no document changes, so no ordinary snapshot), listen with `includeMetadataChanges` and ignore metadata-only answers for the data. A settings read that FAILS before any answer counts as "no settings", so the last performed weights still prefill.
- **The seed never writes over a weight already on the iPad** (speed round, R9): `seedLogs` skips any log key the iPad holds or has queued; both writes merge.
- **An empty `sessionMachineIds` on record is an answer, not a gap** (the first-session design round, Oct 8 2026). Start no longer makes a routine of today's list, so a walk-in started with nothing chosen records `[]` and no routine; the tracker's once-per-session seeding runs that empty list, and a watching iPad's `sessionMachineList` (`session-record/watch.ts`) reads it by the same rule: two readers of one list, kept in step. Only a session with NO list on record and no routine takes the floor as its list: an open session started before Oct 9 2026. One started since records its empty list and opens the FileMaker floor as a VIEW (`features/open-session/README.md`), never as today's list. Falling back to the floor on `length === 0` drew every machine as today's routine. And a routine read for a session runs `todayFor({ routine, plan })`, never `routine.machineIds` alone: an EMPTY Routine A with a plan runs its day one (the consult is not Routine A).
- **A session seeded from Routine A reads `todayFor`, never `routineA.machineIds`** (the first-session design round, Oct 8 2026: "the consult is not Routine A"). A kept starting plan leaves Routine A EMPTY and keeps the first visit's machines as the plan's `dayOne`; `todayFor({ routine, plan })` (`routine-plan/plan.ts`) is the routine's machines, else day one, else none. Every reader that seeds or draws today's machines asks it: the briefing (the routine it picks, the list it compares against, the ids Start passes), the tracker's fallback for a session with no list on record, the watching screen (`session-record/watch.ts`), Log past session, and any caller of `nextRoutine`. A new reader of `machineIds` opens the consult empty, with no error anywhere. A session on Routine B reads `runnableToday`, which leaves out what the client can't do.
- **The client's document never rides in the session's batch or the Wrap-up's, the B switch included** (the whole-branch review, Oct 9 2026; the speed round's rule above, found twice more). Start turned B off over an empty Routine B, and the Wrap-up turned B on when its ticks started a planned B, each inside its own batch; for a client the rules refuse an update to (a cross-train trainer's, or one with no last name) that refusal took the session, or the ticks that start Routine A, down with it. `setRoutineBActive` (`routine-plan/store.ts`) is its own write, and B goes on only after the Wrap-up's batch lands.
- **An add before today's list is seeded is recorded as the session's whole list** (the floor on day one, Oct 8 to 9 2026). So the Now Bar offers nothing to add until the list is seeded, and a session started before the client's routines answered (the consult on slow Wi-Fi) has today's list on screen from Start, with what the trainer adds while the routines load kept beside the routine's machines when they come (`followUpList`). Next time at Finish is worked out in its own `try`: a plan it can't read costs the card, never the Wrap-up.
- **No write names an empty client; an open session keeps its settings on the session** (the open session round, Oct 9 2026; finding 4, AJ's "3a"). The machine card in an open session handed `saveSettings` `clientId: ""`, which wrote `clientMachineSettings/_{machineId}`: one ghost record per machine, shared by every open session and never moved to the client (old ones may still be in production; nothing reads an empty client). The card now keeps them at `sessions/{id}.heldSetup.{machineId}` and Assign saves them to the client in its own batch, deleting the field there (`features/open-session/held-setup.ts`). `saveSettings` throws with no client and is no longer `async` (call it inside a try, as both callers do); `addMachineNote` and `acknowledgeFlag` refuse one too. A move at Assign writes only the dials it set, by name (`dialsOnly`, `writeDials`): the client's settings may not have been read on this iPad, and the usual whole-map write would put back only what `saved` had. Its journal copy and fit row wait for the batch's commit (`queueSettingsSave`'s `afterCommit`), and only the server's answer claims a first set-up or writes the fit row (it is written whole). An Undo goes where its save went, and a keep is refused once the assign batch is built: a save kept on the session must never be undone onto the client the card now shows.
- **The floor is a view, never the recorded list** (the open session round, Oct 9 2026; AJ's "1b"). An open session, and a client session with no routine and an empty list, open the grid's fold on every machine of the floor, each with a +, but the session records ONLY what + added (`sessionMachineIds`). Never write the floor into `sessionMachineIds`, and never read a session whose list is the whole floor as one that ran nothing chosen: `routine-plan/next-time.ts` `ranWholeFloorUnchosen` is the one rule (a session with a list of its own is never Free, and gets Next time, even when every machine was added in the trainer's order), and only an OLD Free session (no routine, no list of its own) is read as before. Laying a routine (Start from a routine…) goes through the one recorder, `applySessionMachineIds`, and writes no routine (`routine-builder/session-scope.test.ts`); the client's own Routine A or B laid is kept in `laidRoutineRef`, never written on the session. And for a client, the floor waits until the routines are known and a Start that couldn't decide its routine has: opened off an empty answer it opens for a frame and folds again.
- **`heldSetup` is read as what is saved, and is never undone across Assign** (the open session round, Oct 9 2026; AJ's "3a"). The card, the Now Bar's Set up, the grid's chips and each set typed after it read the session's held values as the client's saved ones (`open-session/held-setup.ts` `heldValuesOf`, `heldAsSettings`; the tracker's `shownSettings`), so a new reader of a client's settings in an open session reads them through the same door or shows a held set-up as "not set". The toast's Undo outlives the card and can outlive Assign: it goes where its save went (`DialTiles`' `SaveTo`), and a keep is refused once the assign batch is built (`heldClosedRef`), so a held save is never undone onto the client the card now shows. Until the client's settings listener has had the SERVER's answer after Assign, the card writes only the dials it changes (`settingsUnsure`): the iPad's copy may hold only the dials Assign moved, and a whole-map save would wipe the rest.
- **A dial's editor is judged once, as it opens, from the tile and the SAVED value, never from the draft** (the open session round, Oct 9 2026, finding 5). `DialTiles`' `judgeEditor` decides the row or the field and its keypad from what is on the tile when the editor opens, and holds it while it is open (`editorLock`). Judged on every render from the half-typed draft, the first digit typed into an empty dial turned the field into a one-position row ("12" cost eleven + taps); judged again when machine fit or the record answered, it swapped under the finger. A dial is EMPTY only when the tile and the saved value are both blank, and an empty dial is always typed, on the number pad for a number: the studio's span and the record's may not hold a new client's value. What counts as a change is judged against the saved value too: "Why?" is asked only when a SAVED value changes (a blank dial filled is not a change), and asked, never required, there and on Programming → Setup. A first save on a machine with no Gap standard wrote Gap 0; a value nobody typed is never written.
- **The journal stream's index-less fallback, at its limit, is not the newest 300** (speed round, R11). `useClientJournal` sets `newest: false` then, and `sessionJournalOf` hands the read back to the screen. The machine menu's `machineJournalOf` has the same gap and was left alone.
- **A shared hook that builds a map in render re-runs every memo below it on every render** (speed round, R10). `useMachineCatalog` built `byId` afresh each time, which rebuilt the floor and every grid row on every screen using `useStudioMachines`; it is memoised now. Memoise derived maps in shared hooks, and keep a one-second clock in the leaf that shows it (the Now Bar, the send line), never in the tracker's state.

<a id="renewals"></a>

## Packages, renewals and InBody

- **Package facts come from `client.renewal`** (the nightly snapshot), read through `src/lib/directory-row.ts` on lists — never from `packageTier`, `remainingSessions` or `nextSessionDate`, which nothing keeps current. There is no field for leader-granted extra sessions yet.

- **`clients/{id}.renewal` belongs to the nightly job.** The rules refuse any app write that changes it, so never write a whole client object back — write only the fields that changed.

- **A single-client screen reads `renewalOf(client)`, never `client.renewal` directly** (auto-renew, Sep 25 2026 — `src/features/renewals/auto-renew.ts`). The stored snapshot is last night's; a trainer's "On auto-renewal" mark on the profile, or a Mindbody webhook that landed today, changes whether the contract renews NOW. `renewalOf` re-decides it (and only ever turns a before-the-charge warning off), so the profile header, the package card, post-session, the briefing, the Hub marker and the progress-report cue agree the moment the mark is saved. Lists may read `client.renewal` and follow the nightly run. Whether a contract auto-renews is decided only by `decideAutoRenew` (nothing under a paid-in-full lock > Mindbody > the mark on THIS contract > nothing for an unmatched package > the package > the studio > ON): never test `isAutoRenewing` or a studio setting on your own. The two readers of Mindbody's raw flag that remain are its record of each contract — the contract history's tiles (`client-admin/contract.ts` → `membershipTimeline`) and the profile header's no-count fallback (`client-profile/client-package.ts`) — and both drop "Auto-renews" on a running contract under a paid-in-full or banked-sessions lock (`lockSaysNothingBills`). A null answer is not a "yes": the lock's null must never bring back a before-the-charge warning a "no" had switched off, so the engine and `renewalOf` give no warning under the lock.

- **A studio's auto-renew answer is absent until it answers, and absent reads as ON** (Sep 25 2026). A FAILED settings read must never be read as "absent": `useLiveRenewal` gives no live snapshot then, and Operations → Renewals only flags "hasn't answered" once the read succeeded for that studio. The rule (`renewalSettingsValid`) checks the whole merged document, so a key added to `config/renewals` needs the rules deployed before the app that writes it — and rolling the rules back after a studio stored it locks that studio's renewal settings.

- **A renewal cycle is checked as a whole document.** A leader's write to `studios/{s}/renewals/{cycleKey}` must fit `renewalCycleKeys()` in `firestore.rules`. Any new field the job writes has to be added there, or leaders are locked out of that cycle.

- **Pricing options are replaced, contracts are merged.** Each pull replaces `client.mindbodyServices` whole and merges `client.mindbodyContracts`. Mindbody dates are read as UTC days (`mindbodyDayKey`).

- **Attendance before a studio's first synced booking is unknown, not zero.** Pace and proof say so rather than showing "no visits".

- **InBody is health data.** Scans live in `clients/{id}/inbodyScans` under the sessions-style rule. Never copy InBody numbers into `progressReports`, which any signed-in user can read.

- **An InBody change is only called a change beyond the client's HOME studio's variation** (client codex, phase 2, Sep 24 2026; AJ's decision 8). The scanner reads the same body differently each time — 3.5 lb of muscle, 5.3 lb of fat mass and 2.7 points of body fat by default, each studio's own on My Studio → Studio (`studios/{id}.inbodyVariation`). Every word or colour about an InBody change goes through `src/features/inbody/variation.ts` (`callChange`, and `formatCalledChange` / `changeTone` / `summarySentence` in `scans.ts`); **never compare a delta with 0 to decide it is progress** — that is how "+1.2 lb muscle" became renewal proof and an upgrade candidate. The variation is a REQUIRED argument on every reader, so the typecheck names each one; pass the CLIENT — `useInBodyVariation(client)`, or `useInBodyVariationLookup()` for a list (a lookup in the studios already in context — no read) — never a studio id: neither hook takes one, so no screen can hand it the active studio. `clients/{id}.inbodySummary` and `renewal.proof.inbody` stay RAW deltas and the nightly job does not read the variation: it is applied only at display. "Use Max Strength's defaults" DELETES the field, so absent always means the defaults. Weight has no variation and is never toned.

<a id="layout"></a>

## Layout and CSS

- **A name in a fixed-height row clips** (names wrap, Sep 29 2026). Sixteen places cut a person's or a machine's name short with `truncate`, `text-overflow: ellipsis` or `nowrap` in an `overflow: hidden` box: the live session's bar and Now Bar, the calendar's blocks and lanes, history's rows, My Profile's lists, the top strip's studio name. The rule: a name wraps (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), and a row that holds one has a `min-height`, never a `height`. `src/lib/names-wrap.test.ts` holds every one of them.
- **A flex row that lets its text shrink can clip a number** (hub cherry round, Sep 28 2026). The Hub card's time line shrank with `overflow: hidden` when its glyphs needed the room, so "#212" could read "#2" — a confident wrong number, and no test sees it, because tests draw no layout. Keep a number in a part that never shrinks (`.hs-card-when`, `flex: none`) and let words give way beside it (`.hs-card-rest`, `text-overflow: ellipsis`).
- **"Say less" means drop whole, never cut** (hub fixes, Oct 1 2026). The Hub card's "New to Jo…" came from `text-overflow: ellipsis` on the words after the time. A part that may give way now sits in a ONE-LINE-HIGH wrapping flex row with `overflow: hidden`, each part `flex: none`, so a part that doesn't fit drops to the hidden second line whole. Two details make it work, both found only in a browser: a flex line always takes its FIRST item even when it doesn't fit (so a zero-width lead goes first), and `align-items: baseline` beside an empty lead pushes the parts 4px down and clips their bottoms (use `flex-start`). And don't drop a number UNDER a name in a fixed-height card: under a name that already wraps it makes a third line and pushes the time off a half-hour card; float the number at the right of the name's first line instead, and give the block the name's own line height.

- **The status bar and the home indicator are paid for ONCE, at the true edge** (the Home Screen app, Sep 26 2026). `index.html` has `viewport-fit=cover`, so the page runs under the home indicator: about 20px at the bottom. The status bar is the `default` style since Sep 27 (the trap below), which iPadOS draws above the page, so the top inset is 0 in the Home Screen app; under `black-translucent` the page ran under the status bar too, about 24px. The shell's `StatusBarStrip` pays the top for everything in the shell, and `AppBottomBar` pays the bottom on every signed-in screen. Anything inside `<main>` must NOT add `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` again. Before the Home Screen round every inset was 0 (there was no `viewport-fit=cover`), so three such footers had been paying it twice unseen, and the moment the insets turned on they became 20px of dead space. Only a layer that covers the bar or reaches the top of the screen (a sheet, a full-height slide-over, a screen shown before the shell) pays its own, with the `pt-safe` / `pb-safe` utilities. `features/home-screen/home-screen.test.ts` lists every file that pays one, with the reason; a new one fails the test until it is added. If the style ever goes back to `black-translucent`, the status bar's text is always WHITE there: in the light theme the `.status-band` paints the dark neutral under it, so never remove the band. Emulate the insets with headless Chrome's `Emulation.setSafeAreaInsetsOverride` (the round document has the script); a desktop browser always reports 0.
- **Keep the Home Screen app's status bar `default`: iPadOS 26 cuts the bottom off a `black-translucent` app** (AJ's iPad, Sep 27 2026). Under `black-translucent` iPadOS lays the app out a status bar taller than it shows it. The page's last 20 to 24 points fall into a black strip at the foot of the screen, and the bottom bar's labels were cut in half. No CSS or script gets the strip back, because iPadOS does not draw the page there; other web apps hit the same WebKit bug on iOS 26 and made the same switch. Neither a desktop browser nor headless Chrome shows it: only an iPad does. **iOS reads the style when the icon is added**, so any change to it is seen only after the Home Screen icon is deleted and added again. `home-screen.test.ts` holds `default`; `features/home-screen/README.md` says how to go back once iPadOS fixes the bug.

- **A component imports the stylesheet it draws with** (client codex, phase 19, Sep 24 2026). The app is split into chunks, and a stylesheet arrives with the chunk that IMPORTS it, not with the first component that names its classes. The FORD detail dialog draws with `ford.css`'s capture classes but never imported it; the FORD hub that did lived in the profile's chunk, and when the codex deleted the hub only the session and Operations chunks still imported `ford.css` — so the dialog opened UNSTYLED on the profile of an iPad that had not started a session. Tests load no CSS, so a green suite, typecheck and build all missed it; it was found by listing which chunk's CSS holds the dialog's classes in the build output. `features/ford/ford-css.test.ts` now fails any component that names a `ford.css` class without importing the file. When a component moves to another screen, check its stylesheet came with it. **`src/features/my-studio/css-imports.test.ts` is the same check for My Studio** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026): `MachinesSection`, `StudioSection`, `TeamSection`, `InBodyVariationPanel` and the Context Panel each import every one of `my-studio.css`, `planner.css`, `relay.css` and `admin.css` they draw with, because Operations → Floor mounts Machines without My Studio's shell (an iPad that had never opened My Studio drew Operations' Machines unstyled). `ContextPanel.tsx` imports `relay.css` itself, and `.cp`'s width is `var(--rl-panel-w, 360px)` for the same reason.

- **A Relay Context Panel hosted outside My Studio's shell has no height to hold it** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). My Studio bounds its frame to the screen, so the panel (a machine's door) sits beside the list; Operations → Floor does not, and the door opened at the top or the foot of a long list, often off screen. Operations marks the frame `.ms__frame--hosted` (`MachinesSection`, whenever there is no Relay context), and `my-studio.css` makes the door sticky to AppContent's `<main>`: a column on the right from 900px, a sheet at the foot of the screen below it, both capped at the screen less `--ms-hosted-chrome` (12rem for the header, the bottom bar, the status strip and Operations' own top). A new host of `MachinesSection` needs the same class; check it on an iPad in the Home Screen app, where the status strip takes its share.

- **A filled button variant must restate its fill on `:hover`** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). In `studio-tasks.css` a plain `.st__btn:hover` (a light grey) beats `.st__btn--done` / `.st__btn--primary` on specificity once hovered, and an iPad keeps `:hover` after a tap, so a tapped green or blue button turned grey with white words on it and stayed that way. `.st__btn--primary:hover` and `.st__btn--done:hover` now repeat their background and on-colour. Any new filled variant of a class with a hover rule does the same.

- **One meaning per colour, in Learning and My Studio as in the codex** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026, under AJ's "I trust your color choices"). Blue acts and selects, and **every Save is solid brand blue** with its on-colour (`--wk-live-on`, `--cat-live-on`, `--tp-live-on`, `--st-live-on`), never `#fff` and never orange. Hero orange is the one loud action of a screen (Start Session; since the Navy Frame, Oct 4 2026, the logo orange with navy words, below. My Studio's Capture is a quiet round + since the Relay Board rebuild). **A flag or caution is plum** (`--eq-warn`, `--wk-warn`, `--st-flag`): a flagged machine, a late job, a tight gap. Crimson is critical or destructive only (an error, Retire, Delete, "Never to failure"); the red kaizen mark stays rep quality's. **This reverses the voice review 9 note** that Learning's flag red was the app's crimson: a flagged machine was crimson on its badge and amber in the Upkeep card below it on one page. `wiki/learning-tokens.test.ts` refuses any raw hex in Learning's sheets and the Catalog's upkeep card (`.stu`); `studio-tasks/studio-tokens.test.ts` and `my-studio/look.test.ts` do the same for My Studio.

- **One stylesheet owns a class** (voice-review round, Sep 27 2026). The other half of the chunk rule above: once a chunk's CSS has loaded it stays in the page, so two files that both define `.pk-title` style each other's screens as soon as both have been opened on one iPad, in whichever order they loaded. Relay's kit and the Packages sheet both defined seven `.pk-*` classes (the sheet a client is shown took Relay's italic capitals and a scroll area capped at 64% of the screen once My Studio had been opened); Relay's Now Bar and the note body were both `.nb` (a shared plan on Goals & Focus took the Now Bar's grid); Relay's shift rings and the Pulse report were both `.sr`. Relay's side became `.rk-*`, `.rnb` and `.shr`. `src/css-class-owners.test.ts` now fails when two stylesheets define the same class as a plain `.name` rule; a scoped rule (`.scope .x`, `button.x`) is an extension, not a second owner. The few roots shared on purpose (`.dark`, the codex's `.cx` / `.cx-kit`, the Planner's `.pl`) are listed there with their reasons. Pick a prefix no other feature uses before you name a new block.

- **A feature's colour tokens carry the app's values** (voice-review round, Sep 27 2026). `--wk-*` (the Catalog and the Academy), `--cat-*` and `--adm-*` are copies of `equipment.tokens.css`, light AND dark, held there by `features/wiki/learning-tokens.test.ts` and `features/admin/admin-tokens.test.ts`. `--st-*` (My Studio, `studio-tasks.css`) joined them in the follow-up (Sep 27 2026), held by `studio-tasks/studio-tokens.test.ts` with an explicit name map because the names differ (`--st-done` is `--eq-ok`, `--st-flag` is `--eq-warn`); its new tokens are `--st-done-on`, `--st-live-on`, `--st-hero-text`, `--st-hero-fill`, `--st-hero-on`, `--st-alert` and `--st-alert-fill`, and `--st-flag-border` matches Learning's `--wk-warn-border`.
