A hand-copied palette drifts: Learning said caution in amber while the rest of the app said it in plum, and its dark mode put white text on light orange. Change a colour in `equipment.tokens.css` and copy it, or the test fails; a token with no original there (a category accent, the figure) is the feature's own. Status words are never `text-green` / `text-amber` / `text-cta`: those are FILL colours, and as text on white they measure 1.8:1, 2.0:1 and 3.1:1 against AA's 4.5:1 — use `--eq-ok`, `--eq-warn`, `--eq-hero-text`.

- **A sticky bar in this app needs the scroller's padding negated.** The app shell is a bounded 100dvh column, so the document never scrolls — an inner `p-6` container does, and every engine pins a sticky box inside that padding. `--hist-stick-top` (History's month headers) and `--psub-stick-top` (the sub-toggle) are both that measurement. A sticky bar also needs an OPAQUE background from `--background`, or content scrolls through it. Inside the Activity Archive the month headers additionally offset by `--psub-stuck-h`, which `ProfileSubnav` publishes on the enclosing `.ptab`.

- **A `<button>` centres its own text.** Several FORD details render inside buttons so they can be opened, and a Tailwind `text-left` on them did not survive layer ordering in every build. Text alignment on a button belongs in the feature's CSS file, not in a utility class (FORD round, Sep 15).

- **One loading mark.** `components/LoadingMark.tsx` (`LoadingMark`, `LoadingArea`) is the wait state — never hand-roll another `animate-spin` div. Hub card marks come from the ONE engine the Opportunities list reads (`features/hub-opportunities/moments-today.ts`, through `features/hub-schedule/card-marks.ts`, since the calm Hub round, Sep 28 2026) and read only what the Hub already holds (no reads per card); `src/lib/hub-markers.ts` is left for the briefing.

- **A container query measures the container's INSIDE** (calm Hub round, Sep 28 2026). `@container (min-width: 196px)` compares the content box, padding and border left out, so a Hub card 218px wide with 23px of padding and edge measured 195px and the glyph's word never showed, though the card looked wide enough. `hub-card.css` asks for 176px (a card of about 200px). Measure the content box when you pick a threshold.

- **In a flex column, a card with `overflow: hidden` must be `flex: none`**, or a short container shrinks the card instead of scrolling (this was the machine sheet's "truncation"). The same rule bit the Hub card: `truncate` *is* `overflow: hidden`, so a truncated text line in a fixed-height flex column is the line the browser squeezes to 0px — give the lines that must survive `shrink-0` and let one line (`min-h-0 overflow-hidden`) be the one that yields (fix round, Sep 13).

- **Two grid items in the same named `grid-area` are drawn on top of each other**, not stacked. A block that should push content down goes in its own row (`col-span-full`, no area name) — this was the profile's "Trained by" list covering the stat tiles.

- **The base layer draws every `svg` as a block** (the screens preview, Oct 9 2026). An icon that leads a line of words sits on a line of its own above them unless the line is a flex row (`.rpl-line--icon`: `display: flex; align-items: center`, the icon `flex: none`).

- **A flex basis is a height in a column** (the screens preview, Oct 9 2026). `flex: 1 1 200px` on a title meant to grow beside a button in a row stood 200px of empty space under it when the same element sat in the card's column (the briefing's "How does Priya start?"). Scope the basis to the row (`.rpl-brief__head > .rpl-brief__title`).

### The Navy Frame (the colour round, Oct 4 2026)

`docs/rounds/2026-10-04-navy-frame.md` is the round. Every trap here has a test that fails if it comes back.

- **The dark ramp is the studio navy, not Tailwind slate.** From Sep 12 2026 the dark `--n-*` ramp (what every `slate-*` class means) had to be byte-identical to Tailwind slate, and dark mode was a navy shell over near-black content; AJ found it "so gray". The Navy Frame retinted the dark ramp and the dark tokens together, on purpose, so a `dark:bg-slate-900` is now the dark card `#14293D` and `slate-950` the ground. Don't "restore" Tailwind's values: `neutral-ramp.test.ts` pins the navy rungs, and `core-tokens.test.ts` fails on a grey (low-chroma) dark surface or a pure-white light one.

- **The frame does not follow the theme.** The header, the bottom bar and the iPad status bar are `--chrome` (`#002341`) in light AND dark (AJ's answer 1A). `HEADER_TOKEN` in `features/home-screen/theme-color.ts` is `"--chrome"`, not `--bg-dark-2`; `index.html`'s meta and pre-paint script and the manifest's `theme_color` say `#002341` whatever the theme. Don't "fix" the frame back to following the theme: `home-screen.test.ts`, `theme-color.test.ts` and `components/frame-colours.test.ts` (the frame draws only in `--chrome-*` tokens, with no `dark:` look of its own) hold it, and `core-tokens.test.ts` fails if `.dark` redefines a frame token.
- **The frame's inks vanish on a theme surface.** `headerIconClass` (in `AppContent.tsx`) paints `--chrome-ink-2` for the navy header; on the phone's avatar menu, a light popover in light mode, it would be about 1.8:1, so ThemeToggle and FeedbackButton there take `menuIconClass`. A control that sits on the frame never carries its own palette hover either: the Bug icon's `hover:stroke-orange-500` stuck after a tap on the iPad (`frame-colours.test.ts`).
- **A header search on the frame restates its dark background.** Input's own `dark:bg-input/30` beat a plain `bg-chrome-field` in dark and drew a pale box in the navy; the search says `dark:bg-chrome-field` too (`AppHeader.render.test.tsx`). Since type and depth's phase 4 the Input's fill is the card's well, `bg-(--well)` in both themes, which the search's own fill replaces the same way.
- **No white words on any orange.** White on the logo orange is 2.99:1 and on the light hero 3.99. A loud button or an orange chip with words reads the go pair: `--eq-go` / `--eq-go-on` (navy), `bg-cta text-cta-foreground`, or a copy's `--X-go` / `--X-go-on`; `--eq-hero` carries marks, never words. A filled orange button restates its fill on `:hover`, never fades or brightens. `loud-orange.test.ts` (every `.tsx`), `hub-schedule/hub-colour-rules.test.ts` and `journey-grid/session-colour-rules.test.ts` hold it.
- **A Save or a selection is the blue, never the go pair** (AJ, Oct 4 2026: "yes" to the follow-up). The phase that moved white-on-orange to navy-on-orange left eight Saves and five selections orange, because "no white on orange" was the only rule it held. Orange is now and go only (Start, Finish, the paused Resume, a screen's one loud action). A Save is `bg-primary text-primary-foreground hover:bg-primary` (or a feature palette's live pair); a picked chip or tab is the blue with its on-colour; a destructive confirm is `text-destructive` on its tint; a caution icon is plum (`--eq-warn` on `--eq-warn-fill`). `loud-orange.test.ts` names each Save and selection, and `palette-copies.test.ts` holds the Pulse's. A selection drawn in the hero orange rather than the go pair is the same mistake: the review found the Equipment tab's picked machine (`.eq-item--selected`, held by `hub-colour-rules.test.ts`) and the Pulse's switch that is on (`.sr-switch--on`), both now the blue, the picked machine's fill restated on `:hover` and on a machine not in use. A Base UI `Checkbox` is ticked as `data-checked`, so a `data-[state=checked]:` override (the Radix name) never draws.
- **Dark fills are opaque and keep their accent's hue.** Orange is the navy's complement, so ANY little orange over the navy cancels to grey: an rgba wash (`bg-cta/10`, `rgba(243,109,33,.18)`) or a `color-mix(in srgb, orange 8%, the card)`; crimson and plum washes go violet and become the same colour. Use the opaque fill (`--eq-hero-fill` `#4b2915` and its copies), or mix from it (the Floor Map's dark heat, `relay.css`). `equipment-tokens.test.ts` checks every dark fill's chroma and hue, `core-tokens.test.ts` the running tab's box, `my-studio/look.test.ts` the Floor Map, `loud-orange.test.ts` the bell and the Wrap-up's renewal button (and First-time setup, until it was retired on Oct 8 2026). A blue wash over the navy keeps its hue and is fine.
- **Blue words on a blue fill read the `-live-text` token, never `-live`.** The dark `--st-live` (`#65abe9`) is 4.4:1 on its own fill `#23405c`; `--st-live-text` (`#98caf9`) is 6.2. `--st-live` draws the edges and icons there. `studio-tokens.test.ts` fails on any stylesheet rule or dialog class string that puts `--st-live` words on the `--st-live` fill.
- **`ActiveStudioContext` must not write `--cta`.** An inline style on `<html>` beats `:root` and `.dark`, so the studio accent colour hid every new orange; it was removed with its Admins control (AJ's answer 3A). `contexts/ActiveStudioContext.render.test.tsx` holds it.
- **A Hub card rule keyed on a MISSING attribute never matches.** `HubCard` always sets `data-kind`, `data-state` and `data-recede`, so `:not([data-state])` matches nothing in the app (it worked in the mock). Key a rule on the value (`[data-state="live"]` is hubCardState's "still to come or under way, not logged"). `hub-colour-rules.test.ts` refuses a `:not([data-…])` card rule.
- **Tailwind 4 shadows need a geometry and the `color:` hint.** `shadow-(--token)/40` compiles to a broken `--tw-shadow: var(--token)`, and a colour-only `shadow-cta/40` draws nothing on its own. Write the geometry with the token's colour and the colour class beside it: `shadow-[0_0_12px_var(--cta)] shadow-cta/40`, or `shadow-[0_14px_34px_-14px_var(--eq-go)] shadow-(color:--eq-go)/40`.
- **A panel is `bg-card`, a menu `bg-popover`, the page `bg-background`.** Never `bg-white` (a pure-white panel beside the off-white cards is a brighter glare edge) and never `dark:bg-bg-dark` for a panel (that is the page). `page-grounds.test.ts` holds the grounds and the white panels.
- **`--input` is two things.** It was retuned as a control boundary (`border-input`, 3:1 on a card), and shadcn also paints it as a FILL (`dark:bg-input/30` on the Checkbox, and on every Input, Select, Textarea and outline Button until type and depth's phase 4 gave those `--well` and `--raised`; `disabled:bg-input/50` in light, and the outline Button's dark hover), so retuning it changes those fills too. Measure both before moving it. And a divider token is not a boundary: a `border-div-l` class on a Textarea overrides its `border-input` through tailwind-merge (the two required reason fields all but vanished in dark).
- **Words are never in the faint ink.** `--*-ink-faint` is 3.2:1, for chevrons, separators, empty dashes and icons; anything a person reads is `--*-ink-muted` or darker. The Hub, the session grid's small words and the Dial are held (`hub-colour-rules.test.ts`, `session-colour-rules.test.ts`); about 120 older rules elsewhere still break it and move reader by reader.
- **In the session grid a control's edge is `--jg-control-edge`, a separator's is `--jg-border-strong`** (AJ, Oct 4 2026: "yes" to firmer outlines). The Navy Frame left the Now Bar's buttons on the separators' soft line, 1.87:1 on the bar in light. The control edge is the Hub's `--eq-border-strong` (`#7a8694` / `#6e8397`, 3:1 on the bar and on a control's fill); the soft line stays on the sticky separators, the rail and the decorative edges, where 3:1 would be heavy. A new control in `journey-grid.css` takes the control edge and joins `CONTROLS` in `session-colour-rules.test.ts`, which fails on any other reader; `contrast.test.ts` holds the 3:1 in both themes.
- **Moving a ground moves AJ's own colours under 4.5:1.** The Navy Frame left his Oct 2–3 Journey-look picks (`--jg-pf-*`) alone and darkened the header band under them, which put the session numbers at 3.5–3.6:1 and the newest day's number at 3.0. They were retuned by lightness only, each hue kept (AJ, Oct 4 2026: "yes"); a token he picked changes its lightness to pass, never its hue, without asking. `contrast.test.ts` now measures every Journey-look word on its grounds: the dates on the header band and on a spotlit header (`HEADER_WORDS`; the header row is never banded, so they are measured as drawn), today's reps and prescription on the orange tile and the cells, and a set's reps on its tile, banded rows included. The newest day keeps its orange words on a spotlit header (a more specific rule than the spotlight's blue), so its orange is measured on the blue live fill too.
- **A new feature palette copies `equipment.tokens.css`, and a test holds it,** or it drifts the next time the Hub's palette moves: the six unpinned copies had drifted for a month before phase 8. The pinned ones are `admin-tokens.test.ts`, `studio-tokens.test.ts`, `learning-tokens.test.ts`, `session-colour-rules.test.ts` (the grid) and `src/palette-copies.test.ts` (briefing, Pulse, FORD, calendar, trainer profile, routine builder, the profile shell). Add a new copy to one of them.

### Type and depth (Oct 4 2026)

`docs/rounds/2026-10-04-type-and-depth.md` is the round (AJ's answers "1a 2a 3b"); the plan it built, with the kits, the maps and each phase's working files, is `harness/depth/` on AJ's PC.

- **A `var(--x)` that nothing declares draws nothing, and nothing warns.** A `box-shadow` that reads one undefined name is invalid as a whole, so the box goes flat while the console, the typecheck and the build stay quiet. The plan's review found three such names a later phase would have read (`--psub-elev-1`, `--jg-elev-2`, `--wk-elev-3`). `src/css-vars-declared.test.ts` fails on any `var(--x)` without a fallback, in the stylesheets the round has touched, that nothing in `src` declares (a `--x:` in a stylesheet, a `"--x"` set from a script, or Tailwind's own theme, which `index.css` imports). Add each stylesheet a phase touches to its `FILES`; never add a fallback just to quiet it.
- **Depth is tokens, carried by every palette copy as the same strings.** `index.css` holds the depth scale (`--elev-0` to `--elev-5`, `--elev-4-up`, `--elev-card`, `--shelf`, `--frame-down` / `--frame-up`, `--press`, `--glow-live` / `--glow-go`, `--solid-light`, `--go-light`, `--panel-highlight`) and the fills and lines (`--raised`, `--well`, `--tray`, `--edge`, `--edge-control`, `--divider`, `--highlight`, `--scrim`). Every feature palette carries them under its own prefix in every block (light, dark, the fallback): each shadow as the app's token of the same name (`--eq-elev-2: var(--elev-2)`), each colour as the same literal. Never write a copy's depth token as `var(--eq-…)` (the copy tests rename or compare strings) or as a new value. `equipment-tokens.test.ts` holds the names (a shadow token must alias the app's of the same name, and nothing else may alias outside `--eq-*`); `palette-copies.test.ts`, `admin-tokens.test.ts`, `studio-tokens.test.ts` (`FROM_THE_APP`) and `learning-tokens.test.ts` hold the copies; `src/elevation.test.ts` holds the tokens: navy, never black (white only as a top light or the frame's hairline), a resting blur of 18px at most, lighter is higher in both modes, and a raised fill keeps the control's 3:1 edge (`--input`, `--eq-border-strong`, `--jg-control-edge`). Two exceptions, each held: the Pulse carries no `--sr-glow-go` / `--sr-go-light` (it keeps no orange button, and `palette-copies.test.ts` refuses a `--sr-go…` name), and the Catalog's `--cat-*` gets each key in the commit whose rule first reads it (`learning-tokens.test.ts` refuses an unread `--cat-*`).
- **A well is `--well` (the Hub's `--eq-surface-2`), never `bg-muted` or `--bg-dark-3`.** Both go LIGHTER than the card in dark (`#203549` on `#14293D`), so a "well" drawn with either rises instead of sinking. `elevation.test.ts` measures it.
- **A shadow in a className is `shadow-(--token)`, never `shadow-[var(--…),…]`.** tailwind-merge files an arbitrary shadow that starts with `var()` as a shadow COLOUR, so a caller's `shadow-none` or `shadow-md` does not replace it and a caller's `shadow-red-200` silently drops it; and Tailwind rewrites the last colour of an arbitrary shadow to `var(--tw-shadow-color, …)`. A shadow built from tokens is ONE token, read whole: `--raised-lift` (a raised control: `--elev-1` and the top light), `--solid-lift` (solid blue) and `--go-lift` (an orange that is not Start session) in `index.css`, written `shadow-(--raised-lift)` with no `/opacity` modifier (that form breaks, as the Navy Frame trap above says). `components/ui/button-sizes.test.ts` refuses `shadow-[var(` in any `.tsx` and proves a caller's `shadow-none` still wins.
- **The shared Button's default variant carries the BLUE drop** (phase 4). A caller that repaints it says its own shadow, or a red, amber or orange button gets a blue glow under it: `shadow-(--go-lift)` on orange, `shadow-(--elev-1)` on any other colour, `shadow-none` on the progress report's navy band. The outline variant is raised on `--input` (3:1, AJ's answer 2A); a caller never swaps that edge for the decorative `border-border` or `border-slate-200`.
- **Nothing tappable under 40px, the shared primitives included** (phase 4). Every Button size is 40 (lg and icon-lg 44), and so are an Input, a select's trigger, a menu's or a select's item and a tab; the frame's icon buttons are 40 at every width (they were 36 on a phone). `button-sizes.test.ts` reads the class strings (jsdom loads no Tailwind, so nothing can be measured) and scans every `<Button>` for a caller that shrinks it (`h-6`…`h-9`, `size-6`…`size-9`, `h-[<40px]`) or brings back `transition-all`. Moved on purpose with it: `page-grounds.test.ts`, whose open tab is now `--raised` on `--tray` (the profile's tray took `bg-(--tray)` in the same commit, slate-200's own value in light and below the page in dark), the floors unchanged at 1.1:1 and 1.25:1.
- **A shadow shows only on what is stacked under it** (phase 5: the frame and the shelves). The header casts `--frame-down` at z-20 and the bottom bar `--frame-up` at z-30 (both kept: the plan's 12 would put the frame under anything a room pins between 13 and 29, and the cast would vanish there); each room's bar over scrolling content casts its family's `--X-shelf` at 9 to 11 (the Hub's `.hd-top` 10, `.msh` 10, Relay's sub-bar 9, `.wk__mast` 11 over `.wk__bar` 10, `.ops-tabs` 10); the Hub's names row `.hs-head` casts at 8. Two traps: a later sibling at the SAME number paints over the earlier one's cast, so a masthead sits one above its own sub-row; and two bars that read as one band cast once, from the band's foot (the Hub's command bar and its day line through the `.hd-top` wrapper; My Studio's header and Relay's sub-bar through `.msh:has(+ .msh-sub:not(:empty))`), or a shadow splits the band. A positioned bar with a z-index is a stacking context, so its popovers can rise no higher than it: check them before moving a bar's number. The Active Session's grid keeps its own documented order (journey-grid.css's "Layers"); `src/frame-and-shelves.test.ts` holds all of it, the session's numbers included.
- **The frame's edge is a cast, and two frame tests moved on purpose** (phase 5). `AppHeader.render.test.tsx` and `frame-colours.test.ts` pinned the white hairline `border-chrome-line` on the header and the two trainer bars; they now pin `shadow-(--frame-down)` / `shadow-(--frame-up)` and no hairline, and the Operations bar's orange `border-chrome-go/30` stays, asserted (it says which mode you are in). The bottom bar's labels are Geist 11/12 at 600 (700 for the tab you're on) in ordinary capitalisation, and WRAP instead of truncating (`names-wrap.test.ts` now holds NavButton's label: the session's tab names the client). With the capitals gone, how a label is written shows: `liveSessionTabLabel` says "Start session" (`live-session.test.ts` moved), and "Active Session" stays the screen's name. Your initials are the display face upright at 800 (the slant is the studio's name and Go's alone), 40px on a phone.
- **`inset-shadow-(--token)` writes "inset" in front of the token** (phase 7). Tailwind 4 compiles it to `--tw-inset-shadow: inset var(--token)`, so a token that already starts with `inset` (the dark `--panel-highlight`) becomes `inset inset …`, the whole `box-shadow` is invalid, and the box goes flat in dark only, with no warning. A shadow built from tokens is one token read with `shadow-(--token)`: `--panel-lift` (`--elev-2` and the top light) joined the lifts in `index.css` for a panel drawn in a className (the profile header's card). `button-sizes.test.ts` refuses `inset-shadow-(` in any `.tsx`.
- **The profile's top has a height budget, and it is held by arithmetic** (phase 7; AJ, Oct 2 2026: "the top of the profiles just feel so bulky"). The header became a card, the facts one well, the tools one raised control, and nothing on it may make it taller: its padding and margin are 28px (the band's 29), and the facts 63.5px with a booking (the outlined strip's 64.75), because the count's line-height is 0.8 (a 22px figure in a 14px line would otherwise add 2px) and the icons are `flex` boxes (an inline 16px svg made its line 20px). The commit for phase 7 sets out the sums at 744 and 820 portrait, 1133 and 1180 landscape and the one-band 1366. Change a size, a padding or a line-height in `ProfileHeader.tsx` and redo them: in landscape the Journey grid's machine rows depend on it. The tests that moved on purpose: `next-session-tile.test.ts` (the day and its dot, and the time and its AM / PM, are joined by no-break spaces, so "Tomorrow ·" over "7:30 AM" is the only break, U+202F before AM included), `profile-nav-css.test.ts` (the base meta was 9.5px uppercase; it is 12/500 in its own capitalisation, and the bar is raised), `loud-orange.test.ts` (the count's class list is Saira 22/800; still the hero's text orange, measured on the well too), `palette-copies.test.ts` (`--psub-panel-highlight` and `--psub-ink-2` join the shell's aliases; an idle page's words are measured on the raised bar) and `page-grounds.test.ts` (the profile's tabs are the shared trigger's ink-d2, no slate, no display face, no truncate).
- **A raised button keeps its 3:1 edge, presses with a transform, and lies flat when it can't be pressed** (phase 8: buttons and Go across the rooms; AJ's 2A). Every room's secondary button is `--X-raised`, `box-shadow: var(--X-elev-1), inset 0 1px 0 var(--X-highlight)`, radius 12, on the `--X-border-strong` it had (or took, where it drew the decorative `--X-border`), with `:not(:disabled):active { transform: translateY(1px); box-shadow: var(--X-press) }` and `:disabled { box-shadow: none }`; a quiet or ghost variant says `box-shadow: none`, a solid blue one `var(--X-glow-live), var(--X-solid-light)`, a tint its lift without the top light. Six traps it found. (1) **Moving a generic `.x:hover { background }` into `@media (hover: hover)` must leave it where it was in the file**: a variant's restated fill (`.x--hero, .x--hero:hover`) has the same weight and wins only by coming later, so a block moved to the file's end paints the hero grey under a pointer. (2) **A generic hover that sets a background flattens a solid variant unless the variant restates its fill**: Notes' `.nt-btn--solid` turned grey under a pointer until this phase. (3) **`--X-border-strong` is not always 3:1**: the progress report's is a card's hairline (`#cbd5e1`, 1.4:1 on the raised fill), so its outline button draws the app's `--eq-border-strong`. (4) **A press that must beat a picked state needs the weight**: `.x:active` and `.x[aria-pressed="true"]` weigh the same, so the press is written `:not(:disabled):active` (or comes later). (5) **A dashed control is an empty place to add something** (No set?, Link a client, New folder, the Notes door): it stays flat and only presses. (6) **`loud-orange.test.ts` pins the Wrap-up's renewal-due class string literally**, so its lift sits in a second `${…}`, not inside that string. Go is Start session's voice alone (the display face's slanted capitals at 800 and 17, 0.04em, `--X-glow-go` and `--X-go-light`): the profile header and its In progress, the peek's and the run sheet's primary, the Directory's Start, the briefing's `.br__cta` and the Deep Dive's Generate; every other orange button (`.adm-btn--hero`, `.eq-btn--hero`, `.fit-btn--hero`, `.rt-btn--hero`) takes the depth in the 14/700 button voice. The Catalog's `--cat-*` gained `raised`, `highlight`, `elev-1`, `press`, `glow-live` and `solid-light` with `.cat__btn`, their first reader. `src/buttons-depth.test.ts` holds all of it, measures each palette's edge (3:1) and words (4.5:1) on its raised fill in both modes, and scans this phase's stylesheets for a button under 40px (it found the routine builder's row tools at 36 and its switch at about 30, now 40). No existing assertion moved; `ProfileHeader.render.test.tsx` and `WrapUpScreen.render.test.tsx` gained theirs.
- **A box inside a panel is a well, a line in a list is a row, and a control that sinks keeps its 3:1 edge** (phase 9: wells and rows across the rooms). A well is the family's well tone (`--X-surface-2`), its inner shadow (`--X-elev-0`), no edge (`border: 0`, or `1px solid transparent` where a state colours the edge: Openings' times, the briefing's routines, the floor tiles) and radius 12; dashes stay only where they mean "nothing here" (an Openings time nobody is in) or "not this client's own record" ("Clients built like them"). Two traps came out of it. The session palette's `--jg-surface-2` (and so `--cr-surface-2`, the Deep Dive's) is the grid's header band and goes LIGHTER than the card in dark (`#1b3044` on `#14293d`), so the Deep Dive's empty well reads `--cr-well` (the app's `--eq-surface-2`) instead: a well drawn in the grid's surface-2 rises in dark. And a 4px coloured left border on a rounded box tapers into a crescent, so the briefing's carried regions paint their urgency as a 4px background band (`--br-carried-band` over `--br-carried-fill`) with no border at all. "Write a note…" kept its 3:1 edge (solid now) and sank like a field: a well never takes an edge away from a control. Settings' reports and doors, and the notes to review, are rows divided by `--X-divider` with no box; their hovers, and every new hover that changes a fill or an edge, sit inside `@media (hover: hover)`. On the page ground (the Archive's figures, Programming's counts, Openings' times, the clinical strip) the well tone is only 1.05:1 off the page and a hair LIGHTER, so those wells read as soft pads; if the iPad walk finds them too faint, the page's sunk tone is the tray (`--X-tray`), and the muted ink must then move to ink-2 (it reads 4.4:1 on the tray in light). `src/wells-and-rows.test.ts` holds all of it and measures every well's words in both modes; `palette-copies.test.ts` holds the five new `--psub-*` aliases (`--psub-elev-0`, `--psub-raised`, `--psub-highlight`, `--psub-press`, `--psub-border-strong`).
- **A panel is an edge seen from outside and a short lift; its head has no band and no rule; a coloured rule is a background band** (phase 10: panels and heads across the rooms). A panel draws `border: 1px solid var(--X-edge)` with `background-clip: padding-box` (or, where a band is painted, its fill's layer says `padding-box`) and `box-shadow: var(--X-elev-2), var(--X-panel-highlight)`; the hero takes `--X-elev-3`, a booking on the Next 30 minutes strip the Hub card's `--X-elev-card`, and what is done, off or not a client lies flat. Its head is `background: transparent; border-bottom: 0`, the title in the panel-title voice (17/700 in ink, -0.01em, its own capitalisation) with a leading icon in a 32px tinted square, and the body 12px under it; a head over a list speaks the label voice (14/700 in ink-2). Five traps came out of it. (1) **A head with no bottom padding needs a foot where it is all the card shows**: a folded panel (`.adm-ov__panel--folded`) and a disclosure head (`.eq-card__head--button`) keep their own. (2) **A child's background paints over its parent's band**: a 3px band on a card whose first child has its own fill (Learning's tiles, the infobox) is hidden under that child, so the band is the HEAD's first background layer, not the card's. (3) **An inset shadow on a rounded box is a crescent too** (`inset 4px 0 0` follows the corner's curve): the briefing's safety band, the Journal's open card and the Floor Map's flagged tile are straight background bands now. (4) **A scroller clips its children's lift to its padding box**: the Next 30 minutes row scrolls sideways, so its bookings' lift lives in the row's own padding (`4px 12px 8px 0`) and the strip lost its own vertical padding to stay 60px. (5) **`box-shadow: var(--x-color)` is not a shadow**: `.sh__strip` read `--st-shadow`, a colour with no lengths, so the declaration was invalid and the strip drew nothing. Tests that moved on purpose: `my-studio/look.test.ts` (the eleven heads of Sep 27 2026, 12px/800/0.12em capitals, now the panel-title voice for a card's head and the label voice for a head over a list, decided class by class; Team's four header-strip cards `.tm-card`, `.tc`, `.stm__panel`, `.op-person` now hold the edge, the padding-box clip, `--st-elev-2` and a transparent head with no rule; `overflow: hidden` stays, which never clips the card's own lift). New: `src/panels-and-heads.test.ts` (the recipe on 47 panels in seven palettes, the heads, the two voices measured at 4.5:1 light and dark, the icon squares, the fifteen bands, the presses, no shadow transition and no raw black in this phase's stylesheets; checked by breaking a panel, a head and a band on purpose). Not converted, and on phase 14's list: 20 other rules that draw a 3px to 5px left border on a rounded box (callouts in the briefing, the Pulse, Learning, Notes, FORD, the clinical flags, Operations' Limbo, the Calendar's blocks, machine fit, machine-db, the Deep Dive's caveat, Relay's summary, the Journal's shared message and studio-hub), the session's legend swatch (phase 13's file), and the state rails the plan keeps on purpose (`.hs-card`, `.hn-item`, `.ph-card`).
- **On the Hub a booking lifts only while it is a client's and not over; the command bar sinks inside its 3:1 edge; the peek is a popover** (phase 11: the Hub). `.hs-card` draws `1px solid var(--eq-edge)` with `background-clip: padding-box` and keeps its 4px state rail; the lift (`var(--eq-elev-card), var(--eq-panel-highlight)`) is SCOPED to `.hs-card[data-kind="client"][data-recede="false"]` rather than put on the base and taken back from five states, and an open card's ring and `--eq-elev-3` sit on `.hs-card[data-kind="client"][data-recede="false"][data-open="true"]` (0,4,0, over the lift's 0,3,0), which sets only the shadow so a rail keeps its colour. A card that is over still drops its ring when open, as before. Three traps came out of it. (1) **The `background` shorthand resets `background-clip`**: a state rule that repaints a padding-box card's fill (in session, over, unlinked, pending, unknown, staff, and the Next 30 minutes strip's in-session item) restates `background-clip: padding-box`, or the soft edge sits over the card's own fill instead of the grid; `hub-depth.test.ts` checks every such rule. (2) **A switch that clips its corners clips the lift of what it raises**: `layer-switch.css` gives `.hl-switch` `overflow: hidden`, so in the bar `.hd-bar .hl-switch` says `overflow: visible`, or the raised layer chip loses its shadow at the bottom. (3) **A rule keyed on the soft edge fades a dash to nothing**: an unlinked booking keeps today's hairline dashes (`--eq-border` on top, right and bottom), as the strip's unlinked item does. Moved on purpose: `hub-colour-rules.test.ts` "raises the layer you're on as a chip" now reads `background: var(--eq-raised)` and a shadow containing `var(--eq-elev-1)` (it was `var(--eq-surface)` and `var(--eq-shadow)`); today-and-picked's `0 3px 0 var(--eq-hero)` and your column head's `inset 0 -3px 0 var(--eq-live)` are unchanged. The peek reads three new palette keys, `--eq-popover` (the card in light, index.css's `#22374b` in dark), `--eq-popover-rim` (`--eq-edge` in light, white at .055 in dark) and `--eq-popover-light` (none in light, white at .07 in dark).
- **A title is the display face upright, in its own capitalisation; the slant is the studio's name and Go's alone; Geist never slants for a heading** (phase 12: headings, fake italics and numbers; AJ's 1A). Page titles, a room's title in its bar, names, headline figures and codes are Saira Condensed with `font-style: normal` and `text-transform: none` (Learning's and the wiki's titles, the Catalog's region, My Studio's section name, Relay's sheet titles, the routine builder's names, the briefing's client and routine, the calendar's and History's title, a machine's name on its panel, the Deep Dive's name, the Story's year, FORD's day, a package's months, a year and a routine letter in History, a routine's badge); section titles are Geist 22/800 (`.lh__h2`, the report's `.pr-card__title`, the session pop-up's day), panel titles Geist 17/700 (the Deep Dive's panels, the Pulse's head and its dialog); a machine's code is a jersey tag (`.wk__row-code`: Saira 14/700, ringed inside in its own ink at 22%). Every `DialogTitle` that set its own type (italic, capitals, the display face, 18 to 24px) lost the override, so the shared DialogTitle's 17/700 speaks. The slant stays only on the studio's name, the front door's display line and Go (Start session in the profile header and its In progress, the peek, the run sheet, the Directory, the briefing, and the Deep Dive's Generate); the briefing's safety heading keeps its capitals, upright. Four traps. (1) **Saira Condensed as vendored has no `tnum`** (its GSUB holds ccmp, dnom, frac, liga and locl) **and proportional digits** (at 800 a 1 is 319 units wide, an 8 489), so `tabular-nums` does nothing in it: Saira sets a number that stands alone (a count, a year, a day), and a number that changes in place or lines up in a column of changing figures (a clock, a set's reps) stays in Geist. The Hub's hour labels (phase 11) are Saira as the kit renders them; they are right-aligned one to a row, so nothing jitters. (2) **`text-transform: none` shows the words as they are written**: a title typed in capitals in the source (the session's intro banner until it went on Oct 8 2026, LegacyChartImporter's table head) stays in capitals, and `Machine Progression` keeps its capital P; change the words, not the rule. (3) **A child that inherits the display face and slants itself is read as Geist** by the guard, which reads a rule at a time like `fonts.test.ts`: put the face in the rule that slants, or keep it off the slanted list. (4) **A quiet line may slant, a heading may not**: an empty place, a quoted note, a placeholder and the information mark (`.cd-why-mark`, now 700) are on the quiet list, and a quiet line is never in capitals or over 700. Moved on purpose: `learning-scale.test.ts` (the six titles pinned as italic capitals: five are the display face upright now, and `.lh__h2` is Geist 22/800; it also holds the masthead at 22, the jersey tag and Learning's facts at Saira 22), `my-studio/look.test.ts` (`.msh__sect-name` `font-style: normal`, `text-transform: none`; "slants capitals only in the display face" unchanged) and `WrapUpScreen.render.test.tsx` (the h1 is `font-display font-extrabold text-[30px]` without `italic` or `uppercase`). New: `src/type-voice.test.ts` (the display face slanted and in capitals only on its allow-lists, the Active Session's eleven caps labels on it for phase 13; no faked italics, with the 38 quiet rules and seven files' quiet class lists listed exactly; 35 titles, names, figures and codes upright in the display face; 15 Geist heads; no dialog or sheet title asking for a slant, capitals or the display face; checked by breaking each on purpose), and `names-wrap.test.ts` holds `.br__name`, `.eq-detail__name` and `.tp-identity__name`. The Active Session's dialogs lost their slant here; their capitals are phase 13's.
- **The Active Session speaks the app's voice: nothing under 11px, nothing in capitals; its controls are raised on the edges AJ said yes to, and its layers are untouched** (phase 13: the Active Session; AJ's 3B). In `journey-grid.css` the 55 declarations under 11px (7.5 to 10.5px: 53 font sizes and the reps token twice) are 11 or 12 (the reps line `--jg-r-size` is 11 at every density) and the 30 capital-letter rules are in their own capitalisation, each measured against the row that holds it first (the numbers are in the rules' comments; every one fitted). Names, days and column heads stay the display face, upright (the client on the bar, the machine on the Now Bar and on Next, a session's day, the corner, the Analytics head); the labels that were its capitals are Geist (the Today and Latest tags, the section labels, the Older rail). The steppers, the quality buttons, Reps | Sec and Notes and Pulse are `--jg-raised` with `--jg-elev-1` and a top light, and press (`translateY(1px)` and `--jg-press`); No set? stays dashed and unfilled and only presses; Finish takes Go's glow and press in the 14/700 button voice, never Go's slanted capitals; Next and Done drop the solid blue's tinted shadow (`--jg-glow-live`, `--jg-solid-light`, new in all three token blocks). The grid lifts as one panel (`--jg-elev-2`) and the Now Bar docks over it (`--jg-dock`, or `--jg-dock-side` leftward as the landscape column). The menus, the Key and the order sheet lift as popovers in the navy (`--jg-elev-4`; three were raw black). Six traps. (1) **A shadow shows only if its element paints after what it falls on**: the grid is `position: relative`, so the Now Bar is positioned too (`position: relative`, no z-index) to paint after it in tree order; without that its upward dock lay under the grid. No z-index was added anywhere, and the grid's own layers sit inside `.jg-grid`'s isolation. (2) **A per-cell sticky head cannot take the shelf**: `--shelf`'s -10px spread on an 84px date head casts a row of separate blobs, so the session's heads keep their pinned separators and no shelf. (3) **Preflight draws `<small>` at 80%**: the session bar's "3 of 6" said its "of 6" at 8.8px; `.jg-sbar__progress-text small` now says 12px itself. (4) **A running clock in the display face changes width every second**: Saira has no tabular figures, so `.jg-clock__time` is Geist 17/800 `tabular-nums` (phase 12's rule for a number that changes in place). (5) **A word given its own colour loses the state colour it inherited**: a skip's reason moved to the muted ink, so the focused Today cell restates `--jg-live-on` for `.jg-today__skip-why` beside `.jg-today__skip`. (6) **A one-word reason written in lower case** (`set-outcome.ts` `SKIP_REASON_SHORT`, "pain", read in the Now Bar's "Skipped · pain") takes `text-transform: capitalize` in a cell, the one transform the session keeps. Small things beside them: the settings line under a machine's name wraps instead of being clipped now that its keys are 11px; the legend swatch rounds its right corners only (a 3px edge on a rounded corner is a crescent), and the round Blood flow bubble lost its 3px edge; the clock's pause (32px) and the Today column's add (28px) reach 40 through an `::after`; Do next and the menu's note are 40px; every background hover is inside `@media (hover: hover)` but Finish's, which restates its fill; `.jg-cell` fades its opacity only; the words that read in the faint ink (3.2:1) moved to the muted one (the Now Bar's Started line, a section's count, the corner's count, the key's glosses, "‹ older", the settings boxes on the Journey look). The session's closing dialogs, its Pulse slide-over, the flags sheet and the session notes sheet speak the same voice: a 22/800 title or a 17/700 sheet head as written, labels 14/700 in the second ink, the buttons' own 14/700 with no decorative slate edge, the note field's own 3:1 edge, and the shared dialog's lift in dark too; the intro banner said "New client introductory session: conversational baseline" as written (it went with the intro-session path, which nothing set, on Oct 8 2026). A phone's count label is 12px as written. Moved on purpose: `type-voice.test.ts` (the eleven `journey-grid.css` entries on the display-face capitals allow-list are gone, and six of the session's names and heads joined the upright list), `loud-orange.test.ts` (the intro banner's slice ends at its words, which are written as said now; its assertions are unchanged), `elevation.test.ts` (the session's shadows-by-name and its token list gain `--jg-glow-live`, `--jg-solid-light` and `--jg-dock-side`) and `css-vars-declared.test.ts` (`journey-grid.css` joins its files). `contrast.test.ts` and `session-colour-rules.test.ts` did not move. New: `src/features/journey-grid/session-depth.test.ts` (the whole stylesheet under 11px and in capitals, the Geist labels, the raised controls and their presses, Finish, Next and Done, the lift and the dock with no new z-index, no black or animated shadow, the pointer-only hovers, the 40px reaches, the words at 4.5:1 on each ground in both modes, and the dialogs' and sheets' class lists; checked by breaking it sixteen ways on purpose).
- **The depth rules hold across every stylesheet and component, not a list; Operations' buttons speak the button voice** (phase 14: the guards and Operations). `src/elevation.test.ts`'s rules half reads all of `src`: the most-seen panels read their family's lift; no shadow or shadow token is raw black; no transition lists `box-shadow` or `all` (or names no property, which is all); no class list pairs `transition-all`, `transition-shadow` or a list naming either with a shadow, nor a plain `transition` with a shadow that changes on a state; no side border wider than the rest on a rounded corner (the Hub's rails, the strip's, the phone card's, the Key's swatches and the calendar's block stay, listed with their reasons and exact both ways); no button rule under 40px (three listed exceptions); every raised control (a `btn`, `seg` or `filter` rule with an elev-1 lift) on a 3:1 control token, a solid fill, a tint whose own ink measures 3:1 on its card in both modes, or a picked segment in a well; and no well in `bg-muted` or `--bg-dark-3`. `src/type-voice.test.ts` holds the plan's six panel heads at 17/700 and `.adm-btn` at 14/700 as written (it was 12px/800 capitals at 0.06em, one rule for 260 `AdminButton`s; `size="sm"` is the padding only now). Seven traps. (1) **A class list split across a `cn()` call or a multi-line template hides its pairs from a literal-at-a-time scan**: eleven of the fourteen `transition-all`-with-a-shadow lists the guard found were split that way; it reads a `className={...}`, `cn()`, `cva()` or `clsx()` whole. (2) **A crescent needs a rounded corner on the bar's side**: a 3px left bar on `border-radius: 0 10px 10px 0` is square where it meets the bar and tapers nowhere (Relay's summary, the Journal's shared message, a worked entry, the Pulse's help line, a Learning page's quoted line), so the guard reads the four corners, the longhands included, not just "has a radius". (3) **A static scan sees one rule's selector, not the classes an element carries together**: the Deep Dive's `.cr-rhythm` drew a 4px left bar and its rounded corners came from `.cr-card` on the same element; it was fixed by hand, and its comment says why. (4) **A state that changes a banded box's fill says `background-color`, never the `background` shorthand, which wipes the band**: each band's colour is a custom property its states set (`--cfl-band`, `--fit-band`, `--rb-row-band`, `--sr-anchor-band`, `--cr-rhythm-band`), and the Pulse's print rule whites a card with `background-color: #fff !important` so its bands print. (5) **A tint's soft line is under 3:1**: the codex's blue tint drew `--cx-live-line` (45% of the live blue, 2.3:1 on the card in light), the Catalog's Couldn't save a 32% pink in dark and its Saved no edge; a tint that lifts draws its own ink. When a token loses its last reader in a family that refuses unread tokens (`--cat-warn-border`, `learning-tokens.test.ts`), it goes with it. (6) **A `%` in a Python or printf template doubles**: one band was written `100%%`, which is invalid CSS and draws nothing; the band list in the guard (11b) caught it before the commit, which is why each new band is pinned by its width. (7) **Tailwind reads a test file's words for class names**: `button-sizes.test.ts` spelt a made-up class (`shadow-` + `[var(...)]`) in a comment and a test title, and the CSS optimiser warned twice at every build from phase 4 until the two strings were reworded. Moved on purpose: `client-codex/kit/depth.test.ts` (the blue tint's edge is `var(--cx-live)`, it pinned `var(--cx-live-line)`), `css-vars-declared.test.ts` (reads `clinical-flags.css` and `client-notes/notes.css` too) and one describe title in `button-sizes.test.ts` (its assertions unchanged). Checked by breaking the code eleven ways on purpose; each failed.
- **A well on the page ground goes in the tray; a chip or toggle a trainer taps draws the firm edge; slanted capitals are four places** (AJ's three answers, Oct 5 2026, "1a 2a 3a"). Six traps. (1) **A shared well class takes the tray only where it is placed on the page**: `.adm-empty`, `.cx-empty`, `.cr-empty` and `.rk-empty` are the well tone in a card and the tray as a direct child of the page's own column (`.adm-screen >`, `.ops-page >`, `.adm-pipeline >`, `.adm-fit__detail >`, `.st-page >`, `.cr >`, `.cr-section >`, `.st__scroll >`, `.tm-people >`); wrap one in a div and it goes back to a pale pad, and no test sees the DOM. (2) **Coloured words fall under 4.5:1 on the tray in light** (plum 4.03, orange 4.15, the muted ink 4.42): words take ink-2, and a colour moves to a band (rule 3) or a chip on its own fill. (3) **A band painted on a box's border-box is crossed by the box's own edge**: once that edge is the firm grey, a band along it shows a grey line across its first pixel, so the band's colour is that edge too (`border-top-color: var(--ops-stop-band)`, the flag toggle's `border-left-color: var(--cfl-band)` on a tone only, with `--cfl-band: transparent` by default so a flag with no tone draws no grey band). (4) **The page tone set into a card is below 3:1 for the firm edge** (`#7A8694` on `#DEE6EE` is 2.94:1): the SMART toggle's edge is measured against the card it stands on. Two more, from the review of the answers: (5) **A stylesheet sweep cannot see a chip drawn in a class list**: Times with room's Whose times, Post and Submit an initiative's chips and the Wrap-up's next weight sat on `border-div-d` in a `className`, so `firm-chips.test.ts` section 6 reads every tappable element's class list too (a JSX tag scanned with its braces, not to the first `>`), and a class list kept in a variable (the next weight's `btn`) is pinned by name. (6) **An informational class reused as a button inherits its hairline and its size**: the Kaizen roster's reasons were `.tp-chip` (a 24px tag) with an inline 34px height and pointer; a chip a trainer taps gets its own modifier (`.tp-chip--pick`), and the tag stays a tag. Moved on purpose: `wells-and-rows.test.ts` (nine wells measured on the tray and below the page, eleven since the review with Too new to judge and the routine builder's empty list, whose palette it now reads, not the well tone on a card; Programming's "not set up" measured on its chip), `buttons-depth.test.ts` (`.cr-generate` from Go's list to "every other orange button: Go's depth in the button voice"; the Journal's views pinned on the firm edge, they pinned the soft one), `type-voice.test.ts` (`.cr-generate` off the display face's slant allow-list). New: `src/firm-chips.test.ts`.
- **The sheets a session opens speak the session's voice too, and a raised control on a popover steps up again in dark** (the review's fixes, Oct 5 2026). 3B had reached the grid but not what the session opens: the machine sheet (`equipment.css`), the Pulse slide-over and its quick log (`.sra-*`, `.pq*`), the note composer (`.nc-btn`, `.nc-kicker`), the briefing (`.br__label` and the rest), the notes sidebar's cards (`JournalEntryCard`), the Critical strip, the stale-session and pick-a-client dialogs, a toast and the leave question. `src/session-sheets-type.test.ts` holds them: no text under 11px and no capitals in those stylesheets (only Go; the briefing's safety voice too until the follow-up below), none in those components, and the navy scrim under a slide-over. **In dark the popover (#22374B) is LIGHTER than the page's raised fill (#203549)**, so an outline Button in a dialog, sheet or menu read sunk while its lift said raised: inside those surfaces (`[data-slot=dialog-content]`, sheet, menu, select) `--raised` and `--input` become `--popover-raised` #283E54 and `--popover-input` #7D92A6 (`index.css`), and the Hub's peek does the same through `--eq-popover-raised` / `--eq-popover-edge` (also in `admin.tokens.css`, which mirrors every `--eq-` key); light is unchanged. Go's slanted capitals in the peek belong to Start session alone (`data-go`); its other primaries (Open session, Resume or start new, Log past session, Edit session) keep Go's depth in the 14/700 voice. Tests that moved on purpose: `WrapUpScreen.render.test.tsx` (a card's head is the 14/700 label, no longer 12px capitals), `CalendarView.render.test.tsx` (the view segments say Month, Week, Day as words; the capitals hid lower-case source), `CreateClientModal.render.test.tsx` and `BriefingScreen.render.test.tsx` (labels in sentence case), `ProfileHeader.render.test.tsx` (the tools 12 under 768px; In progress in navy words), `frame-colours.test.ts` (the avatar menu w-60, no `dark:shadow-none`), `loud-orange.test.ts` (Resume's row went with the timer's dead card variant; two pins are token sets now, `someStringHas`), `session-depth.test.ts` (the Today add's tap area never leaves its row; the quality marks in the muted ink). New guards: `fonts.test.ts` refuses a stylesheet `font-weight` of 900 (Geist is variable, so 900 drew heavier than every class list; 136 rules were calmed to 800), `css-vars-declared.test.ts` reads every stylesheet in `src`, and `elevation.test.ts`'s 40px scan names tabs and back buttons too (it missed `.hsd-tab` at 32px and `.eq-back` at 36px).
- **The follow-up (Oct 5 2026): the last capitals on the briefing and Learning, and the dialogs the review left, moved on purpose.** AJ's 1A reaches the briefing's safety block: `.br-safe__clear` ("Nothing flagged — clear to go.") and `.br-safe__title` (a limit's name) keep the display face at 22/800 in their colour and drop their capitals, so `type-voice.test.ts`'s `DISPLAY_CAPS` is the slanted list alone and `session-sheets-type.test.ts`'s `CAPITALS` is Go alone (both were exact both ways, so both moved). `rating.css` (the Dial and Loudness: "Not asked", the legend, Note · Heads up · Critical) joined `session-sheets-type.test.ts`'s sheets. Learning's switch (`.wk__seg`, also the Catalog figure's Front / Back) is the run sheet's well with the picked segment raised: `elevation.test.ts`'s `SEGMENTS_IN_A_WELL` names it. Four literal pins in `loud-orange.test.ts` became token sets (`someStringHas`): the feedback kinds' picked blue, the routine slot's, Log a conversation's chip (each gained `shadow-(--solid-lift)`) and Edit trainer's Home chip (12/700 as written). New guards: `type-voice.test.ts` section 9 reads the deferred dialogs' class lists (no capitals, no tracking, nothing under 11px, no `font-black`, no `dark:shadow-none`, no field painting its own ground or a decorative edge, a solid red button restating its fill) and section 10 Learning's switch; `buttons-depth.test.ts` holds their secondary buttons raised on `border-input` with the lift, or the outline variant. **A JSX scan must let `=>` through a tag**: `<Input onChange={(e) => …} className="…">` ends a `[^>]*` match at the arrow, so the field scan read `(?:=>|[^>])*?` (it missed an Input with an arrow before its class until a deliberate break caught it). `RenewalCardDialog` is mounted (ClientProfileView opens it), not dead as the review said.
- **The sweep (Oct 5 2026): one capitals style across all of `src`, held by a scan, and the tests that moved on purpose.** The finish round read every stylesheet and class list for the old voice and moved about 250 of them. `type-voice.test.ts` section 11 now fails on capitals outside `CAPITALS_ON_PURPOSE` (the eyebrow over a page title, each held at 12/700/0.08em; Go and the brand; a sentence's first letter; the progress report), on text under 11px outside `SMALL_ON_PURPOSE` (initials and a count in their dots, the report), on tracking at 0.06em or wider outside those capitals, and on any file's class lists in the old voice beyond `TSX_OLD_VOICE`'s counts. **A rule that drops its capitals shows its words as written**, so the words behind it must be in sentence case: the sweep re-cased what capitals had hidden (the routine builder's tags, a roster badge's raw value, "small sample", "note", "vs today's rate", Add Client, First-Time Setup). A raw lower-case value in a badge needs a word of its own. `elevation.test.ts` section 12 also scans by behaviour: any rule with `cursor: pointer` and a height under 40px is on `SMALL_TAP_ON_PURPOSE` with its reason (`min-height: 0`, a flex child let shrink, is not a size). Moved on purpose: `ConsultationSetupWizard.render.test.tsx` clicks "Skip setup" and "Start consult workout"; `loud-orange.test.ts` pins the wizard's `shadow-(--go-lift)` (it pinned a 30px arbitrary glow) and its picked chip's `shadow-(--solid-lift)` measured against the raised unpicked chip (it measured the ground); `TrendsPage.render.test.tsx` strips "Small sample"; `buttons-depth.test.ts` reads the briefing's palette.
- **The machine menu merged with type and depth (Oct 5 2026): the guards that named the code the menu retired now name the menu, none loosened.** The two rounds were built from the same master; type and depth restyled the machine sheet, its weight steppers and fields (`.eq-step__btn`, `.eq-step__input`, `.eq-field__input`, the composer, `.eq-btn--hero`) and the grid's ⋯ popover (`.jg-menu`, `.jg-machine__more`), all of which the menu deleted; the sheet's `.eq-btn` (and its `--live` and `--ghost`), the detail pane's head (`.eq-detail__head`, `.eq-back`, `.eq-detail__name` and the rest, `.eq-chip--idle`) and `.eq-composer__whose` went with it after the merge, with their guard rows (`buttons-depth.test.ts`' four `.eq-btn` rows, an `.eq-detail__name` row each in `type-voice.test.ts` and `names-wrap.test.ts`, the `.eq-back` check in `session-sheets-type.test.ts`); `buttons-depth.test.ts` still reads `equipment.css` for its hover and animation scans. Each exact-both-ways list dropped the retired entries (`type-voice.test.ts` six quiet lines, `elevation.test.ts` the ⋯'s 22px exception, `session-depth.test.ts` and `session-colour-rules.test.ts` the popover's rules and its z-index) and gained the menu where the guard's intent applies: `buttons-depth.test.ts` (`.mm-step` and seven more raised, four flat when disabled, `.mm-btn--live` and Add note's quiet blue solid, `.mm-save` and `.mm-add` in Go's depth and the 14/700 voice), `session-sheets-type.test.ts` (`machine-menu.css` is a sheet a session opens; its fields `.mm-field__input`, `.mm-upd__input` and `.mm-cmp` sink inside the 3:1 edge), `type-voice.test.ts` (`.mm-head__machine` and `.mm-head__num` upright in the display face; `.mm-h` and `.mm-chart__title` 17/700), `wells-and-rows.test.ts` (five menu wells). **A hover block in `machine-menu.css` goes last**: `look.test.ts` reads everything before the first `@media` as the base rules and expects that first `@media` to be the phone's, so a `@media (hover: hover)` placed mid-file would hide every rule after it from the menu's own guard. **A raised menu button that is also solid blue**: the hover block names `.mm-btn:not(.mm-btn--live):not(:disabled):hover`, because `.mm-btn:not(:disabled):hover` outranks `.mm-btn--live:hover` and would paint a blue button grey under a pointer.

<a id="server"></a>

## Mindbody, the server, scheduled jobs and sync

- **A booking in Firestore is never "Completed" or "No-Show".** The pull-sync (`lib/mindbody-api-sync.ts`) and the webhook write `Scheduled` or `Cancelled` and nothing else, and End Session writes the session, never the booking. Until Sep 24 2026 every screen that asked the booking alone was wrong in its own way: the Overview's Done and No-shows were always 0 and every finished booking was "never logged", Team showed every trainer's finished sessions as unlogged, the trainer page's "Upcoming" listed yesterday's clients, the attendance watch counted a no-show as a visit, and the Hub card went grey at its start time, which hid its flags from a trainer running late. The Hub draws the rule through `lib/hub-card-state.ts`, which adds one floor-only exception (a card never fades as done before its own start). **Read what happened to a booking through `lib/booking-state.ts` (`bookingState`)**: done when a Journey session was completed for that client (by `clientId`, never by name) on that studio day (AJ's rule); otherwise the clock. Hand it `loggedSessions(...)` from ONE bounded read scoped to the studio or the client, never a query per client — and `null` when that read failed, which makes a finished slot `unknown`, never never logged. The attendance watch adds one migration rule on top (`renewals/attendance.ts`): an unlogged past booking is still a visit before its studio's `journeyCutoverDate`, or with none set, because FileMaker holds that record. Mindbody's own marking stays manual (AJ: fine); pulling its status is a Mindbody-integration change and needs his OK.

- **Mindbody routes need a sign-in.** Browser code must call them with `authedFetch` (`src/lib/authed-fetch.ts`); a plain `fetch` gets a 401. A new `/api/mindbody/*` route inherits the check. One that should be admin-only goes in `ADMIN_ONLY_MINDBODY_PATHS` in `server.ts` — lowercase, with no trailing slash. The check refuses a `siteId` or `mindbodyClientId` that isn't a plain id.

- **An `/api` route declared in `server.ts` above the `/api/mindbody` gate answers anyone** (Sep 24 2026). The two Gemini OCR routes sat there from the AI Studio days: no sign-in, a 50 MB body, no limit, so anyone with the URL could spend the studio's Gemini quota. They now live in `server/gemini-routes.ts`, each with `requireStaff()` in front, a per-person limit (`src/lib/request-limit.ts`: two at once, sixty in fifteen minutes) and a 20 MB body read only AFTER the sign-in: `server.ts`'s shared body parser skips `/api/gemini/*` on purpose. Never declare an `/api/gemini` route in `server.ts` directly; `src/services/gemini-routes.test.ts` fails if one appears. The browser calls them with `authedFetch`, and the importer shrinks each photo before sending it (`src/services/read-chart-file.ts`); page count and body size are one answer in `src/services/chart-upload.ts`. `/api/log-error` is still open (a 1 MB body into the log).

- **`npm run dev` serves files to the whole network, and dist/ is public** (Sep 26 2026). `server.ts` listens on 0.0.0.0 so an iPad can test against the PC, and Vite's dev server hands out any readable file under the project folder unless told otherwise: `http://<the PC>:3000/service-account.json` returned the production admin key to anyone on the same Wi-Fi (Windows Firewall allows `node.exe` inbound on Public networks on AJ's PC). `server/served-files.ts` is the one answer: in development only `index.html`, `src/` and `node_modules/` are served by name, anything else only if the app imports it, and keys, logs, backups and archives never. Setting Vite's `server.fs.deny` REPLACES its defaults, and a pattern with a slash in it is matched against the whole absolute path, so `backups/**` matches nothing (write `**/backups/**`) and `**/.claude/**` would refuse everything in a worktree. Don't deny `firebase-applet-config.json`: `src/firebase.ts` imports it, and a denied import is a white screen. In production the server bundle is built to `build/server.cjs`, never `dist/` (until this round `/server.cjs` and its source map were public), and `serveBuiltApp` refuses any `.cjs`, `.mjs` or `.map` in `dist/` because `vite build` does not empty the folder. `src/services/served-files.test.ts` runs both servers against planted secrets.

- **The web service has no Firestore admin key.** Server code can't read or write Firestore as an admin; `server/auth.ts` reads with the caller's own token over REST — and only single documents, never a collection list: a list is refused when any one document in it is off-limits, which took the schedule sync down on Sep 13. The cron jobs do have the service account.

- Two Mindbody sites share one client-ID range (both start at 100000001), and on Sep 23 2026 43 numbers named a different person at each. Never look a Mindbody client up by `clients/{id}` alone: go through `src/lib/mindbody-site.ts` (`chooseClientDoc`), which sends the second person to `clients/{site}-{id}`. `scripts/check-mindbody-client-collisions.ts` finds the shared numbers; `scripts/check-collision-damage.ts` finds bookings on the wrong person. `docs/rounds/2026-09-23-client-identity.md`.

- `scripts/mindbody/register-webhook.js` now takes `--site` and `--list` and includes the contract and membership events, but neither site is subscribed to them yet. Deploy the Cloud Functions first.

- **`machineTrends/*` is aggregates only, built weekly** (`server/machine-trends-job.ts`, one 90-day range on `exerciseLogs.createdAt`). Never put a client row in it — it is readable by any signed-in trainer and clients are studio-scoped; "this client vs everyone" is `client.machineStats` against the distribution. Medians are `null` under `MIN_CLIENTS`; a screen says "not enough data yet". One screen reads it: the Settings card's height-based suggestions (`features/equipment/useMachineTrend.ts`, one read per machine per session). The Cloud Function `calculateFacilityAnalyticsV2` still reads every exercise log nightly — needs an OK to touch.

- **The schedule is three live days plus a fetched cache** (`useLiveSchedule`, `src/lib/schedule-window.ts`): the listener watches yesterday–tomorrow; anything else comes from `ensureRange()` (cached by id, per-day coverage, 15-minute freshness, the week ahead refreshed on a timer while visible). A screen that shows a day outside the live window asks for its range — the calendar does; don't reach for a wider listener. `schedules` is the merged list. **It no longer fetches clients.** *(The freshness is an hour since the cost plan, Sep 26 2026: `SCHEDULE_STALE_MS`, as the same trap says under Always on.)*

- **The app's clients are the studio roster** (`useStudioRoster`, `src/lib/studio-roster.ts`, hub sync fixes Sep 16): one listener on `clients where homeStudioId == activeStudioId`, plus booked visitors read by id. `AppContent`'s `clients` is that roster plus the selected client — the Hub, the directory and the auto-sync all read it. A studio switch empties it at once (`status` is `loading` until the listener answers — say "loading", never "Not synced", in that beat); a failed read never empties it. The directory trusts it for the current studio (`studioRosterReady`) and queries only for "Search entire corporate network". The listener's cost grows with every client whose `homeStudioId` is the studio — filter it to active clients before a historical import.

- **The schedule sync never reads a whole collection** (hub sync fixes, Sep 16). Phase 1 checks only the client ids the caller's roster lacks (`checkClientIds`: existing / missing / refused / unchecked) and never writes the full create payload over an existing client — that payload zeroes `sessionCount` and friends. The stale-booking sweep reads and cancels only rows inside the window Mindbody was asked about; anything outside it was simply not asked. A for-trainers corollary worth remembering: **the rules refuse a read of a client document that doesn't exist**, so "refused" means "missing, or someone else's", and a batch `in` query with one such id is refused whole.

- **The Render cron service is still named `journey-cron-leaderboards`** although it runs the machine-trends job — a renamed blueprint service is a new service to Render.

- <a id="jobs-per-studio"></a>**Jobs read per studio with select** (job memory, Oct 1 2026). Both cron jobs run on 512 MB "Starter" instances, and until Oct 1 both read the whole company into memory first: the weekly machine-trends job every client record (machine stats and InBody summary included) and every set of 90 days as a full document; the nightly renewals job every booking of four months, every workout of three and every client, all held to the end. Estimated on Sep 27 at about 0.54 MB per active client for the trends job and 0.40 MB for the renewals job (0.34 and 0.19 MB of live heap measured on a generated company on Oct 1, before Node's own ~60–90 MB and the garbage a run leaves between collections): they would have run out somewhere between roughly 430 and 1,100 active clients company-wide, and the four studios had about 700. The rules since:
  - **One studio at a time.** Read a studio's documents, use them, let them go before the next studio. The renewals job goes round twice (`server/renewals-job.ts`'s header: a first look that keeps only who is due a first sync and who could use a pull, the company-wide choice, then a second look that does everything else per studio); the client-states step takes one studio (`runJourneyStudio`); machine fit's company tier reads one studio's index and the clients its rows name, by id (`server/machine-fit-company.ts`).
  - **`select` the fields a step reads, and keep the list next to the code that reads them.** `LOG_FIELDS` in `server/machine-trends-job.ts` is every field of a set the trends, the outcome rule, the Demo rule and the performance watch read, and `weekly-job.test.ts` fails if a set cut down to it gives different trends or drops. A field a rule starts reading must go on its list, or that rule silently stops seeing it. Where a step reads most of a document (the renewal engine reads most of a client; Openings' fold most of a booking) the whole document is read, but still only one studio's.
  - **A company-wide sum is a running total, never the documents.** The trends, the performance watch and machine fit's company tier each have a streaming form (`createTrendsAccumulator`, `createPerformanceAccumulator`, `createCompanyAccumulator`) that takes one set or one studio at a time, and the old one-shot function is built on it. Each is held to a word-for-word copy of the old function in a `*-stream.test.ts`. The one read that must cover the whole company (the window's sets) is streamed with `eachDoc` (`server/job-memory.ts`, the Admin SDK's `stream()`), never `get()`.
  - **By client, on an index that exists.** A client's history is hers wherever it was booked, so the renewals job reads bookings and workouts thirty clients a query (`in`) on the existing (clientId, startTime), (clientId, date DESC) and (clientId, createdAt DESC) indexes, with the `orderBy` that matches each index, and puts each client's rows back in the order the old whole-company reads returned them in (by start time or day, then id). Production is Enterprise edition, which builds no index by itself: the old `schedules` and `sessions` range reads had no index starting with their field and scanned the whole collection every night. The weekly job's `exerciseLogs.createdAt` range read was still that shape until the speed round (Oct 5 2026, R24, AJ's OK): the composite `exerciseLogs (createdAt, machineId)` makes it a range read, with no code change.
  - **Say the headroom.** Each job logs the heap's peak after each studio and for the run (`memoryWatch`, "Solon: memory peak 41.2 MB heap used (process 120.3 MB now; the instance has 512 MB)."), so Render's log shows how close a run came. After the change the same generated company measured about 0.02 MB per active client company-wide for the trends job (its running totals) plus about 0.03 MB per client of the studio being read, and for the renewals job only the latter: `docs/business/running-costs.md`, "Cron memory".
  - **What they write did not change.** The new jobs were run beside a copy of the old ones on generated companies (two nights of renewals with first syncs, pulls, outcomes and All stars; the weekly job with machine fit and Openings; dry runs, budgets and `--only-studio`) and every write matched. Two things moved on purpose: a studio's outcomes, snapshots and client states are now written before the next studio's rather than every studio's outcomes before any snapshot, and a failed read of one studio's clients' sessions now leaves only that studio's All stars unwritten (it used to leave every studio's). The Cloud Function `recalcTrainerWindows` (256 MiB, about 0.19 MB a client) was not changed: functions deploy separately and need AJ's OK.

- **The standing week never writes to Mindbody, and never calls an unread day open** (voice-review round, Sep 27 2026 — `src/features/standing-week/README.md`). Journey checks the week's bookings against each trainer's AGREED week; the front desk books the regulars in Mindbody as recurring appointments. Don't add a hold, a booking or an availability write "to lock the slot": AJ ruled it out (holds cost money, can email a client, and clash with the front desk), and it would reopen ARCHITECTURE §1.7's fence. The check gives a state and no findings for a failed or unfinished read, and for a studio whose Mindbody isn't linked (`bookingsKnown`) — an empty list there means "can't tell", never "every slot is free". **A booking's trainer is an id OR a name:** the sync writes `trainerId` only when it matched the Mindbody staff member to a Journey trainer, and otherwise only the staff name (or "{studio} Rotation" when Mindbody named nobody), so match by id, then by name, and treat a nameless booking as nobody's (`check.ts`, WHOSE BOOKING). **A move needs proof:** a regular's other booking that week is "instead" only when Mindbody moved the booking, or a real rebook followed a stamped cancellation (`isRealRebook`, the client calendar's rule) — never merely because it exists. **The week is keyed by the Auth uid, the booking by the trainer document id:** `standingWeeks/{uid}` carries `trainerId` for the match; on older accounts the two differ (`team.ts` `uidOf` is `authUid || id`). Since the voice review follow-up (Sep 27 2026) a trainer's own write may not change `trainerId` once the week exists (a leader sets it from the roster), a trainer's days away (`away`) are never checked, and a "{studio} Rotation" booking at a regular's time is as usual — recognised before any id is compared.

- **Staff ids are per site on schedule rows too** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). A trainer holds ONE `mindbodyStaffId`, from one Mindbody site, and the other site numbers its staff from the same range. The webhook stamps `mindbodyStaffId` on most rows it touches and the pull never clears it, so it can be stale, and a Rotation booking carries the rotation's own staff id. The standing week's first cut let a different staff id decide, and so called every webhook-written Rotation booking, and every booking of a trainer who also works at a studio on the other site, a move with a Free slot. **Never read a different staff id as proof a booking is someone else's.** The row's `trainerId`, matched at that studio, decides; a staff id may only CONFIRM a match, and only for a trainer whose Mindbody record names this studio's site (`trainers/{id}.mindbody.siteId`, read by `staffIdsAt` in `standing-week/check.ts`; written only by the staff webhook and the staff-profile function, so for a trainer without it the staff-id match is simply off).

- **`windowComplete` says every page arrived, not that the pull checked what Journey holds** (the whole-read record's review, Sep 27 2026). `syncMindbodySchedules` sets it from the proxy's answer before the location filter, and an answer holding none of this studio's bookings returns early, before the sweep ("an empty answer never cancels anything by itself: it can be a glitch"). A pull with no `settleSweepWith` (the morning month, Operations -> Mindbody's Sync, every settle pull) then cancels nothing and asks nothing more, yet reports `windowComplete: true`, and a settle pull that came back empty still sets `settledWithMonth`. A studio with a wrong Location ID answers empty and whole on every pull. Anything that must know the pull really looked at the studio's days reads `studioAnswered` (and `settleAnswered` for the pull a near window handed its losses to) as well: `readWhole` in `features/openings/coverage.ts` refuses a count of 0. `windowComplete` also stays true when a single booking is skipped with an error (an unreadable start time, a write that threw inside the per-appointment loop). Leave `settledWithMonth` as it is: `useAutoSync` stamps `lastDeepScheduleSyncAt` from it, and changing it changes when the month is pulled.

- **Openings reads the bookings Journey already holds, and a few rules keep it honest** (Openings round, Sep 27 2026; read `src/features/openings/README.md`, `src/features/openings/ui/README.md` and `docs/rounds/2026-09-27-openings.md` first).
  - **The Sunday job's Openings step never joins the main batch.** Step 8 (`server/openings-step.ts`) writes after the job's `commitInBatches`, in its own batch, inside its own catch: in the main batch, one bad document would take the trends, the Kaizen reports and the performance watch down with it.
  - **A failed read of a studio's standing weeks, bookings or last Sunday's summary skips that studio; never fold with an empty list.** An empty standing-weeks list closes every agreed week on last Sunday's date, and a missing previous summary loses the agreed weeks' history for good (nothing else holds it). A month of the whole-read record that can't be read is the one exception: its days just don't count.
  - **Never reuse the machine-trends window for Openings.** It counts UTC days. Openings' window is `foldWindow(studioTodayKey(now, tz), tz)`: the studio's own days, never "now is Sunday" and never the zone of the computer running the job.
  - **The weekly-job test's fake database ignores `where` and date ranges.** So the step filters rows to its own studio itself, the window is tested in `openings/fold.test.ts` (a Wednesday, a Sunday and the Nov 1 clock change), and the fake records the queries and reads so the index shape and the read count (six a studio, two a run) are still held.
  - **A Mindbody "Unavailable" row is a trainer's blocked time, not a booking.** Ask `isStaffBlock` (`src/lib/booking-state.ts`) before counting any booking. Before the Openings round Team's check could say "Unavailable is booked in Judy's slot", and Operations → Changes could list a cancelled or moved block, or read one as a client's rebook. And don't just drop it: anything that says a trainer has ROOM must take them out for the time their block covers (Openings' `addBlock` in `features/openings/room.ts`), or the blocked hour reads as room (the Openings round's final review found "Times with room" offering it to a client).
  - **Don't copy the booking-to-trainer rule; call it.** `whose.ts`'s `placeBooking` is the standing week check's own `trainerOf`, so Openings and the check can't disagree about whose booking it is.
  - **`useWeekSchedule` also returns rows whose `movedFromDay` is in the window while their `startTime` is outside it.** Place a booking by its own studio day (`next-days.ts` does), or a booking moved to next week shows up this week.
  - **Anything that points to Openings counts Openings' lines, never the check's findings.** The check also holds slots earlier today and on Sundays, which Openings leaves out: `teamLine` and `teamNoneSentence` take the `nextDays` result, and the Overview uses `overviewLines`, so Team's "3 free slots" is exactly what Openings lists.
  - **"Rebook" means the client's own other booking, on Operations → Changes.** Openings' cancellation line is about the TIME and says "and nobody has booked into it since", never "not rebooked"; it borrows `isRealRebook` for its timing only.
  - **A booking Mindbody MOVES keeps its row and its old `createdAt`.** The sync and the webhook stamp `movedAt` and leave `createdAt` alone, so "when did this booking come to this time" is the later of the two (`arrivedAt` in `features/openings/next-days.ts`), never `createdAt` alone. A booking reassigned to another trainer at the same time gets no stamp at all.
  - **"Booked again from" reads only the days after the slot**, so its sentences name the day they reach ("not booked again through Fri, Nov 13"), never "the next 7 days": a booking between today and the slot was never looked at.
  - **The summary stores who was in (`i`) only on judged days.** A missing or empty `i` on a day that couldn't be judged means "not known", never "nobody in"; an empty half-hour there reads "Usually none booked ... Room can't be judged yet", never blank.
  - **On a past day a trainer is in only with an agreed week in force AND a booking that day.** A late cancellation counts as a booking; one cancelled in good time doesn't (they may have been told not to come in).
  - **Only the weeks of people who still work at the studio go to the check** (`who-works-here`), in `nextDays` and on Team alike, or a departed trainer's agreed week reads as free slots.
  - **The Overview's `week.entries` is empty until the server answers** (its read is `{ confirmed: true }` since the Openings round). A new panel reading it says "Reading the week…" or "Could not be read just now.", never 0 and never "Nothing needs you right now".
  - **Nothing offers a time for good until the marks are read from the server** (`data.marks.read === "ready"`). A time marked Always full is never offered, and `offers()` can only leave out a mark it is given: A new regular time and the Wrap-up's sheet both gate on it.
  - **Other features import Openings through `features/openings/ui/index.ts`, which is reads only.** The Wrap-up is in the Active Session's chunk; importing the section there would pull the section, Relay's Context Panel and their stylesheets into it.
  - **A door from one My Studio section to another calls `openMyStudioSection(next)`** (`my-studio/section-memory.ts`): the mounted shell moves through its leave scope and remembers the section only once it has moved. A door from outside My Studio sets `rememberMyStudioSection(next)` (and `showOpenings(part, whose)` for Openings), then switches the app's view.
  - **`clients/{id}.lastSessionDate` has two writers.** Journey writes it at Finish and on import; the Mindbody webhook writes Mindbody's `lastVisited` into it (`functions/src/mindbody/index.ts`, around line 660). So the date is neither "in Journey" nor proof that nothing came after it: My clients says only "Last visit on file: {date}", true whichever wrote it (`trainer-profile/my-clients.ts`). Don't word it "Last session" or "Last in Journey" until the two writers are kept apart, which is a Cloud Functions change: AJ's OK.
  - **A snapshot's `lastVisitDate` never empties** (the inactive round, Oct 1 2026). The renewals engine carries the last visit forward night after night (`lastVisitHint`), long after it leaves the 90-day window, so "has a last visit" is not "active": the nightly sweep called every past client active and pulled them from Mindbody every month for ever. Who is out of the sweep is `renewals/job-plan.ts` `sweepsPast` (the Journey's Inactive, last night's or a leader's mark that still holds, never with a booking ahead).
