Anything new that asks "is she still a client?" asks the Journey's state, not the snapshot's last visit alone.

### The speed round (Oct 5 2026)

- **Every Mindbody call has a per-attempt time limit** (R22; `server/mindbody-client.ts`): 30 s (`MINDBODY_ATTEMPT_TIMEOUT_MS`), the schedule pull's 500-booking pages 60 s (`MINDBODY_LARGE_PAGE_TIMEOUT_MS`). A caller that needs longer passes `{ timeoutMs }` to `mindbodyGet`, never its own signal: `mindbodyFetch` replaces a caller's signal with the attempt's. A timeout is a 504 in the route's usual error shape, never an empty answer. The cron jobs share the client, so a slow night page now fails that call through the job's own failure handling instead of hanging it.
- **The Gemini OCR routes share ONE request slot for the whole process** (R20; `server/gemini-routes.ts` `createScanGate`), taken after the sign-in and before the per-person limit and the body, answering `503 busy` with `Retry-After` at once, never a queue. A new OCR route goes through the same gate, or the memory risk comes back on the smaller plan. A scan is many requests, so a browser caller must wait a busy answer out (`waitWhileScanBusy`, `src/services/chart-scan.ts`) rather than fail, or a multi-page scan loses its pages. Each attempt has 90 s and the whole request 120 s (`GEMINI_SCAN_DEADLINE_MS`); the slot is kept while the model works even if the caller left, because the pages are still in memory.
- **`shutDownGracefully` owns SIGTERM** (R19; `server/shutdown.ts`). Nothing else in `server.ts` calls `process.exit` on it, and SIGINT is left alone so Ctrl-C still stops `npm run dev`. The web service's `startCommand` is `node build/server.cjs`, Node itself: through npm the signal might never arrive (a test holds it equal to `npm start`'s target). Render waits 60 s before the signal; `maxShutdownDelaySeconds: 120` after it is what `render.yaml` asks for, but the Blueprint is not linked and the dashboard has no field for it (checked Oct 6 2026), so the live service has Render's default until it is set through the API or a linked Blueprint. The drain works (the Oct 6 deploy's old instance logged "SIGTERM: no new connections"); only a chart scan still running past the default is cut.
- **`/api/log-error` sits ahead of the shared JSON parser** (`server/log-error.ts`, `isLogErrorPath`): its own 16 KB parser, 120 reports a minute per address (about six iPads share a studio's address) and 1,200 overall. Moving it below the shared 1 MB parser undoes the cap.
- **The sign-in check's reads time out as "unknown"** (R22; `server/auth.ts` `restGet`, 15 s, `AUTH_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`): a timeout is an HTTP 504 with a reason, which rides on the last good cached answer or answers 503 "Couldn't confirm your account just now", never "signed out" or "not staff", and a cut-off body is never read as an empty document.
- **The webhook's idempotency record is a CLAIM** (R25; `functions/src/mindbody/idempotency.ts`). Never write `state: done` before the work commits: a platform kill at the 10 s timeout runs no catch, and a record written first answered Mindbody's resend as a duplicate, losing the event. A new early `return 200` inside the handler's `try` still goes through `markEventDone`, or the claim stays "processing", is taken over on each resend and is dead-lettered after four. If the function's `timeoutSeconds` is ever raised past about two minutes, raise `CLAIM_MS` with it. Mindbody's own rule: a 2xx within 10 s, else a resend every 15 minutes for 3 hours.
- **Removing a function's export does not delete the deployed function** (R25). Ship a deletion with `npx firebase functions:delete <name> --region us-central1 --force --project prod`, and never deploy with a plain `--only functions`, which would also ask about every deleted function and deploy the undeployed staff-photo pair.
- **The night's month tally reads sessions the screens' way** (R27; `server/month-tally-step.ts`): hosted at the studio, by `createdAt` (the existing `(hostedAtStudioId, createdAt DESC)` index), not the renewals job's by-client read, because Hours counts sessions hosted at the studio, visitors included. It writes five months because Insights' 90 days plus two weeks of late logs can span five.
- **A night's tally, or a kept copy of sessions, never serves an In-Progress session's state** (R26, R27). Sessions the night or the 15-minute keep saw open are read again by id (`documentId() in`, ten a read, in parallel) on every use; one that can't be read stays as it was seen, never guessed closed. What the night cannot see until the next night: an edit to, or removal of, a past session it counted as completed. An answer only the cache gave is never kept.

### The iPad round (Oct 6 2026)

- **Never add a heavy field to `clients/{id}`: every open streams the whole studio's client documents** (the iPad round, Oct 6 2026; `docs/rounds/2026-10-06-ipad.md`). The Hub, the Directory and Operations hold ONE listener on the studio's clients (`useStudioRoster`, up to 1,500 documents), and on an iPad the Firestore SDK and its stream decoder spend their time in proportion to the bytes and fields taken in. The perf lab measured a 300-client roster at about 4.15 MB, 14 KB a client, **73% of it two maps no list draws** (`currentMachineMetrics`, `machineStats`), and it was most of the 5.5 s (iPad 10th gen) to 7 s (older iPad) between the trainer record and the Hub's data. Per-client detail that only the profile, the session or a job reads goes in its own document beside the client (a subcollection of `clients/{id}`, read by id: the rules take access from the parent, so no copy and no index), never on the client document. Before adding any field to a client, ask what every open pays for it: 1 KB a client is 0.3 MB on a 300-client open and 1.5 MB at 1,500. The roster split (`oct6/ipad-data`, AJ's OK) is the first such move.
- **The roster's clients have no machine maps once the split has run** (the roster split, Oct 6 2026; `src/features/machine-totals/README.md`). `currentMachineMetrics`, `machineStats` and `machineStatsBackfilledAt` live at `clients/{id}/machineTotals/current`. Anything that reads them off a client from `useStudioRoster` (a list, a studio report, machine fit's studio tier) gets nothing after the migration, silently: read `useMachineTotals` for the one client on screen, or `getAll` the documents by id in a job (`server/machine-totals-read.ts`). Machine fit's studio tier already lost "performed since" this way (fewer samples, never a wrong one; AJ's open question).
- **A machine count is never copied across without deleting it where it was, in the same write** (the roster split). Every reader SUMS `timesPerformed` across the client's old field and the totals document, so a write that copies a count into one side and leaves it on the other doubles it. Increments go to the totals document through `machine-totals/store.ts`; a whole-history rebuild (the profile's backfill, the console repair, the duplicate-merge script, the migration, the way back) replaces one side and deletes the other in one batch or transaction. The rules refuse SETTING `machineStatsBackfilledAt` on a client for that reason: an iPad still on the old version backfilled a whole-history `machineStats` onto the client.
- **A totals document still loading is not "nothing on file"** (the roster split's review). Ask `machineTotalsKnown(client)` before acting on the maps as an answer: Start's prefill waits for it, Finish with the totals unknown writes the counts and today's last set only (never a first pair, never an old "last time"), "Finish it as it was" and Log past session's Save hold ("Reading the machines..."), and a document only this iPad has written (offline, `hasPendingWrites`) is still loading. An empty answer from the cache is loading too; only the server says "missing".
- **A push to `master` deploys nothing on Render, and the crons are deployed separately** (found Oct 6 2026, after the release). Render has no access to the repo (the build log's "It looks like we don't have access to your repo, but we'll try to clone it anyway"), so no push event reaches it and every deploy in the web service's history is "Manually triggered by you via Dashboard". The two cron jobs have Auto-Deploy "On Commit" too, which therefore never fires, and nobody pressed theirs: after the speed round and the release both crons still ran `c20d2ab`, the night before both, so the renewals job had no month tally and would have read clients without their machine maps once the roster split ran (its strength proof reads `machineStats`). A ship is not done until the web service's Manual Deploy AND both crons' Builds → Manual Build show the new commit. The fix is Render's GitHub app on the repo (only the owner, `MaxStrengthFitness`, can install it); then Auto-Deploy "After CI checks pass" and a build filter ignoring `docs/**` and `**/*.md` become possible.
- **The totals' rules must be live before the app that writes them** (the roster split). If the app reaches the iPads first, Finish's batch is refused whole; `totalsCommit` then retries the client's counters alone, but that session's machine maps are lost. `ship-release.ps1` deploys the rules first and checks the file it deployed holds the totals' match.

- **An effect lists only what it reads, above all one that opens listeners** (the floor group, Oct 6 2026; `799e20c6`). The Active Session's sessions, routines and settings listeners had the studio's whole client list in their dependencies though nothing in the effect read it, so every roster change (a webhook, the nightly job, another iPad, the selected client's own read a moment after Start) tore all three down and opened them again, and the briefing could be drawn from a new listener's partial cached answer before the server's. When a value DOES need to reach the screen (the roster split's totals), let it reach it through state the effect doesn't own (the kept `[clientId, clients]` effect that refreshes `selectedClient`), not by re-listening.
- **A pseudo element some rule hides can't carry a veil for every cell** (the floor group's review). The Journey grid dims an older column with the cell's `::after`, but the lanes hide a practice cell's `::after` (its dashed frame), so that one cell kind stayed at full strength. A new rule on a shared `::before` / `::after` must show it again where another rule hid it; `dim-veil.test.ts` checks every cell kind.
- **`will-change` is for the loading mark only** (`paint-cost.test.ts` section 4). A transform and opacity `@keyframes` is composited without it; asking WebKit for a layer per confetti bit cost 24 to 36 layers for a 1.3 s burst.

<a id="rules"></a>

## Machines and the template boundary

*Machine authoring, Sep 20 2026; the catalog gate, Sep 20 2026.*

**The template boundary does NOT cover a submission, and cannot.**
`scopeOverrides` protects the roster write, where a studio is editing a COPY
of a catalog machine. A studio's own machine (`source: "custom"`) inherits
nothing, so it legitimately stores the whole definition — method included —
and publishing it copies that into `machines/{id}` verbatim. The protection
there is a human reading it (`features/admin/catalog/review.ts`,
`SubmissionReview.tsx`), not a filter. **If you add another path that turns a
studio document into a catalog document, it needs the same review, not a call
to `scopeOverrides`** — stripping the method off a submission would publish a
machine with no cadence, which is worse.

**`BLOCKING_GAPS` must stay clearable by the catalog itself.**
`review.test.ts` asserts all twenty generated MSF definitions pass every
blocking check. Adding one that the Academy's guides do not state — dial
defaults, synergists, secondary musculature — fails that test, and it is
telling you the truth: a gate the standard would fail stops being read.

**Publishing sends `reviewedDefinition ?? definition`.** Read it through
`definitionUnderReview()`, never off the raw document, or the row, the
suggested id, the gate and the write drift apart. And never edit
`definition` in place: it is what the studio sent, and it is what makes
`correctedFields` — and the sentence the studio reads on its floor — true.

**The catalog documents were in the LEGACY shape, and that is why the editor
looked empty.** `machines/{id}` was seeded by Operations → System Tools →
"Restore standard machines" from `data/default-machines.ts`, the legacy
`Machine` type (`targetMuscles` as one comma string, `settingOptions` as bare
labels, nothing for the biomechanics template), while the editor reads
`MachineDefinition`. About 6 of 60 inputs filled. The seeder wrote
`data/machine-definitions.ts` until Sep 28 2026, when AJ took the button
out; a correction to the live catalog is made in the catalog editor. **If a machine ever opens sparse again, check
the document's shape before blaming the form.**

**`emptyMachineDefinition()` must not guess the taxonomy.** It used to default
`anatomicalRegion` to "Chest" and `movementPattern` to "Upper Body: Horizontal
Push". No catalog document had a pattern of its own, so `normalize` handed that
default to the form for all twenty machines — leg press included — and saving
wrote it in. Those fields start empty now and the section rail asks for them. A
confident wrong value is worse than a missing one.

**A bag of values merges per key, not whole.** `universalBaseline`,
`bodyTypeAdjustments` and `defaultSettings` are independent fields under one
key. Replacing the object on override meant a studio correcting one seat
position stopped live-inheriting the other four baseline lines forever, and a
later admin correction reached every location EXCEPT the ones that had edited
that object. `mergeMachineDefinition` merges them per key (`""` is a deliberate
clear) and `pruneOverrides` reduces a stored override to the sub-keys that
actually differ. Both halves are needed; either alone does nothing.

**Write a roster override with `updateDoc`, never `setDoc(..., {merge:true})`.**
Firestore merges maps deeply, so a merge write keeps a key the studio has just
reverted — "use the standard" appears to work and then silently does not.

**A screen that edits part of a roster entry writes that part, and never what
the machine IS.** (Sep 28 2026.) Local set-up (`equipment/LocalSetupDialog.tsx`)
saved by rebuilding the whole roster document with `buildClone` (`source:
"catalog"`, `basedOn` its own id, `status: "active"`) and merging it in, and it
was offered on every machine: the door on My Studio → Machines and every row of
Admins → All locations → Equipment. On a studio's own machine that made it a
copy of a catalog machine that does not exist, and `useStudioMachines` skips a
catalog entry whose `basedOn` has no catalog document (a `console.warn`,
nothing else): the machine left the floor list, the Active Session and the
Catalog, and no screen could open it again to put it right. On a machine out of
service it put it back in service, and a cleared box kept its old value (the
merge trap above). The Sep 20 audit had found the last two
(`rounds/claude-experiment/05-studio-ops.md`, #1). **Identity (`machineId`,
`studioId`, `source`, `basedOn`) is written when an entry is created, and never
by an edit; `status` is the floor list's switch and nobody else's.** The dialog
now writes its four fields as paths with `updateDoc`, deleting what was left
blank (`localSetupUpdate` in `equipment/clone.ts`), and is offered on a Max
Strength machine only. Nothing had been damaged in production
(`scripts/check-roster-damage.ts`, read-only). **To test a roster writer, read
the result back through the real `useStudioMachines`:** a dropped entry is a
machine gone from every screen and nothing fails loudly, so only the floor
itself shows it (`LocalSetupDialog.render.test.tsx` does, over an in-memory
Firestore that keeps the merge and update rules).

**`machineId` is a foreign key. Never re-mint it on a rename.** It is queried
across studios in `exerciseLogs`, `clientMachineSettings` and `routines`.
`MachineSettingField.key` is the same kind of thing one level down: it is written
into every client's saved settings, so the editor shows it as a fixed badge and
only the label is editable. Renaming a key orphans every stored value for that
dial.

**Compute a studio's overrides from the whole DRAFT, not from the save patch.**
The patch is the difference between this edit and what was on screen; an override
is the difference between the studio's machine and the catalog's. Using the patch
drops every override made in an earlier sitting.

**Kinematic class is not the turnaround protocol.** Compound = multi-joint,
Simple = single-joint, and it comes from `kinematicClassification`. The Academy
gives the COMPOUND ROW a pause and a squeeze at the contracted position;
deriving the class from that relabels the row, the pulldown and the pullover as
single-joint and splits every cross-studio roll-up.

**`isStandardSetMachine` treats an ABSENT flag as in-the-set.** A UI that reads
`m.inStandardSet` directly shows OFF for every legacy document while the seeder
treats it as ON. Read through the function, and write an explicit `false` to take
a machine out.

**The clinical matrix and MACHINE_DATABASE speak a different id language from
the floor — go through `canonicalMachineId`, and test with catalog ids.**
(Sep 24 2026.) `clinical-matrix.ts` names machines by MACHINE_DATABASE keys
(`lumbar_extension`, `abdominals`, `4_way_neck`); the floor carries catalog ids
(`m-lumbar`, `m-abs`, `m-neck`) and studio names (`LUMBAR`, `CX (4 WAY
NECK)`). Matching by normalised id-or-name meant nine of the twenty standard
machines never showed a clinical watch-out anywhere — the tracker's shield, the
Now Bar, the Machine sheet, the routine rows — and every test passed, because
every test used the matrix's own key as the machine id. `lib/clinical-watchouts.ts`
now resolves both sides through `features/catalog/machine-identity.ts` (the one
table; do not add a second), a studio's own machine is matched on its lineage
(`comparisonKey`, which `toFloorMachines` now carries — and ONLY it: the
app-wide `machines` list has no studio machines and no lineage, so a screen
holding that list goes through `studioFloorOf`, as the client profile does for
the codex's Watch-outs since the landing), and
`clinical-watchouts.test.ts` fails if a matrix key stops resolving to a real
catalog machine. The same mismatch hid the first-time set-up guide's steps
(`MACHINE_DATABASE[id]` with an `m-…` id): `knowledgeOf` in
`features/equipment/adapters.ts` is the lookup. **A test for anything that
matches machines should use the ids the floor actually carries.** Never "fix"
it by rewriting the matrix's keys — the watch-outs are quoted from it.

**The generator must not read `imageUrl` from `machine-database.ts`.** It bundles
the data files with esbuild's text loader, so that field comes back as the
`.webp`'s bytes and gets baked into the generated source — 30 KB per machine.

- **A merge `setDoc` keeps a map key you took out** (the routine template editor, Oct 8 2026, `admin/routines/template-save.ts`). `setDoc(ref, data, { merge: true })` writes a map key by key, so a starting routine's word removed, its head office default switched off, its day one shortened or a machine's note deleted all came back on the next read. The editor writes only the fields that changed, each whole, with `updateDoc` (the admin kit's "only the diff is written"); a starting routine switched off has its `start` part removed and parked (`startParked`), so switching it back on brings back the steps, source and kind the editor has no control for.

- **"Before the seed" is decided from evidence, never from an empty list** (starting routines, the whole-branch review, Oct 9 2026; `routine-plan/starting-read.ts` `seededFrom`). Start a plan offers the Academy's eleven from code only before the seed has run. A head office starting routine, one switched off in the editor, or a seeded `academy-` id means it ran, so every one retired is an empty offer (the trainer builds the lineup), never the eleven back. The seed itself never brings back an id an administrator deleted (`system/startingRoutinesSeed` lists what it wrote; `--again <id>` on purpose). Ids are stored for good in plans and studios' choices, so they never carry a gender either (`academy-clear-dip-adduction`, `academy-clear-chest-pulldown`).

## Security rules and permissions

- **Never deploy indexes with `--force` once the TTL policies are set** (the speed round, Oct 2026). Production holds two TTL field overrides, `expiresAt` on `mindbodyEventLog` and `mindbodyBookingCancels`, set by hand with gcloud after the round's golive. `firestore.indexes.json` does not hold them (the Enterprise edition refuses field overrides in it), and firebase-tools lists TTL configs as field overrides: an interactive index deploy asks to delete them, and `--force` deletes them without asking, so the webhook's logs would grow without bound again. Deploy indexes with `--non-interactive`; its note that "2 field overrides ... are not present" is expected.

<a id="cross-train"></a>

- **A cross-train visitor could read the client but not finish her session (Sep 24 2026, packages round).**
  Approving a client for cross-training (`approvedCrossTrainStudioIds`) opens
  her document to READ at the other studio (`clientIsReadableBy`), but the
  `clients` update rule asked only `isTrainerOfStudio(home)`. Finish put the
  client's totals (`completedSessions`, `sessionCount`, `lastSessionDate`,
  the machine metrics, the tallies) in the same all-or-nothing batch as the
  session and its sets, so the refused client write took the whole session
  with it: a visiting trainer tapped Finish and nothing saved. Proved on the
  emulator before it was fixed. Fixed twice over: the rules let a trainer
  whose studio the client is approved at write the totals Finish, Start and
  a History delete write, and the summary of a Pulse they finish, and
  nothing else (`crossTrainSessionTotals`, an allowlist of seventeen fields;
  her name, her home, her approvals, her medical history and `renewal` stay
  refused), and `completeWorkoutSession` (`src/lib/sync-utils.ts`) now
  commits the session, sets and settings as one batch and the totals as their
  own write, so any refusal of the totals costs the totals and says so
  (`totalsSaved: false`, a toast), never the session. The totals are queued
  straight after the batch, BEFORE either is awaited: offline, a commit waits
  for the server, and a reload in that wait replays only what was already
  queued, so totals sent "after the batch lands" were lost for good (the
  review caught it). **A write that must never fail does not share a batch
  with one a rule may refuse, and a write that follows it is queued, not
  awaited. A read grant is not a write grant: when you open a document to
  another studio, list what that studio's screens write to it and test each
  one.** Still refused for a visitor, knowingly: the machine-stats backfill
  marker. The consultation wizard's `requiresConsultation` (a prospect
  cross-training before her first consultation) was another, until the
  tracker's First-time setup that wrote it was retired on Oct 8 2026 (the
  first-session design round, §4.8); Use today (`preferredTodayRoutineId`)
  was the third, until it went on Sep 26 2026. Tests: `a cross-train visitor's session` in
  `tests/firestore.rules.test.ts` (the full eight-machine batch fits the
  rules' expression budget) and `src/lib/sync-utils.finish.test.ts`.

- **A menu item is not a gate: Operations had no check behind the menu (Sep 24 2026, sign-out round).**
  The App Mode switch in the trainer menu was the only place that asked who
  may open Operations. The route (`currentView === "admin-dashboard"`) and
  `AdminDashboardView` asked nothing, so a Life Transformer got in two ways:
  a sign-out left the last person's view in place for the next one, and Demo
  Mode - open Operations there (everyone may), choose a real studio, tap
  Operations on the bottom bar. Fixed with ONE question,
  `mayOpenOperations` (`src/features/admin/operations-access.ts`), asked by
  the menu, by the route (`useGuardedPlace`, which derives the view during
  render so a shut screen is never drawn, and writes the refusal back so
  going back into Demo Mode does not reopen it) and by the shell itself.
  **A screen a role may not open checks on the screen, and again on every
  change of person and studio. Hiding the door only helps the honest.**

- **A sign-out must forget everything that was the person's (Sep 24 2026, sign-out round).**
  `App` kept `AppContent` mounted through a sign-out - the signed-out screen is
  an early return INSIDE it - so every `useState` survived to the next person:
  the view, the app mode, the selected client. `ActiveStudioProvider` kept the
  active studio in memory, and `localStorage.clear()` reached neither. Module
  scope (`let rememberedTab` and friends) outlives every component, so it
  survived too. Now `App` keys the signed-in tree on `personKey(user,
  trainer)` and `src/features/sign-out/` clears the rest: local storage but the
  pinned studio, the one-shot session-storage handoffs, and every module
  memory registered with `forgetOnSignOut`. **A new module-level variable
  that remembers what a person was doing registers a reset with
  `forgetOnSignOut` beside it**; one keyed by the signed-in uid does not need
  to. **Don't key the tree on the bare uid:** the Microsoft domain check signs
  an outsider in and straight back out, and its sentence would be lost to the
  remount in between.

- **A list the rules must check place by place runs out of the 1,000-expression budget fast** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). A rule cannot loop, so `standingWeekAwayValid` checks the `away` list's six places one by one, and every write to a standing week re-validates the stored list. In the emulator a leader's write already ran out at ten ranges (a trainer's at fourteen), so the app and the rules allow six (`MAX_AWAY` in `standing-week/week.ts` and the six in `standingWeekAwayValid` — change them together), and each check is kept lean. The rules tests hold the fullest real writes to it: a leader agreeing, and a trainer proposing, on a week with six ranges and a full proposed and agreed week. **Measure a new per-row check in the emulator for every role, on both create and update.**

- **`writesForStudio` is itself most of the budget** (the whole-read record, Sep 27 2026). A write rule of `writesForStudio(studioId)` and one list check (a month's 31 possible days) ran out for a **guest** trainer (found last, through `activeGuestStudioIds`) with no role claim on the token, while a trainer at home squeaked under. The same people, asked with the caller resolved once, cost less than half: `let r = getRole(); let t = callerTrainer();` then `roleIsSuper(r) || roleIsFranchise(r) || trainerWorksAt(t, studioId) || trainerLeads(r, t, studioId)` (`coverageWriterAllowed`). Reads that check nothing else still use `writesForStudio`. **To measure rather than guess:** the emulator's rule-coverage report, `GET http://127.0.0.1:8080/emulator/v1/projects/{projectId}:ruleCoverage`, read before and after ONE request; the sum of every `values[].count` in `report` is what that request evaluated. On that scale the guest's write stopped at 2,107 and a home trainer's full month took 2,069 (it passed), so the budget runs out at about 2,100 of those counts; as built the costliest write is 907.

- **The 1000-expression budget bites three times now, and it reads as a permission error.**
  Firestore refuses a rule that evaluates more than 1000 expressions with
  "Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to
  evaluate has been reached" — which surfaces as PERMISSION_DENIED and looks
  like a rule saying no. `getRole()` reads the trainer document TWICE on its
  document-backed path, and `isStudioOwnerOrHeadTrainerOnly()` calls
  `getRole()` three times and `getTrainerData()` five, so stacking
  `isSuperAdmin() || isFranchiseOwnerOnly() || isStudioOwnerOrHeadTrainer()`
  in one condition is enough on its own. It took the sessions read rule down
  (Sep 9), the hub_announcement rules (fixed with `let r = getRole()`),
  `teamJobs` (Sep 19, `teamJobLeaderAllowed`), and the **trainers update
  rule** (Sep 20, Demo Mode - see the next trap). **The fix is always the same:
  extract the condition into a function, `let r = getRole()` and
  `callerTrainer()` once, and ask `trainerLeads(r, t, studioId)` rather than
  the helper that re-reads.** `let` is legal in a function body and not in an
  `allow` condition, which is why these are functions.

- **The trainers UPDATE rule went over the budget (Sep 20, Demo Mode), and reordering did not save it.**
  Demo Mode added a clause opening with `isAnyAuthenticatedTrainer()` - an
  `exists()` - followed by the whole of `isDemoTrainerPayload()`, running on
  EVERY trainer update including a trainer saving their own bio. The first
  assertion of "lets a trainer edit their own profile" began failing with
  PERMISSION_DENIED.

  **The first attempt was to reorder that clause so its free literal tests led
  and `&&` short-circuited out of it. The test still failed.** Worth knowing
  why: the demo clause was never the expense. The expense was everything under
  it - `isSuperAdmin()`, `isFranchiseOwner()`,
  `isStudioOwnerOrHeadTrainer()`, `roleChangeAllowed(getRole())` and
  `grantChangeAllowed(getRole())` each re-deriving `getRole()`, which reads the
  trainer document twice on its document-backed path. Roughly ten expansions in
  one condition. Demo Mode did not create that; it added the last straw.

  **Ordering is a trim, not the cure. The cure is always resolve-once.** Fixed
  with `trainerUpdateAllowed(trainerId, getRole(), callerTrainer())` - one
  `getRole()`, one `callerTrainer()`, and pure map lookups below, with
  `trainerLeads(r, t, studioId)` standing in for
  `isStudioOwnerOrHeadTrainer()` (it mirrors that helper's DB branch exactly,
  demo studio and grant included) and `grantChangeAllowed(r, t)` taking the
  caller document instead of re-reading it. **When a rule is near the budget,
  count the `getRole()` expansions before you touch anything else.**

- **"Everybody may do X in the demo studio" is a door into every rule that calls the helper (Sep 20).**
  Demo Mode put `hasRunOfDemo(studioId)` at the top of
  `isStudioOwnerOrHeadTrainerOnly()` - correct for the demo studio, and
  instantly enough to make every OTHER rule asking "is this person a studio
  leader" answer yes for `demo-studio`. The `trainers` create rule has a
  studio-leader clause that never constrains `accessibleStudioIds` or
  `ownedStudioIds`, because it trusts the leader. So any signed-in trainer
  could create a trainer document at ANY id with `primaryHomeStudioId:
  'demo-studio'` and `accessibleStudioIds: ['demo-studio', 'studioA']` - a
  claim on a REAL studio, plantable at a colleague's uid before their first
  sign-in. Fixed with `!isDemoStudioId(...)` on that clause, so demo trainers
  are minted by `isDemoTrainerPayload` and nowhere else. **Before widening a
  role helper for the demo studio, list every rule that calls it and ask what
  each one lets a leader do.**
- **A self-made trainer document is a claim or the owner's bootstrap, nothing else** (Oct 3 2026, `docs/rounds/2026-10-03-trainer-self-create.md`). The `trainers` create rule's bootstrap clause (`request.auth.uid == trainerId`) capped the role but never the studios, so any Google account could create `trainers/{own uid}` at any real studio and skip the leader's approval. It now also needs `isOwnerBootstrap()` or `selfCreateIsAClaim()`: the payload names its placeholder in `claimedFromId`, the placeholder is `pendingClaim`, unclaimed and carries the signed-in email, and the new document differs from it only by `claimedProfile()`'s own keys. **Add a field to `claimedProfile()` (`features/trainer-identity/claim.ts`) and you must add it to `claimMatchesPlaceholder`'s list,** or every claim is refused and the person is left on a placeholder they cannot write; the rules tests build the claim with the real `claimedProfile`, so they catch it. A new screen that writes `trainers/{uid}` for the signed-in person needs its own clause, not a wider bootstrap.

- **The role lives on the token now (cost round, Sep 16).** `syncTrainerClaims` (`functions/src/claims.ts`) mirrors `trainers/{id}.role` onto the auth user's custom claims; the rules read `request.auth.token.role` first and fall back to the document only when it is absent. Consequences: a role change reaches the rules at the next sign-in or within an hour (the token is checked first, so the OLD claim wins until then); **never set a `studioId` claim** (as written it grants studio-leader access with no role check); a new trainer role must be added to `TRAINER_ROLES` in `claims-logic.ts` as well as `UserRole`; the function has to be deployed (`firebase deploy --only functions:syncTrainerClaims`) and `scripts/backfill-trainer-claims.ts --commit` run once — until then it just costs the read it always did.

- **Closed Sep 16 (cost round):** a trainer can no longer edit their own `role`, `ownedStudioIds`, `accessibleStudioIds`, `activeGuestStudioIds` or `primaryHomeStudioId` (`writesAccessFields()` in the `trainers` update rule); the rest of their own document is still theirs. **Still open, needs AJ's OK:** any trainer can edit any `studios/{id}` document, including `mindbodySiteId`.

- **Also open, needs AJ's OK:** any trainer at any studio can create and update another studio's `taskInstances` and `taskRequests` (studio tasks and requests).

- `progressReports`, `journalEntries`, `exerciseLogs`, `clinicalIncidents` and `schedules` are readable by any signed-in user. Studio-scoped: `clients`, `sessions`, comments, and (since the Learning + Planner round) a studio's `playbook` and `wiki`.

- **Studio content writes need `writesForStudio(studioId)`** since the Learning + Planner round: machine notes, the upkeep log, playbook, wiki blocks and comments. `writesForStudioPerRules` (`src/features/learning/permissions.ts`) mirrors it for buttons.

- **Use the Auth uid, not `authTrainer.id`, for anything a rule pins to the signed-in person** — note paths, comment authors, `readBy`, personal tasks. The two differ on older accounts.

- **Shared lists are collection-group reads.** Each needs a `{path=**}` rule its filters satisfy (`shared == true`) and a collection-group index. Until a new index finishes building, the screen says it couldn't load the shared part.
- **A new query on the Enterprise edition works without its index — by scanning** (wave 2 hub, Sep 28 2026). This database is Firestore's Enterprise edition: it builds no index by itself, and an unindexed query still answers, reading every document in the collection group company-wide, billed by the byte. Nothing fails, so no test sees it. A new query ships with its index in `firestore.indexes.json`, and the index is deployed before the app (the Hub's Get to know read ships with two `ford` indexes). **Since the speed round (Oct 5 2026) a test holds it** (next entry).
- **The Enterprise edition refuses single-field index settings** (notes round, Oct 3 2026). A `fieldOverrides` entry in `firestore.indexes.json` fails the deploy with "Enterprise Edition does not support updating field index configuration", and the ship script stops there. Give a query a composite index in `indexes`, or let a query over one studio's small subcollection scan it; never add a field override.


- **Roster entries name their own studio** (`studioId` must match the path), and a copy adopted from another studio can't be shared.

- **`access_requests` holds two kinds of request with different fields (Sep 24 2026).** A **sign-up** (`AccessRequestView`, no `type`) has `fullName`, `email`, `userId` and `requestedStudioId`. A **studio-access request** (the studio picker's Request Access, `type: "studio_access"`) comes from someone who already has an account: `trainerId` is their Auth uid (the rules pin it), and the studio is `studioId`. Until Sep 24 the picker wrote no `fullName` or `email` and keyed `trainerId` on the trainer document id; the roster read `fullName`, so each request was a nameless row at every studio, and two of them crashed My Studio → Team and Operations → Staff & Roles with `localeCompare` on undefined. The picker now writes through `studioAccessRequest` (`features/admin/staff/studio-access-request.ts`) beside its reader, and `buildStaffRoster` scopes both kinds and never trusts a name to exist. **Approving writes a WHOLE `trainers/{uid}`**, so a studio-access request must never reach that path, because it would replace the person's account. Letting an existing account into another studio is not built. A studio's leader cannot write another studio's trainer under the current rules (`trainerLeads` is checked against the trainer's home studio).

- **A client document must carry a numeric `remainingSessions`, and a home studio** (Sep 24 2026). `isValidClient` requires `remainingSessions` on every create and update, for everyone, super admins included; and the create rule lets a trainer make a client only at a studio they belong to, so a client with no studio is refused for everyone below super admin. A number that looks invented in `remainingSessions` is the rules' price, not a claim: write `0`, as the Mindbody pull sync, Limbo and temporary profiles all do (packages come from `client.renewal`), and never remove the field. Everything else a new client is not told is left ABSENT — no placeholder height, gender or age (`src/lib/new-client-intake.ts`, `consultation-answers.ts` until Oct 9 2026). New-client intake defaults the studio to the iPad's active studio and will not save a blank one.

- **Openings' marks and the standing week's blocks** (Openings round, Sep 27 2026).
  - **A mark is written whole, with `setDoc` and no merge** (`features/openings/ui/marks-store.ts`, the only writer). A merge would leave a removed note behind.
  - **Every mark write is signed again.** The rules require `by.id == request.auth.uid` and `at == request.time` on every create and update, so Keep at the 60-day review, and any change, re-sign the mark as the person making them; an `updateDoc` that changes only the word is refused.
  - **Mark writes ask `coverageWriterAllowed`, not `writesForStudio`**: the same people with the caller looked up once, as the whole-read record does, to stay inside the 1,000-expression budget. The rules tests cover a guest, the grant, a head trainer, a studio leader, a trainer, a studio owner, a franchise owner and an administrator. `openingsMarkValid` is declared INSIDE its match block so it can read the path's `markKey` (the id must equal the weekday and time the mark holds).
  - **The standing week's limits live in two places**, `standing-week/week.ts` (`MAX_RANGES_A_DAY` 3, `MAX_HOURS` 21, `MAX_REGULARS` 80) and `standingWeekShapeValid` in `firestore.rules`. `week.test.ts` reads the rules file and fails if they differ, so change both together. Raising the block count costs no rule expressions (the rule checks the list's length only), unlike the days away, which are checked one by one and capped at six by the budget.
  - **The rules go before the app.** Until the 21-block rule is deployed, a proposal or agreement with a 15th block is refused (the app says so and keeps the week); until the marks block is deployed, the marks read is refused, so A new regular time and the Wrap-up's sheet offer nothing.
  - **An iPad still on a build from before the Openings round cuts a standing week to 14 blocks, silently.** The old build normalises every week it reads with the old `MAX_HOURS` (14), Monday first, so it shows a three-a-day week without its last blocks (Friday's third, all of Saturday and Sunday). A leader who AGREES the week on that build writes the shortened week to both the proposal and the agreed week, and Team then reads "agreed"; a trainer re-proposing there shortens the proposal, and Team reads "changed" only when a week was already agreed. The new rules accept both writes (21 is a ceiling), and nothing can tell which build wrote. So after the push every iPad loads the new version (the Hub, or the new-version line under the header) before anyone proposes, agrees or re-saves a week with three blocks on a day. Repair: the trainer enters the blocks again on the new build, and a leader agrees it again.
  - **Your week's read names the studio** (`fetchSessionsInRange` on `hostedAtStudioId` and `createdAt`), never `trainerId ==`: a query by trainer alone is refused for older accounts, whose sessions carry a trainer document id that isn't their sign-in uid (the rules test "Your week's read" holds both). It passes `fromServer`, so a cache-only answer is "can't read"; Hours, Insights and the Overview don't pass it and read as before. Your week counts exactly what Hours counts (completed, `trainerKeyOf`, `sessionDay`); the exclusions (logged by hand, imported, open, over 180 minutes) apply to first session to last ONLY, or the card stops matching Operations → Team → Hours.

- **No query ships without its index — the guard** (speed round, Oct 5 2026, R3). The Standard-edition habit that "a single-field equality needs no index" is wrong here, and at least twenty comments in the code said it. `src/lib/firestore-indexes.test.ts` (over `src/lib/firestore-index-guard.ts`, a parser on TypeScript's syntax tree) reads every query in `src`, `server.ts`, `server/` (the crons included) and `functions/src`, and fails when one has no index of its scope. An index serves a query when it LEADS with one of the query's equality fields (or its array-contains field, or, with no equality, its range or `orderBy` field); a `collectionGroup` query needs a `COLLECTION_GROUP` index; a query that branches (a conditional constraint, or `or()`) needs an index for every branch; a query whose constraints the guard can't follow (a parameter, a library helper, a name from another module) fails as unreadable — name the collection and fields with literals or a ref helper. To fix a failure, copy the JSON it prints into `indexes` as a composite of at least two fields, replacing the `CHOOSE_A_SECOND_FIELD_THESE_DOCUMENTS_CARRY` placeholder with a field the documents carry (Enterprise indexes are dense, so the second field excludes nothing). Only one client's or one trainer's own small subcollection may go in the test's ALLOWED list, with its reason; a stale allowance fails too. A query that is served but still reads a whole studio through an index's prefix (Query Insights shows documents scanned per call) gets its own composite, as `clients (homeStudioId, renewal.situation)` did. Deploy: `npx firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes --project prod --non-interactive` (never deletes one), before the rules and the push. An index builds in the background; until it is Enabled its query scans as before, so nothing breaks while it builds.
- **The night's month tally is leaders only, carved out of the generic watch read** (speed round, R27): `studios/{s}/watch/hours-*` and `watch/sessions-*` match a regex on `watchId` and need `wave2Leads` (inside Demo Mode, any trainer); nobody writes them from the app. Every other watch document reads as before.
- **Every notification writer sets `readAt: null`** (speed round, R16). The unread badge is a `readAt == null` query, so a notification without the field would never count as unread; the create rule already refuses one without it.

- **A plan change names at most 30 machines, and the WHOLE batch is refused over it** (the routine plan, Oct 7 to 9 2026). `planChangeOk` holds `machineIds.size() <= 30`, the change's `kind` to the twelve it knows, `byUid` to the Auth uid (never `authTrainer.id`) and `at` to `request.time`; `planChanges` are append-only. The plan, the routine and the change go in one batch, so one change over the limit loses all three: `lineup.ts` `signedChange` holds the list to 30 before it is written. A studio's choice of starting routines (`config/startingRoutines`) holds `use` to 80 ids and is signed the same way (`startingChoiceValid`).

- **A set's client changes only in the write that gives its open session that client and closes it: the Assign rule** (the open session round, Oct 9 2026; finding 3). The exerciseLogs update rule refuses a set's `clientId` changing, so the old Assign, which updated the session and then each set one at a time, moved the session and none of its sets, with only a `console.error`. `logTakesItsSessionsClient` lets a set with NO client take a client only when its session was open before the write (`get(...).isUnassigned == true`) and, after it, names that client and is closed (`getAfter(...)`: `clientId` the set's, `isUnassigned` false). So Who's this? is ONE `writeBatch` holding the session and every set (`open-session/assign.ts`), and a set written alone can never follow; Finish closes a session that was ever open (`completeWorkoutSession`), so a Finish after a refused Who's this? is that write too. `logLeavesItsOpenSessionsClient` lets a set of a still-open session let go of a client (a set typed after the tap lands with the client even when the batch is refused, and Finish would be refused for it every time). Each set's rule reads its session with `get` and `getAfter`; the rules tests hold a twenty-set floor session in one batch. Gather the sets when the batch is BUILT, never at the tap: a set typed while the reads were out and left out of the batch can never take the client afterwards.
- **An open session's own fields** (the whole-branch review, Oct 9 2026; `openSessionWriteOk` in the sessions update rule). `heldSetup` changes only while the session is open (`isUnassigned` true before the write): the assign's batch deletes it in the write that closes the session, and on a client's session nothing would ever read or move it. And an open session is given only a client on record once the write lands (`existsAfter`): New client issues the client's own write first, and when that is refused (a home studio the trainer isn't on) the assign is refused with it, never a session and sets naming a client that doesn't exist. These rules went out BEFORE the app: under the old ones every Who's this? batch is refused, and the app before the round never writes what they refuse.

<a id="tooling"></a>

## React, tests, dates and tooling

- **A deploy deletes every screen file an open app hasn't fetched yet** (new-version round, Sep 26 2026; read `src/features/new-version/README.md`). Each `React.lazy` screen is a file named for its build, `server.ts` answers 404 for an old one, and React remembers the failure until the page reloads. So:
  - **A new lazy screen goes inside a `LoadBoundary`.** `lazy-screens.test.ts` lists every file that makes one and fails on a new file until it is added with its boundary.
  - **A screen's own error screen goes OUTSIDE its LoadBoundary** (the calendar's does). Otherwise it catches the missing file first and offers a raw reload.
  - **Never `preventDefault()` on `vite:preloadError`.** The error must reach the boundary; swallowed, React.lazy fails later with a confusing error of its own.
  - **Never `window.location.reload()` from a screen.** A Home Screen app reloaded offline opens to a blank screen, and a reload mid-session is exactly what the floor forbids. Ask `useNewVersion` / `whenToLoad` (`verdict.ts`).
  - **Don't serve `version.json` through the static handler.** It caches root files for an hour, so an iPad would be told the old build's name for up to an hour.

- **A reducer passed to `useReducer` must close over NOTHING declared below it.** React calls a reducer while processing a QUEUED action, and it does that during the next render at the point of the `useReducer` call — so a wrapper arrow reading a `const` declared a few lines further down reads it in its temporal dead zone. The four-tab profile shipped exactly this: it opened fine and threw `Cannot access 'ctxRef' before initialization` on the first tab tap. `profileNavReducer` now takes two arguments, lives at module scope and gets everything from the ACTION, assembled at dispatch time. Same rule for anything else React may call mid-render.

- **A snapshot the cache answered is not a read** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). Firestore here keeps a persistent local cache (`persistentLocalCache`, `src/firebase.ts`), so `onSnapshot` first hands over what this iPad last saw, flagged `metadata.fromCache`, before the server answers, and keeps handing it over while offline. Reading that as a finished read turns "can't tell" into "empty": the standing week's check listed every agreed slot the cache lacked as open, with a Free slot badge, on an iPad with no connection. A screen that must say "can't tell" treats a cache-only answer as loading, and as "can't tell" once the browser is offline or after a wait (`standing-week/server-read.ts`, `useServerWait`, 15 s). **It must also listen with `{ includeMetadataChanges: true }`**: without it a listener is never re-fired when the server merely CONFIRMS the rows the cache already held, so a wait for the confirmation never ends. `useWeekSchedule` takes `{ confirmed: true }` for this; only Team opts in, and the Operations Overview reads exactly as before. **`getDocs` does the same offline** (machine menu review, Oct 5 2026): it does not fail, it answers from whatever this iPad holds, often nothing, with `metadata.fromCache`. The machine menu's Load older took that for a read and remembered thirty sessions as read with no sets until sign-out, and the profile's first page took a partial cache for the whole record, so the card said "First time" or drew a start wall. A one-off read that must be whole checks `fromCache` (throw, or record "cache-only"); a listener whose answer gates a claim listens with metadata changes and skips the rebuild on a metadata-only event (`snapshot.docChanges().length === 0`), as the tracker's logs window does.

- **An `onSnapshot` fake in a render test must accept both call shapes**, `(query, next, error)` and `(query, options, next, error)` (Openings round, Sep 27 2026). A listener that asks for `{ includeMetadataChanges: true }` passes its options second, and a fake that ignores that shape leaves it waiting forever. `WrapUpScreen.render.test.tsx`'s fake takes both, and since the machine menu review (Oct 5 2026) so does `WorkoutTrackerView.render.test.tsx`'s, whose snapshots now carry `docChanges()` and `metadata` too (the tracker's logs listener reads both).

- **Anything that throws in a `useLayoutEffect` takes the whole screen to the error boundary.** Feature-detect browser APIs there: `typeof ResizeObserver === "function"` before `new ResizeObserver` (JourneyGrid and ProfileSubnav do; one JourneyGrid use at line ~569 still does not — pre-existing).

- **`npx vitest run src` mounts only what has a `*.render.test.tsx`** (the profile nav, the notes area, the Dial, Update Pulse, the briefing, the post-session screen, the Pulse panel, the 4 P's, the Deep Dive — jsdom, raw `react-dom/client`, no testing-library). A clean typecheck, a full green suite and a production build all passed while the profile crashed on every tab tap. **Add a render test for any hook or component that does work during render or in a layout effect** — they are cheap and they are the only check that would have caught it.

- **A date-ONLY ISO string is UTC; a date-TIME with no zone is LOCAL.** `new Date("2026-09-20")` is UTC midnight, `new Date("2026-09-20T10:00:00")` is local. Mixing the two in one comparison passes in CI (UTC) and fails on a studio PC (Eastern), which is the worst shape a date bug can have — it cost a red `ship` run on Sep 15. `toDate()` in `src/types/journal.ts` pins a date-only string to local NOON for exactly this reason; never hand a raw `new Date("yyyy-mm-dd")` to anything that then does local-calendar arithmetic. **Run the suite with `TZ=America/New_York` before shipping**, not just in UTC. (Known and unrelated: `src/features/renewals/conversation.test.ts` fails at UTC+14; no studio is east of Eastern.)

- **A test that reads a file sees `\r\n` on AJ's PC** (colour follow-ups, Oct 4 2026). Git there has `core.autocrlf` on, so a fresh checkout or worktree writes almost every file with Windows line endings (`git ls-files --eol` says `i/lf w/crlf`), while the cloud container and GitHub see `\n`. `loud-orange.test.ts` matched its token blocks with patterns that span a line break; in the new worktree none of them matched and the whole file failed to load, on master as much as on the branch. A test that reads a stylesheet, a screen or a doc and matches across lines turns `\r\n` into `\n` when it reads (`readFileSync(path, "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n")`, as `loud-orange.test.ts`'s `read()` does). A green run elsewhere (the cloud container, or a file a tool rewrote with `\n`) does not prove it; a fresh worktree on the PC does.

- **A syntax error anywhere hides every type error everywhere** (Oct 3 2026). `tsc` reports syntax errors first and, when there are any, checks no types at all. `0b2cddaf` left five lines of a replaced test after its close in `DayHeader.render.test.tsx`; the typecheck then read 2 errors, the two syntax errors, which matched the baseline of 2 and would have hidden any real one. Read the error list, not only the count: a `TS1xxx` code is syntax, and the baseline's two are `charts.tsx` and `EditTrainerModal.tsx`.

- **Never type a raw control or invisible character into source** (a NUL, U+F8FF): write the escape (`\u0000`, `\uf8ff`). A raw NUL makes git treat the file as binary, and a binary diff can't ship as a patch. The usual way one gets in is a **no-break space (U+00A0)** \u2014 it looks like a space everywhere \u2014 and Claude's file-editing tools can DECODE a `\u00a0` written in an edit into the raw character (client codex, phase 5, Sep 24 2026: three test strings came out raw). It happens when a whole NEW file is written, too (phase 17: a regex's U+00A0, U+2013, U+2014 and U+2022 escapes in `client-admin/intake.ts` came out as the raw characters — the NBSP invisible, the dashes and bullet merely unescaped). Write such a file, then put the escapes back with a small Node script that builds the backslash as `String.fromCharCode(92)` (a script typed with the escape in it is decoded the same way). After writing an escape, check the bytes (`sed -n 'N,Mp' file | cat -A`). The codex's `kit/scale.test.ts` scans every codex source and test file for them.

- On AJ's PC, Claude's Linux shell reaches the project folder again (Sep 16): it can run git (delete permission needed for the lock files) and `tsc`, but **not** `vitest` or `vite` — the Windows `node_modules` has no Linux rolldown binding. The cloud container **can** `npm ci`, typecheck, run the suite and both builds; it cannot run `test:rules` (the emulator jar's host is blocked), so **AJ's `test:rules` run is the one that counts.** The file bridge (stage and commit files) works either way.

- **On AJ's PC the test commands must exclude `.claude/**`** (client codex, phase 1, Sep 24 2026). Claude Code keeps git worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`, each a full copy of the repo, and vitest's default excludes do not cover them: `npx vitest run src` matches every copy's `src/` too, and `npm run test:rules` ran four copies of the rules suite in parallel against ONE emulator (172 spurious failures). `package.json`'s `test` and `test:rules` now pass `--exclude **/.claude/**`; by hand, use `npx vitest run --dir src` (or add the same `--exclude`). And `firebase emulators:exec` can leave the Firestore emulator (a `java` process) listening on port 8080 after it exits — the next rules run then fails with "port taken". Check `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 8080` first and stop that `java` process if it outlived a run.

- **On AJ's PC, use `git --no-optional-locks` for every read-only git command** (beta-prep trim, Sep 17 2026). Claude's shell reaches the project folder through a mount that cannot delete files until AJ grants delete permission for the session. A plain `git status` refreshes the index: it creates `.git/index.lock`, then cannot remove it, and AJ's NEXT git command - in PowerShell or GitHub Desktop - fails with "Another git process seems to be running". `--no-optional-locks` skips that refresh. If a lock is left behind, move it aside with `mv` (renames are allowed) and say so. The same mount makes `git fetch` leave `tmp_pack_*` files in `.git/objects/pack` that it could not unlink (harmless; move them aside), and a `maintenance.lock` from Sep 2 had been silently blocking git's auto-maintenance there for two weeks. To deliver a branch without touching `master` or the working tree: `git bundle create x.bundle master..<branch>` in the cloud, commit the file into `backups\`, then on the PC `git fetch x.bundle <branch>:<branch>` - a new bundle file name each time, because re-using an outputs path can land the previous bytes.

- **A fresh Claude worktree (`.claude/worktrees/*`) can't typecheck or test as it stands (Sep 24 2026).**
  It has no `node_modules` and no `firebase-applet-config.json` (gitignored),
  so `tsc` reports 12 errors - the baseline 10 plus two "Cannot find module
  firebase-applet-config.json" - and Vite cannot resolve any package. Copy the
  config in from the main checkout (it stays ignored) and make a directory
  junction for `node_modules` (`New-Item -ItemType Junction -Path node_modules
  -Target <main>\node_modules`); on Windows, vitest then runs in the worktree.
  **Remove the junction when done** (`cmd /c rmdir node_modules` removes the
  link, not the target), so nothing that cleans the worktree up can follow it
  into the real one.

- **A worktree checked out with `core.autocrlf=true` has CRLF files, and the suite fails on them** (machine menu, Oct 4 2026). AJ's repository has `core.autocrlf=true`, so a new `.claude/worktrees/*` checkout writes every text file with Windows line ends; `src/loud-orange.test.ts`, which reads the source files and matches their class strings, then fails although nothing is wrong with the code. Convert the working files to LF before measuring (git stores LF and normalises on add, so the tree stays clean), and check with `git ls-files --eol | grep w/crlf` (it should print nothing). Files Claude's Write and Edit tools write are LF already.

- **The desktop app's built-in browser can already be signed in as AJ - against production (Sep 24 2026).**
  A Vite server on a new localhost port opened straight onto Strongsville's
  real Hub, from a Firebase session saved in that browser. Treat it as AJ's:
  never sign out there (it is his session), never click anything that writes,
  and close the tab when done so the app's background sync stops. Sign-in and
  sign-out flows are checked with render tests and on an iPad
  (`docs/ops/TESTING-CHECKLIST.md`, Round 15), not in that browser.

- **Headless Chrome driven over the DevTools protocol throttles the page
  BETWEEN commands (calm Hub round, Sep 28 2026).** A click sent in one
  `Runtime.evaluate`, then a wait on the Node side, left the page's timers
  and React's Suspense retries frozen: the Hub's Opportunities layer sat on
  "Opening Opportunities…" for 25 seconds, in the dev server and in a
  production-style build, and even a `lazy()` that had already resolved never
  swapped in. It looked like a live bug in Round 1; it was the tool. Do the
  click AND the wait inside ONE awaited `Runtime.evaluate` (`await new
  Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 3000))` in the page), or wait in the page
  between commands. The app on an iPad is unaffected; `ClientsView.render.
  test.tsx` proves the lazy layer opens.

<a id="baselines"></a>

**A screen's typing dies when it unmounts, and nothing warns unless it registers** (unsaved changes, Sep 24 2026). There is no router: a screen leaves the tree when `currentView`, the client, the studio, a profile tab, a section or a drawer's `open` changes, and Base UI `Tabs.Panel` unmounts a hidden tab (`keepMounted` is false). A screen that holds typed-but-unsaved work calls `useUnsavedChanges(isDirty, label, { onDiscard })` from `features/unsaved-changes` (every `useDirtyForm` form already does); a component that switches between children wraps them in a leave scope and routes the switch through `scope.guard`; anything set alongside a navigation goes INSIDE the guarded move, or a refused move leaves it behind for the next visit. Give `onDiscard` to any screen that survives a navigation: the client record is not keyed by client, and before this round it kept one client's half-typed edit when the profile moved on to the next — Save would have written it there (the same shape was in All locations' studio details). Since the landing merge (Sep 24 2026) the record is the client codex, which the profile keys by client, so that half is closed by the key; the codex registers its one Save bar with `form.discard` as the discard. A child a switch keeps MOUNTED (the codex, after its first visit) is taken out of that switch's scope with `<ExemptFromLeaveScope>`, or the tab bar would ask about edits a tab change never loses and "Leave" would throw them away. AppContent's screen is `useGuardedPlace` (who may open Operations) set through `useGuardedSetter`: a button's move asks, the route gate's own refusal never does. **Every move a nav hook offers goes through its guard, including one added later**: the codex's `openRecord` (its page switch, and the jump into Notes & Profile from another tab) dispatched straight through, so a jump from Programming dropped Setup's drafts without a word until it was routed through `toTab` (landing, Sep 24). Read `src/features/unsaved-changes/README.md`.

**A door that replaces a page inside the SAME section must ask the leave gate itself, and set its jump only inside the answer** (voice review follow-up, Sep 27 2026). Learning's search pick usually stays in the Catalog, so the view does not change and `useGuardedSetter` has nothing to ask about, while the Catalog swaps its page in place: a half-written studio note vanished, and an open studio-note editor could save one machine's draft as the next machine's note. Each such door now asks `useLeaveGuard` itself (`through` in `LearningView`). And each used to set where it was going BEFORE the question, so a "Keep editing" left the jump behind and the Catalog later opened on a machine nobody asked for; it is now set only once leaving is agreed. A jump from outside the tab is not asked about twice (AppContent's `openLearning` asked first). Any new in-place page switch (a list that opens a detail in the same pane) follows the same two rules.

**A `setState` updater is not guaranteed to run synchronously.** Reading live
state from inside one and acting on a flag it sets is a real bug, not a style
issue: inside an event handler the updater normally runs during the NEXT render,
so the flag is still false when you check it. React's eager-evaluation path
hides this until StrictMode's double render defeats it — and `src/main.tsx`
turns StrictMode on. `useDirtyForm.save()` shipped like this and silently never
called `onSave` while the bar sat at "Saving…" (fixed Sep 20 2026, machine
authoring). Mirror the state in a ref instead. A pure test cannot see this; it
needs a mounted render test.

**On AJ's PC, `vitest` no longer runs from the Linux-side shell.** The Windows
`node_modules` has no Linux rolldown binary and the newer vitest needs one
(`Cannot find module '@rolldown/binding-wasm32-wasi'`). `tsc` and `git` still
work there. Run the suite and the build in the cloud container instead — tar the
source (excluding `node_modules`, `.git`, `dist`, `backups`, and `.env` /
`service-account.json`), stage it, `npm ci`, then push changed files back with
`device_commit_files`. The Sep 8 note saying vitest works on the PC is obsolete.

**Git on this mount leaves a `.git/HEAD.lock` behind after every commit**, and
the next commit fails with "Another git process seems to be running". Deleting
it needs a permission that may be refused — but a RENAME works: `mkdir -p
.git/stale-locks && mv .git/HEAD.lock .git/stale-locks/HEAD.lock.$(date +%s)`
after each commit. Use `git --no-optional-locks` for read-only commands.

### The speed round (Oct 5 2026)

- **The boot's reads run in parallel and each carries what it knows** (R4; `src/features/front-door/boot-lookup.ts`). Studios, trainers and networks are read together, each `unknown`, `cache` or `server`. A screen that says something off one of them asks `studiosKnown` / `trainersKnown` / `networksKnown` first: never "No studios yet", "Not on a studio's team yet" or a team size off a list that has not answered, and a studio is dropped as missing only on the server's word. **The iPad's offline cache holds only what that iPad has read, so a cached collection is not the collection**: it counts only after this iPad has had the server's whole answer (`journey_list_seen_<list>`), and a cached trainers list of one record never counts. The cache can still evict documents, so a cached list can rarely be partial; the listener's server answer corrects it within one round trip. **The seen flags belong to the iPad** (a sign-out keeps them, `DEVICE_KEY_PREFIXES`, because the Firestore copy they describe outlives a sign-out; wiped, the next person on dead Wi-Fi sat on Checking you in), and **the list listeners listen with `includeMetadataChanges`** (`listDeliveryGate`): when the server's answer equals the copy, Firestore raises no other event, so the copy was never confirmed (the final review, Oct 6 2026). A refresh or any other setter that hands a list in passes `fromCache`, and nothing hands in an empty list to clear one (that marks it seen).
- **Anything that rewrites a whole array on a document builds on a server read** (R4; `useKaizenRoster`), never on a list the app opened from the cache, or it drops what another iPad added. Offline it falls back to the copy in hand.
- **A Firestore listener that errors never answers again** (R16; `src/lib/machines-store.ts`). A shared listener must drop itself on error so the next subscriber opens a fresh one, and publish its last answer as failed meanwhile.
- **Never hand `initializeAuth` a persistence or resolver that is not a class** (R14; `src/firebase.ts`): the Node test build's placeholder resolver asserts. `src/lib/auth-boot.test.ts` pins the SDK internals the sign-in warm-up relies on (`@firebase/auth/internal` `_getInstance`, a transitive dependency not in `package.json`); an SDK upgrade that moves them fails it loudly.
- **The Hub's minute is cheap only while these hold** (R6, R7). Anything new in `moments-today.ts` that reads `input.now` adds its instant to `bookingBoundaries`, or it goes stale between boundaries. The engine must not read a directory row's `next` or `today` (the rows are built at the studio's midnight; a test holds it). `HubCard.sameCard` compares every prop by identity except `now` and `entry`: a new prop handed over new on each render defeats the memo, and a card that starts reading another `entry` field adds it to `sameCard`. `sessionsKnown` means the server answered and is for labels only; a `LoggedSessions` with `complete: false` proves what it holds, not what it lacks.
- **The profile's history effect must not depend on `historyRead` itself** (R12): an offline, cache-only answer would loop. It reads it through a ref, and `ClientProfileView.history.test.ts` holds the effect's dependencies.
- **A `motion/react` mock built as a Proxy that makes a new component on every property access remounts the subtree on every render** (R7). A render-counting test makes one component per tag, or it measures remounts.
- **The announcements read is a count window** (R16): the newest 100. At about 25 studios or more it covers less than a notice's life; switch to an audience query (`studioId`, `createdAt`) then.
- **A `NODE_ENV=test` build ships React's development files** (R13). Vite builds production only with `NODE_ENV` unset or `production`; this shell and vitest set `test`, and a bundle measured from such a build overstates the first screen by about 70 KB gzip. `scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs` refuses a development build.
- **A screen's stylesheet is imported by the screen, not by `main.tsx`** (R13). `main.tsx` loads `index.css` and the token files only; a component that draws with a moved sheet's classes imports that sheet, before its own, and `css-imports.test.ts` walks both doors to My Studio's machines (Operations → Floor mounts them without My Studio's shell). Tests load no CSS, so only that scan and a walk catch a screen that lost its sheet. `src/first-screen.test.ts` fails, printing the import chain, if the first screen reaches a heavy module.

### The iPad round (Oct 6 2026)

- **Intl is expensive per call: never build a formatter, or ask `formatToParts`, inside a loop** (the iPad round, Oct 6 2026). `toLocaleDateString` / `toLocaleTimeString` with options build a new `Intl.DateTimeFormat` on every call (843 ms per 10,000 on AJ's PC at 1x, several times that on an iPad), and studio time's `zonedYMD` asked `formatToParts` every call (1.1 s of Operations -> Today at 5x). Per-row day words use `formatDateWords` (`src/lib/studio-time.ts`, one formatter per locale and option set), and studio time reads a zone's offset three times per UTC day and caches it per zone and day. The cache's one assumption (no clock change between a day's first second, noon and last second that the three readings would miss) was checked against 157 zones, every day 1990-2040 hourly; a day whose readings differ is MIXED and every instant in it goes through Intl as before, and so do years outside 1000-9999. Pass CONSTANT option objects: a formatter's key is worked out once per options object, so a literal written inside the call works its key out every time. `studio-time.cache.test.ts` holds every answer to the old Intl reading; a change to the cache keeps it green.
- **`useStudioRoster` keeps each client the same object until that client changes, and the speed depends on it** (the iPad round, `src/lib/studio-roster.ts` `rosterFromSnapshot`). After the first answer only the documents in `snap.docChanges()` are converted; a snapshot that changed nothing hands back the same list. The Directory's row cache (`client-directory/row.ts`, keyed on the client object under the same context object), the Hub's day rows (`use-day-moments.ts`) and `HubCard.sameCard` all skip work by identity, so one webhook write redraws one row instead of 300. Anything between the roster and those screens that copies clients (`clients.map(c => ({ ...c }))`, folding something into every client, a sort that rebuilds objects) defeats all three; fold into the one client on screen instead (the roster split's `withMachineTotals` replaces that one client and no other), and memoise any context object a row cache is keyed on, or every row is rebuilt each render. `ClientDirectory.rerender.render.test.tsx` and `useStudioRoster.render.test.tsx` count it.
- **A page on the boundary clock must name every instant it reads `now` for** (the iPad round, `src/lib/boundary-clock.ts`). `useBoundaryClock` and `useSettledNow` hand out a new time only when the studio's day turns or a watched boundary is crossed (`bookingBoundaries` in `lib/booking-state.ts`: half an hour before a booking, its start, half an hour after, its end, end plus the slack; and `brief.ts` `nightlyStaleAt`). Anything new on the Directory or Operations' Journey, Month, Trends, Team, Week or client pages that reads the time at another instant adds that instant to the page's boundaries, or it is stale until the next one. The Hub's own engine has the same rule over its own list (`moments-today.ts`, the speed round's trap above).
- **A worktree on AJ's PC is CRLF until it is converted, and a merge or checkout there writes CRLF again** (the iPad round, Oct 6 2026; the two entries above say why the suite fails on it). Convert a new worktree's files to LF before measuring, check with `git ls-files --eol | grep w/crlf` (nothing), and run every merge, checkout or reset in it as `git -c core.autocrlf=false ...`, or the files that command touches come back with Windows line ends and the next run fails on them although nothing is wrong. The ship scripts stop on any `w/crlf` file.
- **The perf lab's ports are shared by every worktree on the PC: one lab at a time** (the iPad round; `harness/perf-lab/README.md`). The lab's emulators take Firestore 8085, Auth 9099, hub 4410, logging 4510 and websocket 9160 (the rules tests keep 8080). Two lab runs at once, or a lab run beside a group's own emulators on those ports, fail or measure each other. Nothing may be listening on 8085 or 9099 when a run starts; an emulator a run left behind is a `java` process on them (stop it, as for 8080). The lab starts fresh emulators from its export for every rep because a reused emulator wedges (a closed Chrome left locks: "Transaction lock timeout"). Its numbers are Chrome's slowed down, never Safari's paint or a real device.

### The Wrap-up round (Oct 6–7 2026)

- **A fold into the client on screen must hand back the same object while nothing it was made from changed** (the Wrap-up round, `90461dec`; `docs/rounds/2026-10-07-wrapup.md`). Folding into the ONE client on screen (the trap above) is not enough on its own: AppContent rebuilds its client list on every roster change, other clients' included, and `withMachineTotals` made a new object for that client each time, so the session, the Wrap-up behind it and the profile redrew for another trainer's Finish, a webhook or the nightly job. It now keeps the last merge per client object (a `WeakMap`) and returns it while the client's document object and the totals' answer are the same ones; a new answer or the client's own change still makes a new object. Anything else folded into the client on screen keeps the same rule. `fold-identity.render.test.tsx` mounts the hook with a roster built by `rosterFromSnapshot` and `mergeRoster` and fails on a fresh copy.
- **In the perf lab, the long tasks after any write are mostly the emulator re-sending every listening query WHOLE** (the Wrap-up round; `harness/perf-lab/README.md`, "The Wrap-up after Finish"). After Finish that is about 3,500 documents and 8 MB (the 300-client roster once the client's document is written, the sessions and sets several times over); production sends only what changed. Since the speed round the emulator hands it over in many small frames, so the SDK's CPU on it doubled while the app's fell. A lab regression after a write is not the app's until the profiled rep's "app ms" (the report's By phase) or a `PERF_LAB_TRACE` (`recv` lines) says so; the Oct 6 release A/B's "Wrap-up 60 -> 581 ms" was this, plus the Wrap-up window ending at the database's answer, which the re-send landed on either side of (`PERF_LAB_WRAPUP_TAIL_MS` measures past it). The same goes for a profile opened again within seconds (the emulator's queue behind the previous profile's five set reads). And the lab's numbers move between sessions (the same build's Wrap-up 581 ms one afternoon, 752 that evening): compare A with B inside one run only.

### The first-session round (Oct 8 to 9 2026)

- **"Yesterday" is the studio's yesterday** (the screens preview, Oct 9 2026). The Changes list said "yesterday · Oct 7" on Oct 9: its words counted 24-hour spans from the clock while the day beside them was the studio's day. Both are the studio's days now (`routine-plan/changes-list.ts` `daysAgoWords`, handed the studio's day keys).
- **A converted worktree can show every file modified while `git diff` shows nothing** (Oct 9 2026). After a worktree's files go from CRLF to LF, the index still holds each file's old size, and git calls a different size modified without reading the file (`git update-index --refresh` answers "needs update"). `git -c core.autocrlf=false add -u` re-reads them, stages nothing when the diff is empty, and the ship scripts' clean-tree check then passes. Check `git diff --name-only` is empty first.

### The open session round (Oct 9 2026)

- **The ship scripts' live-rules check needs gcloud, and gcloud is not on AJ's PC** (Oct 9 2026). The golive of several ship scripts (`ship-first-session.ps1` among them) reads the live ruleset with `gcloud auth print-access-token` and, without it, asks AJ to look in the console. Use `scripts/check-live-rules.ts` instead: read only (GETs only, no file written), with the service-account key the data scripts use (`--key`; in a worktree, `..\..\..\service-account.json`), it finds the named database's release and compares the live text with `firestore.rules` byte for byte (line ends aside), says where they part, and with `--index "sessions(hostedAtStudioId, isUnassigned, status, createdAt desc)"` whether an index is READY. Exit 0 means the live rules are the file's. `ship-open-session.ps1` runs it in prepare (the live rules must be master's or the branch's) and in golive, and still prints the console check. `scripts/fetch-live-rules.ts` is the older read with the Firebase CLI's token; it saves the ruleset to a file and checks one regex.

## Git on the connected-folder mount - it cannot replace a file

Learned the hard way, Sep 20 2026, during the note threads round.

**The mount cannot delete, and git replaces a file by unlinking it first.** So
anything that rewrites the working tree - `git reset --hard`, `git checkout
<branch>` across a real difference, `git stash pop` - half-succeeds: it rewrites
the files it can create fresh and fails on the rest with `error: unable to
unlink old '<path>'`, leaving the tree in a state that is neither commit. A
`git commit -a` or `git add -A` on top of that commits the mixture.

What happened: something checked `master` out mid-round (the reflog said
`checkout: moving from note-threads to master`, and nothing in the session did
it - assume an editor, a tool, or another window). The checkout partly reverted
the tree, and the next `git add -A src` committed that mixture onto **master**,
not the feature branch.

The rules that come out of it:

- **Print the branch before every commit.** `git --no-optional-locks branch
  --show-current` costs nothing and is the only thing that would have caught it.
- **Never `git add -A`.** Name the paths. `-A` is what turns a half-reverted
  tree into a commit.
- **Recover the tree with tar, not git:** `git archive <commit> src tests
  firestore.rules | tar -x --overwrite -C .` - tar's `--overwrite` truncates in
  place instead of unlinking, so it goes where `reset --hard` cannot. Better
  still, if the container has a verified copy of the tree (the one the suite
  passed against), tar that back over the mount.
- **Rebuild history with plumbing, which never touches the tree:** `NEW=$(git
  commit-tree <good-tree> -p <parent> -F msg)` then `git branch -f <branch>
  $NEW`. `git branch -f` refuses the CURRENT branch, so move off it first or
  use the tree-restoring route above.
- The lock files are the same problem, and the workaround is unchanged: after
  every git write, `mv` any `.git/index.lock` and `.git/HEAD.lock` into
  `.git/stale-locks/`. Renames work on this mount even though deletes do not.

### It is worse than "something else checked master out" (catalog gate, Sep 20 2026)

Hit again the same day, with nothing else touching the repo. Two additions.

**`.git/HEAD` reverts on its own, and `git` will tell you it did not.** `git
checkout -b catalog-gate` printed `Switched to a new branch`, and
`rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` agreed — four commits were made on it and every
one is intact. Some time later `cat .git/HEAD` read `ref: refs/heads/master`
